Those around him murmured, and he said: "Even if you murmur, by God, this is the truth. Go — you are safe, and those who harm you shall be harmed."

He then returned their gifts to the two emissaries and said: "What need do I have of them? God did not take a bribe from me when He restored my kingdom to me, so I will not take a bribe from Him." With that, the story of al-Najashi was concluded.

*Note: (1) An addition from "B."*

<!-- page 134 -->
**[Verses of Abu Talib urging the Negus to do right]**

When news of the emigrants in Abyssinia grew sparse among the people of Mecca, Abu Talib said:

*Would that Ja'far's kin were like Ja'far himself?*
*'Amr and the enemy are his own kinsmen —*
*yet his companions are shielded from that rabble.*

*Did al-Najashi honor Ja'far?*
*A noble man — do not diminish him.*
*You know that God has exalted you;*
*you are known, I attest, as one of majesty,*
*and all noble causes look to you,*
*and you are a flood whose pools run full.*
*Through you enemies find benefit, and kin find shelter.*

**[The challenge to al-Najashi from within Abyssinia]**

A man from Abyssinia rose up to dispute the Negus's rule, and the Muslims were with him. The Negus went out to face this challenger, but not before sending word to Ja'far and his companions and preparing ships for them. He said: "Board these ships. If I am defeated, go wherever you wish; if I prevail, remain as you are." [69]

He then wrote out a document [53/f] bearing witness that there is no god but God, that Muhammad is His servant and messenger, and that 'Isa ibn Maryam is His servant, messenger, spirit, and word, which He conveyed to Mary. He placed it in his vaults on his right side, then went out to face Abyssinia.

*Note: (1) See: Diwan, p. 247. (2) An addition from "B."*

<!-- page 135 -->
When his people gathered before him, the Negus addressed them: "O people of Abyssinia, are you not the most entitled of all people?" They said: "Indeed." He said: "How have you found my conduct among you?" They said: "The finest conduct." He said: "Then what is the matter with you?" They said: "You have abandoned our religion and claim that 'Isa is a slave of God." He said: "What do you say about 'Isa?"

They said: "We say he is the son of God."

Al-Najashi placed his hand on his chest and said: "I testify that 'Isa ibn Maryam is" — and he added nothing beyond this, gesturing instead to the document he had stored in his garments.

When the Negus died, the Prophet (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) prayed over him in Medina and sought forgiveness and mercy for him.

**[The Islam of Hamza — peace be upon him]**

Islam was spreading and growing in numbers. Then the Prophet's uncle, Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib, accepted Islam. The occasion was this: Abu Jahl had abused and cursed the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family), and news of this reached Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib. He entered the mosque [53/z] in a rage, not pausing for anything, until he stood over Abu Jahl as he sat among his people. Hamza raised his bow and struck him a severe blow on the head, then said: "You dare to curse my nephew while I follow his religion? I am the uncle of the Messenger of God." The Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) came and heard what had happened, and he declared his acceptance of Islam.

Hamza (may God be pleased with him) addressed Abu Jahl in verse about what he had done:

*Note: (1) See: Biography of Ibn Ishaq, p. 152; al-Manaqib wa al-Maqalib [folio 32/f].*

<!-- page 136 -->
*Abu Jahl was shaken by what we had brought upon him.*
*By your unjust command — act on it if you will.*

*You harmed the Messenger of God — we have forbidden*
*your unjust decree, though you persist.*

*You face our defiance in spite of what you have done;*
*had you sought God, you would not have been brought low.*

*You turned from truth when it was offered to you;*
*you were destroyed before destruction came.*

[70]

*You harmed the Messenger of God and have been led astray;*
*I could not live after they returned.*

*Until we made you taste humiliation,*
*souls were healed, and we found our cure.*

Among the most harmful of the polytheists were Abu Jahl ibn Hisham and 'Umar ibn al-Khattab. The Prophet (peace and blessings of God be upon him) prayed that God would guide one of the two to Islam, so that religion would be strengthened and its corruptors diminished.

**[The Islam of 'Umar ibn al-Khattab]**

One day, 'Umar ibn al-Khattab set out with his sword, declaring that he intended to kill Muhammad — the man who, as he saw it, had split the Quraysh, [54/f] corrupted their thinking, disparaged their religion, and reviled their gods. He came to where the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) was sitting in a gathering of his companions.

<!-- page 137 -->
He knocked at the door. When those inside heard his voice, one of the companions of the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) rose and peered through the door. Seeing who it was, he went back to the Prophet (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) and told him. Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib said: "Let him in — if he means harm, we will kill him with his own sword." The Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) said: "Admit him."

The Messenger of God rose to receive him in the room. He took hold of 'Umar by his garment, then seized him firmly and said: "What has brought you here, Ibn al-Khattab? I think God will send down a punishment upon you from heaven." 'Umar replied: "I have come to accept Islam, O Messenger of God." The color returned to the Prophet's face and his trembling ceased, and 'Umar submitted.

When news of 'Umar's Islam reached the Muslims in Abyssinia, they said: "'Umar would never surrender unless even his donkey submitted" — meaning they knew how deeply he had been set against Islam and how fiercely he had hated the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family).

**[The Parchment of Boycott]**

[54/z] When the Quraysh saw that the Negus had shielded the emigrants from them, [71] and that Abu Talib continued to protect the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family), and that his uncle Hamza had embraced Islam with all that we have mentioned, and that those who remained Muslim enjoyed protection such that no harm could easily reach them — they despaired of winning Arab support against him. They gathered, turned every scheme over, debated every option, and in the end saw no recourse but to draw up a document binding the Banu Hashim and Banu al-Muttalib — pledging that no one would marry them, sell to them, or deal with them. They wrote this document, made a solemn pact upon what it contained, and hung it inside the hollow of the Ka'bah. Banu Hashim and Banu

<!-- page 138 -->
al-Muttalib withdrew to Abu Talib ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib, entered his quarter, and stood with him.

**[Abu Lahab sides against the Messenger — peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family]**

Abu Lahab 'Abd al-'Uzza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib, however, broke from Banu Hashim and went over to Banu 'Abd Shams and those among the Quraysh who openly declared their disbelief. The Banu 'Abd Shams praised him for it, and Hind bint 'Utbah welcomed him, saying: "Well done, son of 'Utbah! You have supported the cause and distanced yourself from those who turned away." She said: "Yes, may God reward you well, O Abu 'Utbah."

**[Poetry of Abu Talib to the Quraysh when they conspired against the Prophet — peace and blessings of God be upon him]**

Abu Talib said concerning their pact: (1)

*Have you not carried my message to Luwayy?*
*Truly — carry a message sent in earnest.*

*Our elder cousins are bound to us,*
*and our brothers from 'Abd Shams and Nawfal —*
*yet they rose against us,*
*drawn by a seductive order of enticement and deceit.*

*Nawasi Hashim yielded to humiliation?*
*They say: if we kill Muhammad*
*— in Mecca, the proclamation in every quarter —*
*you have lied! By the House of God, he is in our keeping,*
*with his blood-right and the corner of the manumission.*

*By pilgrimage or by the one whose throat was cut at the Nayb —*
*defenders who free every bone and joint.*

*You will reach him only over our bodies;*
*prayers in the hollow of the wide valley.*

*You call for mercy while you have been unjust —*
*either well, or in the end hastened.*

*Wait, and when tomorrow's war brings its result,*

*Note: (1) See: Diwan of Abi Talib, pp. 117–120.*

<!-- page 139 -->
*you will learn who it is you have wronged.*

[72] *When we pass through it with our swords*
*on a rise at the head of the lofty mountain —*

*and Muhammad received the spring of the Abtah,*
*Ka'b's noble pair after the first —*

*and Hashim sheltered him, for Hashim*
*casts aside whatever would bend and wither —*

*if you wish to kill Muhammad,*
*and the fluid one is the rock of the sanctuary,*
*we will defend him with all our might,*
*and he is like the lightning-flash of a cloud, a cleaver.* [55/z]

*And all my fighting men thirst to follow his heels,*
*Dalas, like Zahaz of the revolving springs,*
*and every charging rider overflowing with resolve.*

*I believe I smell from the wolves of Hashim*
*men who meet heroes in every quarter,*
*and the finest lords of men in every trial —*
*they are the masters of chieftains in every land.*

*Luwayy, a multitude from the sons of Ka'b —*
*shall I not report it to my own soul among us?*

*A prophet like Moses, inscribed at the opening of books,*
*and better than what God has singled out with love.*

*And for him is love among God's servants,*
*and what you have fabricated in your document —*
*you have a creature that writhes like a serpent,*
*and one who has no longing becomes savage as a wolf.*

*Awake, awake, before the wealthy are struck down;*
*we were bound together in affection and closeness.*

*Do not follow the command of the schemer who cuts ties —*
*a command for those who have tasted the war of Aleppo,*
*and you enter a war of allies and perhaps*
*consolation from the bite of time, and no anguish.*

*We and the Lord of the House surrender Ahmad.*

He also said in this regard: (1)

*Did you not know that we have found Muhammad?*

*Note: (1) See: Diwan, pp. 211–213.*

<!-- page 140 -->
*And the hands of the witnesses were filled with resolve,*
*with it and the wall of flesh — they drink of it.*
*It is as though the horses stand firm in their rocky ground,*
*and the cavalry of fierce battle.*

*He commended his daughter to stabbing and striking.*
[1/56] *Is not our father Hashim strong of arm?*
*We do not lament what comes upon us in calamity,*
*nor do we tire of war until it is satisfied.*

*If the souls of the truffles flew away in terror,*
*we would find the people of blood-right and sanctuary.*

[73]

**[Verses of Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib concerning the parchment]**

*And when you raise stories against us,*
*in the battle of your wrongdoing you will see the breaking of the cane.*

Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib (peace be upon him) said concerning the parchment and what the people had unanimously agreed upon: (1)

*Time passes, and those we love depart —*
*are the people not struck with wonder?*

*With shamelessness and aggression without cause,*
*and the words of people whose minds I have lost faith in —*
*to speak a fool's word or a sign of defect.*

*They say: we shall starve Ahmad out.*
*Messages of truth and a revelation that is not false —*
*he came with clear truth and established it.*

*Worshippers who have right and love in God,*
*letters from the One of Might, to stand in line.*

*To you — and the word of the noble messengers:*
*if you accept what has come from your Lord,*
*and during war, the war of kinsmen is grievous,*
*you will not be disgraced before any traveler.*

*Do not think we will abandon Muhammad as Muslims*
*without striking brow and forehead.*

*May God have mercy on us, and exalt his neighborhood —*
*be dazzled by our gathering.*

*Note: (1) See: al-Manaqib wa al-Maqalib [folio 34/z–35/f]; it was also attributed to Abu Talib — see: Diwan, p. 231.*

<!-- page 141 -->
*Noble Luwayy ibn Ghalib,*
*and a branch from Hashim, by whom he was known.*

*Wait, and when war breaks out in our house,*
*the people of the quarter will scatter after their gathering,*
*and some people will be humbled, left exposed.*

*Basha'a means every leader and follower;*
*Fayel reveals the servants of the Kaaba's keepers.*
*They have been struck with an eternity of many trials.*

**[Abu Jahl confronts Hakim ibn Hizam, and Abu al-Bakhtari intervenes]**

The Banu Hashim and the Banu al-Muttalib remained under the boycott for three years, without contracting marriages or conducting trade, with food reaching them only in secret.

Hakim ibn Hizam ibn Khuwaylid ibn Asad frequently sent food [74] to Banu Hashim in general, and to the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) in particular, out of regard for his aunt Khadijah bint Khuwaylid and her bond with the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family). Hakim was a man of sound judgment, wealth, standing, and protection among the Quraysh. He would send caravan after caravan by night, and the people of Banu Hashim would meet him and take what he brought.

One night Abu Jahl ibn Hisham intercepted him and confronted him, saying: "Are you going with food to Banu Hashim? I swear, you and your food shall not pass until I have exposed you before all of Mecca." Abu al-Bakhtari ibn Hisham ibn al-Harith ibn Asad came upon them and said: "What is this?" Abu Jahl said: "He is carrying food to Banu Hashim." Abu al-Bakhtari said to him: "[Rather,] this is food belonging to his aunt that she had with him — he is sending it to her. Are you going to prevent a man from bringing food to his aunt?!

*Note: (1) An addition from al-Manaqib wa al-Maqalib. (2) An addition from al-Manaqib wa al-Maqalib.*

<!-- page 142 -->
Get out of this man's way." Abu Jahl leapt at him, and Abu al-Bakhtari struck him with a camel's jawbone, landing a severe blow. Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib witnessed this not long after, and Abu Jahl hated that the companions of the Messenger of God (peace and blessings of God be upon him and his family) should come to hear of it, so they kept it quiet and mocked him in silence.

*Note: (1) An addition from al-Manaqib wa al-Maqalib.*


<!-- page 143 -->
Mention of the harm the Messenger of Allah received from his people

The Messenger of Allah called upon his people day and night, openly and in secret, summoning them to the command of Allah without fearing anyone in doing so. The Quraysh, when Allah shielded him from them, found that his uncle and his kinsmen of Banu Hashim and Banu al-Muttalib stood between them and their desire to oppress him. So they resorted to mocking him, his family, and his companions.

Among those who did so were Abu Lahab and his wife Umm Jamil bint Harb ibn Umayyah, who is described in the Quran as the "carrier of firewood." It was said that she used to gather thorny wood and scatter it along the path of the Messenger of Allah wherever he would walk. It was also said that she used to spread slander. Allah knows best.

Allah then revealed concerning them: ⟪ar: تَبَّتْ يَدَا أَبِي لَهَبٍ وَتَبَّ ۝ مَا أَغْنَىٰ عَنْهُ مَالُهُ وَمَا كَسَبَ⟫ — "Perish the hands of Abu Lahab, and perish he. His wealth availed him nothing, nor what he earned" [al-Masad: 1–2] — to the end of the surah.

When she heard what had been revealed about her, Umm Jamil came while the Messenger of Allah was sitting in the mosque near the Ka'bah, with Abu Bakr beside him. She had a piece of stone in her hand and said: "O Abu Bakr, I have heard what your companion has said about me":

*We have disobeyed,*
*[and his son commanded him,]* (3)
*and his religion is contemptible.*

(1) Increasing the virtues and defects.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 356/1.
(3) An addition from the Prophet's biography.

<!-- page 144 -->
"And I was kept from seeing the Messenger of Allah."

[75]

The Quraysh used to call the Messenger of Allah by an insulting name, cursing him. But the Messenger of Allah said to his companions: "Does it not amaze you how Allah has turned the harm of the Quraysh away from me? They curse and revile a man they call 'the reproachable one,' and I am Muhammad."

**Abu Talib's poetry pleading with Abu Lahab**

Abu Talib addressed Abu Lahab, reproaching him for withdrawing from Banu Hashim and appealing to him: (2)

*The dreams of nations have left you in foolishness —*
*I marveled, O Ibn Shayba, at what has come to pass.*
*They say whoever seeks Muhammad shall regret it —*
*do something other than evil, or draw near.*
*Yet close to you there is none who stands in rank,*
*only the envious one who harbors treachery.*
*A man has come from the finest of the sons of Manaf —*
*do not ride against him from among them.*
*Be a man of solidarity and honor,*
*and do not abandon him so long as I live and breathe.*
*Their loyalty among people is the finest of all —*
*enemies defend the heights of the Hashimites.*
*Not through an oath nor through addition —*
*if he has kinship, you have a bond of honor.*
*He sails across the encircling seas,*
*yet he is of Hashim at its very core.*

[58/f]

*A minister over enemies who goes unguarded —*
*rally all people away from him and stand by him.*

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography.
(2) See: Diwan, 177.

<!-- page 145 -->
*Our cousins, are you too weak?*
*If the Quraysh are angry with him, tell them:*
*How do they deceive us under cover of darkness?*
*And how the wombs among them hold a hollow.*
*We did not wrong the people with injustice,*
*nor were we light in what they did to them.*
*But we were the hope of the guardians and the sanctuary,*
*and glory in the sight of the great ones fills us.*

**The account of Abu Salamah seeking protection**

Abu Salamah ibn Abd al-Asad sought the protection of Abu Talib against his own people, Banu Makhzum, [76] who wished to kill him. Abu Talib protected him, so they came to Abu Talib and said: "O Abu Talib, you have prevented us from your nephew Muhammad — so why do you also prevent us from our own man?" He said: "He has sought my protection, and he is my sister's son. If I did not protect my nephew, I would not protect my brother's son." They argued with him over this, and Abu Lahab rose and said to them: "O assembly of Quraysh, you have burdened this elder beyond measure — you keep pressing upon him in the presence of his own people. By Allah, either step back from this matter or stand with him in what he has done, until he reaches what he seeks." They said: "We will withdraw from you and from what you dislike, O Abu Utbah." And they had, in that, a guardian and a supporter for the Messenger of Allah.

When Abu Talib heard what he had heard, he was moved, and he composed verses inciting Abu Lahab to support the Messenger of Allah: (2)

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography.
(2) See: Diwan, 178.

<!-- page 146 -->
*Turn around the nursling — for no sooner are grievances raised*
*than Abu Mutab, your darkness stands firm.*
*I say to him, and where has my counsel gone?*
*Do you dishonor it, or have the seasons fallen away?*
*Do not accept a time when I have not devised a plan —*
*you were not created for helplessness.*
*The paths of weakness lie elsewhere.*
*The brother of war gives regret until they find safety.*
*Fight — war is ordained — and you shall not see*
*them strip you as a spoiler or one who spoils.*
*And how have they not gained anything great from you.*
*And orphans — orphans in their stubbornness —*
*may Allah requite Abd Shams and Nawfal*
*for scattering our group after friendship and affection.*
*They lingered and tasted corruption all together,*
*obeying Ibn Dhakwan, Qays, and Disma.*
*And when you see a day the people rise up,*
*a woman — Abu Utayba is his uncle —*
*you lied, and the House of God is offended by Muhammad.*

**The account of the tearing up of the document**

Hisham ibn Amr ibn al-Harith then moved to have the document annulled — the document the Quraysh had drawn up against Banu Hashim. He was a man of standing among his people and was among those who kept watch over Banu Hashim. Having done this for a long time, he went to Zuhayr ibn Abi Umayyah ibn al-Mughirah ibn Abd [77] Allah ibn Umar ibn Makhzum, then to [59/f] al-Mut'im ibn Adi ibn Nawfal ibn Abd Manaf, then to Abu al-Bakhtari ibn Hisham, then to Zama'ah ibn al-Aswad ibn Abd al-Asad.

They gathered and met at the head of the pilgrim track by night, and they said: "We eat food, we wear clothes, we take wives in marriage, yet the sons of Hashim — nothing is sold to them nor bought from them, they cannot marry nor be given in marriage."

<!-- page 147 -->
They then made a pact and agreed to repudiate the document. Zuhayr said: "I will start by speaking and be the first among you."

The next morning, Zuhayr ibn Abi Umayyah circled the Ka'bah seven times, then came to the people and said: "O people of Mecca — we eat food and wear clothes, while the Banu Hashim perish: nothing is sold to them, nothing is bought from them. I swear I will not sit until this unjust and contemptible document is torn apart."

Abu Jahl ibn Hisham, who was in a corner of the mosque, said: "You lie — by the House of God, it shall not be torn." Zama'ah ibn al-Aswad said: "You — by Allah — are the liar. We did not approve of this document when it was written." Abu al-Bakhtari said: "Zama'ah speaks the truth — we are not satisfied with what is written in it." Al-Mut'im ibn Adi said: "You both speak the truth, and whoever says otherwise lies. We disavow before Allah what is written in it." Hisham ibn Amr said the same. Abu Jahl said: "This is a matter that was decided overnight" — meaning it had been arranged without him.

**The Messenger of Allah informing his uncle that the document had been eaten away**

The Messenger of Allah said to his uncle Abu Talib: "O uncle, my Lord has sent a small creature against the document of the Quraysh, and it has eaten away every name of Allah that was in it, leaving only the injustice, the severance of ties, and the falsehood in it. Allah has informed me of this."

When Abu Talib heard this, he went to where the Quraysh were gathered at the side of the mosque and said: "O assembly of Quraysh, my nephew has told me such-and-such — so bring out your document. If it is as he has described, then cease your boycott against us."

<!-- page 148 -->
The people said: "We are satisfied." They agreed to this, then examined the document — and found it exactly as the Messenger of Allah had said.

**Abu Talib's poetry in praise of those who tore up the document**

When the document was torn and what it contained was annulled, Abu Talib composed verses about it: (1)

*By their faces — I swear by God — people resolve*
*that they came to our sea for the work of our Lord.*
[78]
*And all that does not please God is destined to corrupt —*
*he tells them the document has been torn apart.*
*Untangled, its knots undone, its spell assembled*
*[59/60] — those who would not crack it fell before it.*
*The magic of the end of ages could not prevail;*
*its strands wavered about its head.*
*To cut from it an arm and sever its limb —*
*it was enough that this befell it in full.*
*Their quarry thunders in fear of misfortune,*
*and they drive the people of the two sanctuaries to flight.*
*Which of them at that moment shall be saved?*
*Let the plower turn his course around.*
*It has an arrow, a bow, and its string —*
*and you rise between the two woods as scribes.*
*So we fortified ourselves in the heart of Mecca —*
*who will be driven from the civilization of Mecca?*
*We did not yield; we shall increase in goodness and gratitude,*
*we grew up in it while its people were few.*
*If you would freeze the hands of those who would overflow,*
*and feed until people leave behind their bounty,*
*may God reward you — O pilgrims — in succession,*
*may He guide firmly and lead aright.*
*Bound together — yet they are stripped and united,*
*sitting at the head of the pilgrim track, most resolute.*
*If he does not walk in the fold of the shield, I am lost —*
*every falcon helped her as though she were*
*a burning brand, enough to blaze.*
*Bold enough to make the engagements as though —*

(1) See: Diwan, 234–237.

<!-- page 149 -->
*If his face wearies, his face shall turn out*
*from the most noble of Luay ibn Ghalib.*
*Long and sharp, half his leg protruding —*
*on his face the clouds water and are glad.*
*He summons the summer quarters and mobilizes —*
*shelter me, O clan companions of Salih.*
*And he grows greatly wherever he stood against the enemy,*
*so we ranged across the land and paved the way* [60/z]
*slowly, while the rest of the people slept.*
*Repel the enemy — nothing else avails —*
*the great commander has given his order, then praise.*
*Preserve this reconciliation, every justification —*
*and the best of people, Muhammad, was pleased with it.*
*They returned — Sahl ibn Baydha among the pleased —*
*and we used to be content before that.*
*What is the association with nations in most of our affairs?*

[79]

*Attain what you seek and do not hesitate —*
*we are elders; we do not accept darkness.*
*Do you have something to offer tomorrow?*
*By al-Qusay — do you have something in your mouth?*
*A great lord, master and son of a master —*
*they spent what they spent in their nights, and became.*
*You have milk — if you speak, it is pure.*
*I and you are as he said.*

Abu Talib also said concerning the document: (1)

*And the people of steadfastness from your many people —*
*are those who hold a station at the end of the night*
*not to be crowded away from their rightful struggle.*
*And look — see it from Luay ibn Ghalib:*
*he is a wreck yet no guilty man.*
*If the commander devised a plan within it,*
*and an ancient religion whose people are not let down —*
*and he who calls to Allah alone has not been deceived,*
*and if he can nurture the people, he shall nurture them,*
*and he who calls to righteousness and meets it has not withered.*

(1) See: Diwan of Abi Talib, 95–96.

<!-- page 150 -->
*And there is no one who knows a matter like one who has tried it —*
*those who have tried in the past know they are not in a position.*
*When the absent one is reported, the people grow fond —*
*the document held a lesson.*
*And they did not take revenge from the one who speaks the truth, though he is vindicated —*
*may God erase from it their disbelief and disobedience.*
*And whoever invents what is not true is a liar,*
*and what they said has been rendered void.*
*Upon the disgruntled among our people — none to be blamed —*

*We are estranged from us, yet not truly distant —*
*so do not think, Muhammad, that we are losers.*
*His standing among the people is the finest standing —*

*By the people of al-Uqair, or by the people of Yathrib,*
*Talih is in the palm of the harvest.*
*So the one who challenged every light —*
*let us swear, a hero, with the ancient and veiled.*

*O our people, do not oppress us — for we*
*do not depart from your counsel in every direction.*
*And cease your curiosity about your dreams —*
*so reward you with weakness in mother and father.*
*And do not set upon us with darkness and harm,*

[61/0]

*for God — who is his God — shall help him.*

<!-- page 151 -->
**Al-Isra' of the Messenger of Allah from Mecca to Jerusalem**

[80]

**[The night journey of the Messenger of Allah from the Sacred Mosque to Bayt al-Maqdis]**

When Islam had spread, the Messenger of Allah was taken by night from the Sacred Mosque to the Al-Aqsa Mosque — that is, Bayt al-Maqdis, which is Aelia.

Abdullah ibn Mas'ud said:

"The Messenger of Allah was brought the Buraq — the animal that used to carry the prophets before him — (2) and it placed its hoof at the farthest extent [61/z] of its reach. It bore him, and Allah showed him His signs between the heavens and the earth. When he reached Jerusalem, he found there Abraham, Moses, and Jesus — a company of the prophets gathered for him — and he led them in prayer."

Abu Sa'id al-Khudri said:

"I heard the Messenger of Allah say: (4) 'When I had finished what I came to do in Jerusalem, a ladder was brought to me — the Mi'raj — and I had never seen anything more beautiful than it. It is what the dying person looks toward when death approaches. My Lord's Messenger, Jibril, ascended me through the heavens.'

He created and fashioned within His earth and His heavens, and he ascended to the highest horizon, and he descended."

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 397/1.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography.
(3) See: Biography of the Prophet, 403/2.
(4) An addition from "B".


<!-- page 152 -->
It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, from his father, from his grandfather, from al-Husayn ibn 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (may Allah's prayers be upon them all), that he was asked about the people's claim that the call to prayer originated from a vision seen by 'Abdullah ibn Zayd — that the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) was informed of it and then commanded it as the call to prayer.

Al-Husayn ibn 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon them) said: "Revelation descends upon us, and you claim that he took the call to prayer from 'Abdullah ibn Zayd? The call to prayer is the foundation of your religion."

He grew angry and then said: I heard 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) say: Allah sent down an angel who came to the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family). He then recounted the hadith of al-Isra' at length — which we have abridged here. In it, he said: "Allah sent an angel the like of whom had never been seen in the heavens before that time, nor after. He gave the call to prayer and intoned it in pairs. He described how the call to prayer was given, and Gabriel said to him — peace be upon him and his family — 'O Muhammad, this is how permission to pray has been granted.'"

(1) Pillars of Islam, 1/143.
(2) Addition to the Pillars of Islam.
(3) Addition to the Pillars of Islam.

<!-- page 153 -->
The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said:

"When I was taken up to heaven, it was said to me: 'Where does the highest rank lie?' I said: 'I do not know — inform me.' It was said: 'In performing ablution thoroughly even in hardship, walking to Friday prayers, and waiting for one prayer after another.'"

**[On the obligation of prayer]**

It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) that he said:

"Allah made prayer obligatory — fifty prayers per day and night. Then Allah showed mercy to His creation and was gracious to them, and reduced them to five prayers. The reason for this was that when Allah took His Prophet [Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family)] on the Night Journey, he passed by the prophets and none of them questioned him until he came to Moses, who questioned him. Moses told him what had happened, then said: 'Return to your Lord and ask Him to lighten the burden upon your community, for I have seen what the Children of Israel were like — even when the commandments were revealed to them, they rejected them.'

So the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) returned and petitioned his Lord, and Allah reduced the prayers for him."

(1) See: Pillars of Islam 100/1; al-Mu'jam al-Kabir by al-Tabarani 109/20–110; History of Baghdad 147/8.
(2) "The congregation" appears in manuscripts "3" and "B," as well as in the Pillars of Islam. The more likely reading is that which accords with the narration of al-Tabarani and al-Khatib al-Baghdadi.
(3) See: Pillars of Islam 1/133; Explanation, p. 125.
(4) Addition from the Pillars of Islam.
(5) Addition from the Pillars of Islam.
(6) Addition from the Pillars of Islam.

<!-- page 154 -->
He reduced them to five prayers. When the Prophet came to Moses (peace be upon him), Moses said: "Return."

So he returned (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and Allah reduced the prayers by five. Moses continued to urge him back, and Allah reduced them by five each time, until they reached five prayers. At that point, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) felt too ashamed to return to his Lord again.

Then Abu 'Abdallah (peace be upon him) said: "Allah rewarded Moses on behalf of this community — what a reward."

**[Hadith: "I saw an angel in the likeness of my brother 'Ali"]**

It was narrated from the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said:

"When I was taken up to heaven, I saw an angel in the likeness of my brother 'Ali ibn Abi Talib." He was asked about this, and he said: "No — rather, the angels had longed to behold the face of 'Ali (peace be upon him), and so Allah created this angel. When they longed to look upon him, they looked upon it."

**[The occasion of revelation: "And We did not make the vision We showed you except as a trial for the people"]**

When the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) came out to meet his people,

(1) Addition from the Pillars of Islam.
(2) Addition from manuscript "B."
(3) Addition from the Pillars of Islam.
(4) See: al-Kharaij wa al-Jarayeh 811/2–812; Kashf al-Yaqeen, p. 459; al-Sirat al-Mustaqim 244/1.

<!-- page 155 -->
he told them that Allah had taken him on this journey and had shown him wonders of what he had seen. Some of them accepted him, and some called him a liar. Some people turned away from Islam altogether when they heard this. Then Allah revealed the verse concerning those who apostatized from Islam: *"And We did not make the vision We showed you except as a trial for the people, and the tree cursed in the Quran — We threaten them, but it increases them only in great transgression"* [al-Isra': 60].

It was narrated that 'A'isha bint Abi Bakr said:

"The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) was taken by night on the Isra' in body, and we were cleared of that [claim]."

Umm Hani bint Abi Talib said:

"The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) spent the night of the Isra' in my house. He slept after the last of the 'Isha prayer, and before dawn broke the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) woke us. He prayed the Fajr prayer and we prayed with him, then he told us the account of the Isra'. When the Quraysh denied him, he came out to them — peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family — and described the roads of Bayt al-Maqdis to them, and they recognized that he spoke the truth."

**[The steadfastness of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family)]**

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) remained throughout all of this steadfast in supplication to Allah, earnest in his desire to guide Allah's servants, and patient in enduring

(1) See: Tabaqat Ibn Sa'd 234/2; Musannaf Ibn Abi Shaybah 529/7; Mu'jam al-Tabarani 30/22; Ahkam al-Quran by al-Jassas 50/1.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 402/1.

<!-- page 156 -->
the harm inflicted upon him by his people. The Book of Allah was being revealed to him, and revelation came down from Allah the Exalted and Almighty — with every descent, new verses and proofs came to bear witness to him.

The polytheists pressed their opposition to him relentlessly, setting traps against him and seeking to repel those among their own people who might otherwise have inclined toward him, as well as those who came from elsewhere and to whom his news had reached, and who had come to see the signs and proofs of his prophethood.

His uncle Abu Talib stood by him, supported him, and fought on his behalf — so that no serious harm reached the Prophet except through words of abuse and veiled insinuation. The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) would find relief with Abu Talib whenever his chest tightened, and would be eased of his distress whenever the Quraysh oppressed him.

He also found in Hamza (may Allah have mercy on him) great support and much benefit — many of the reckless among the Quraysh were kept at bay on his account.

When the Prophet took refuge in his home and told Khadijah what he had endured, he found in her pride in him and in her wealth and all that her hands possessed she devoted entirely to him, and she served him herself. He never found another who provided him the way out of what weighed upon him as she did, nor a protector like his uncle Abu Talib. They all died — may Allah have mercy upon them and be pleased with them — in a single year.

**[Abu Talib's position as he neared death]**

It was said that when Abu Talib was dying, there came to him 'Utbah and Shaybah, the sons of Rabi'ah, Abu Jahl ibn Hisham, and Umayyah ibn Khalaf, along with men from Banu 'Abd Shams and Banu Shaybah and those Quraysh who followed them. They said to him: "O

<!-- page 157 -->
Abu Talib, you are one of us in standing and in what you have come to know, and you see what is before you, and you know what stands between us and your nephew. If you call him and restrain him from us, and take from him in turn, then let him cease from us and we will cease from him."

So Abu Talib sent for the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and when he came to him, the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said: "Yes — one word: say it, and the Arabs will submit to you and the non-Arabs will be indebted to you."

They said: "Yes — and your father's word, and ten more."

He said: "Say: There is no god but Allah, and cast off what you worship beside Him."

They said, clapping their hands: "Does Muhammad want us to make the gods into a single God? That is indeed a strange thing!"

Then Allah revealed these verses from the Quran: *"Make the gods one God? This is truly a strange thing. And the chiefs among them walked away: 'Walk on and remain steadfast to your gods — this is assuredly a thing contrived. We have not heard of this in the latter religion; this is nothing but an invention"* [Sad: 5–7], and the verses that follow.

**[What is claimed regarding Abu Talib's acceptance of Islam]**

They dispersed from Abu Talib, and Abu Talib said to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him):

(1) Addition from the Prophet's biography.
(2) Addition from the Prophet's biography.

<!-- page 158 -->
The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "By Allah, I asked of them nothing unreasonable."

When he said that, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) said to him: "Say this word, uncle, and through it I shall be able to intercede for you before Allah."

He said: "O son of my brother, were it not that it would be said I spoke out of fear as death approached, I would have said it."

They said: When the end drew near, his lips began to move, and al-'Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib put his ear close to him. He said: "By Allah, my nephew, Abu Talib has said the word you asked him to say."

They said that the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "I did not hear it from him."

**[The lament of the Commander of the Faithful for Abu Talib]**

Abu Talib (may Allah be pleased with him) died, and his son 'Ali ibn Abi Talib said:

*O rain cloud of abundance and light amid darkness —*
*Abu Talib, shelter of the seeker of refuge.*
*I pray for you, my uncle —*
*the people of nobility are laid low by your loss.*
*You were the finest uncle to the chosen one,*
*and may your Lord meet you with His good pleasure.*
*May He release his face from every grief —*
*he strove without rest against the polytheists.*

Lamentations:

**[The lament of Hamza for Abu Talib]**

Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib mourned his brother Abu Talib (may Allah be pleased with him), saying:

(1) Addition from manuscript "B."
(2) See: Diwan, p. 132 [verses 1–2–3].
(3) See: al-Manaqib wa al-Qala'ib [folio 38/z]; attributed also to Imam 'Ali (peace be upon him), see: Diwan, p. 64.

<!-- page 159 -->
*Suddenly the chief is mourned — the one who gave freely —*
*and this calamity has no successor and no equal.*
*The sons of Hashim are now exposed and besieged.*

*Yet if he speaks, he is answered with requital.*
*Amen — for what Allah has turned away from his heart,*
*and you see nothing that lives and is immortal.*
*So the Quraysh rejoiced at his passing —*
*but they will encounter from him a day that cancels their rejoicing.*
*They cherish hopes well-greased with their illusions,*
*and they slander and deny him.*
*They hope the prophet will be proven a liar and be killed —*
*high places and iron-clad chests, like India's.*
*You lie — by the House of Allah — we shall make you taste us,*
*if we do not let flow the iron that has been forged.*
*And it appears to us as an ill prospect from our side,*
*or you will see the guided ladder of the clan.*
*Either you look at us or we come for you —*
*Abu Hashim, best of the open plain.*
*Otherwise, the neighborhood is not without Muhammad.*
*And I am not alone among the friends of Allah,*
*for he has among you a helper from Allah.*
*Allah named him in the Book: Muhammad.*
*I seek him from every quarter and in every place.*
*The clouds clear from him and they brighten —*
*as though the full moon were the radiance of his face.*
*I lay sleepless in lamentation at the end of the night, singing:*
*O Abu Talib — shelter of the wanderer and the dew.*
*I would wish you a sweet hill that fills it all.*

**[On the death of Khadijah bint Khuwaylid]**

It was said that Khadijah bint Khuwaylid died three days after Abu Talib. The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) was deeply grieved and wounded by their loss.

Abu Talib had been his supporter and guardian in all his affairs, his defender among the people. And it was

<!-- page 160 -->
Khadijah who comforted him, lifted his sorrow, cared for him herself, and helped him bear his grief.

It was narrated from Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali ibn al-Husayn (peace be upon him) that he said:

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said to Fatimah (peace be upon her): "Gabriel (peace be upon him) gave me glad tidings that the house of your mother Khadijah bint Khuwaylid, and the house of Mary bint 'Imran, and the house of Asiya, the wife of Pharaoh, are in Paradise — fashioned from a hollow pearl, free of noise and toil."

**[The polytheists' boldness toward the Prophet after the death of Abu Talib]**

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) used to say: "I was never overwhelmed by grief as I was in the days that saw the deaths of Abu Talib and Khadijah." For Abu Talib had defended him, and Khadijah had consoled him, been patient with him, and made lighter what he bore for the sake of Allah.

After the death of Abu Talib, the command of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) became weightier; his grief for him deepened, and the reckless among the Quraysh grew bolder against him once Abu Talib was gone and began to inflict harm upon him.

The authority of Banu Hashim weakened after Abu Talib's death, so the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said to his family: "The Quraysh had never visited upon me anything I found hateful — until Abu Talib died."

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 17/3.
(2) Addition from manuscript "B."


<!-- page 161 -->
After Abu Talib's death, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had no one left to shelter him and bring him comfort — no one to watch over his affairs — except Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him). Ali had grown to manhood under the Prophet's guardianship; the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had raised him, and Allah Most High had honored him by singling him out as the Prophet's brother and designating him as his successor. After his father's death, Ali remained the Prophet's companion and stay, the one he leaned on in his affairs.

Meanwhile, the Prophet's enemies multiplied their hostility and harassment, causing him distress and grief, lying against him — though Allah (Glorified and Exalted be He) repelled their evil and foiled their scheming. Still, the harm they inflicted reached him (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him).

[The Prophet's Journey to al-Ta'if and the Thaqif]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) traveled to the Thaqif in al-Ta'if to seek their support. When he arrived, he sat with them in their gathering place and called them to Allah, speaking to them about what had been sent down to him and urging them to support Islam and stand with him against those among his own people who opposed it.

They gave him a response he and his family found deeply painful. He despaired of any good from them and asked them at least to keep his mission secret, so that his people would not use their rejection to further humiliate him — but they refused even that. Instead they provoked the foolish and the slaves against him, who jeered and shouted at him until crowds gathered around him. He took refuge by the wall of a garden belonging to the sons of Rabi'ah — Shaybah and his brother — having retreated from the mob of Thaqif's fools who had pursued him.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then said: "O Allah, to You I complain of my weakness,

<!-- page 162 -->
my lack of resourcefulness, and my humiliation before the people. O Most Merciful of the merciful, You are the Lord of the weak, and You are my Lord. To whom do You entrust me? To one far away who will treat me with contempt? Or to an enemy whom You have given power over my affair? If You are not angry with me, I do not care, but Your well-being is far more ample for me. I seek refuge in the light of Your face — by which the darkness is lit and the affairs of this world and the next are set right — lest You bring Your anger down upon me or Your wrath descend upon me. I will continue to seek Your forgiveness until You are pleased. There is no power except in You."

[The Story of Addas the Christian]

When the sons of Rabi'ah — Shaybah and his brother — saw him resting by the wall and the state he was in, compassion stirred in them. They sent a Christian slave known as Addas, saying to him: "Take a bunch of these grapes, put them on a plate, and carry them to that man and tell him to eat."

Addas did so. He came forward and placed the grapes in the hands of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) reached out his hand and said, "In the name of Allah," then ate. Addas looked at his face and said: "By Allah, these are words the people of this land do not know."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to him: "And what land are you from, Addas? And what is your religion?" He said: "I am a Christian, a man from the people of Nineveh."

<!-- page 163 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "From the village of the righteous man Yunus ibn Matta?"

Addas said to him: "And what do you know of Yunus ibn Matta?"

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "He is my brother — he was a prophet, and I am a prophet."

At that, Addas fell upon the feet of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, kissing them, and kissed his head, his hands, and his feet.

One of the sons of Rabi'ah said to his brother: "He has already corrupted your slave."

When Addas returned to them, they said: "Woe to you, Addas! Why did you kiss that man's head and hands and feet?" He said: "O my master, there is nothing on this earth better than him. He has told me something that only prophets know." They said: "Addas, do not let him draw you away from your religion."

[The Prophet's Presentation of Himself to the Arab Tribes]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family then went to the Arabs, presenting Islam to them, seeking their support during the pilgrimage seasons and urging people toward their lands.

['Abd al-Karim ibn al-Haytham, with his chain of transmission,] from the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi

(1) An increase from "B".

(3) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.

<!-- page 164 -->
Talib (peace be upon him), who said:

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was instructed to present himself to the Arab tribes during the pilgrimage seasons, he set out for that purpose and ordered me to accompany him, so I went out with him. Abu Bakr — a man of deep knowledge of lineage — went out with him too. We made our way to a gathering of people, and Abu Bakr stepped forward to address them.

He said: "Who are these people?" They said: "From Rabi'ah." He said: "Are you from her nobles, or from those who were defeated by her?" They said: "From her nobles." He said: "And who among her nobles are you?" They said: "The mightiest of the mighty." He said: "Is your strength 'Awf ibn Muhamalim, who used to say, 'Awf ibn Muhamalim,

[89]

is there no generosity in Wadi Auf?'" They said: "No." He said: "Are you Bastam ibn Qays, the magnanimous one, the pride of the living?" They said: "No." He said: "Are you Jassas ibn Murrah, the defender of honor and the protector of the neighbor?" They said: "No." He said: "Are you the son of Shreek, the slayer of kings and the one who put them to the test?" They said: "No." He said: "Are you the one who bears the unique crown?" They said: "No." He said: "Are you the uncles of kings from Kindah?" They said: "No." He said:

"Are you the sons-in-law of the kings of the flesh?" They said: "No." He said: "Then you are not the eldest wonder, nor the wonder of the youngest."

At that, a man from Bani Shayban with a lowly bearing rose to face him; he was known as Daghl. He said:

"The burden we neither know nor carry — our questioner must ask elsewhere."

"You there — you have questioned us while we put our questions to you. Do not conceal it from us: who is this man?" He said: "From Quraysh." He said: "A fine people of honor and leadership. And from which branch of Quraysh?" He said: "From Bani Taym ibn Murrah." He said: "I swear by Allah, the Thrower, you come from the very same threshing floor. Do you know that Qusai ibn Kilab ibn Murrah gathered the tribes of Fihr and was called al-Mujammi'?" He said: "No." He said: "Are you Hashim, who crumbled bread for his people and fed the pilgrims?" He said: "No." He said: "Are you Shaybah al-Hamd — 'Abd al-Muttalib — whose face shone like a moon in the deep darkness?" He said: "No." He said: "Who then are you from among the overflowing people?" He said: "No." He said: "Are you from the people of the council?" He said: "No." He said:

"Are you from the people of al-Rifadah?" He said: "No." He said: "Are you from the people of the hijab?" He said: "No." He said: "Are you from the people of the watering?" He said: "No."

At that, Abu Bakr pulled the reins of his camel and turned back toward the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). The man called after him — meaning something to the effect that the flood had been diverted, pushed away, digested by force, or split — and said: "By Allah, if I had stood my ground I would have told you that you are one of the fools of Quraysh — or what else would you be?

[90]

A Badghug."

Ali (peace be upon him) said: When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) heard this, he smiled. I said to Abu Bakr: "I have found you to have fallen into a fool among the Arabs." He said: "Yes, O Abu al-Hasan — for every affliction there is a greater affliction, and the affliction of wit is entrusted to logic."

Then we rode on to another gathering with composure and dignity. Abu Bakr went forward to greet the people, and they returned his greeting. He said: "Who are these people?" They said: "From Shayban ibn Rabi'ah." Abu Bakr turned to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "By my father and my mother, there is no greater glory to be found among their people." Among those gathered were Mafruq ibn

(1) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(2) Addition from: al-Jalis al-Salih; Dala'il al-Nubuwwah.
(3) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(4) Addition from Majma' al-Amthal 1/26–27.

<!-- page 167 -->
'Amr, Hani' ibn Qabisah, al-Muthanna ibn Haritha, and al-Nu'man ibn Shreek. Mafruq ibn 'Amr was the most commanding of them in speech and the most eloquent of tongue; he had two locks of hair that fell onto his chest, and the ears of the people in that gathering were turned toward Abu Bakr.

Abu Bakr said to him: "How many are you?" He said: "We number in the thousands, and a thousand are not overwhelmed by a small force." He said: "And how strong is your defense?" He said: "We spare no effort, and every people has its mettle." He said: "And how do you fare in war against your enemy?" He said: "We are the most fierce in the press of battle. When we are stirred to anger, we drive our horses over our children, preferring the weapon above all else. Victory comes from Allah — sometimes for us and sometimes against us. Yet perhaps you are the brother of Quraysh?" He said: "If the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) has reached you" — and he pointed to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) — "this is he." He said: "We have heard that this is what he says."

He drew near to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "To what do you call, O brother of Quraysh?"

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "I call you to bear witness that there is no god but Allah and that I, Muhammad, am the Messenger of Allah; and to protect me and defend me and support me. The Quraysh have set themselves against the command of Allah, denied His Messenger, and clung to falsehood over truth — except those whom Allah has protected and guided to His religion. Allah is the Protector and the Sufficient."

He said: "And to what else do you call, O brother of Quraysh?

(1) Addition from Dala'il al-Nubuwwah.

<!-- page 168 -->
All that is in the earth" — meaning: if these words were from their own, we would have recognized them. So he recited to them — may Allah bless him and his family —: "Say: Come, I will recite what your Lord has forbidden to you" ⟪ar:تَعَالَوْا أَتْلُ مَا حَرَّمَ رَبُّكُمْ عَلَيْكُمْ⟫ to the Almighty's words: "This He has enjoined upon you, so that you may use reason" [al-An'am: 151].

He said: "And what else do you call for?"

He recited to them: "Indeed, Allah commands justice and good conduct and giving to relatives, and forbids immorality and bad conduct and oppression ⟪ar:إِنَّ اللَّهَ يَأْمُرُ بِالْعَدْلِ وَالْإِحْسَانِ وَإِيتَاءِ ذِي الْقُرْبَىٰ وَيَنْهَىٰ عَنِ الْفَحْشَاءِ وَالْمُنكَرِ وَالْبَغْيِ⟫ — He admonishes you, that perhaps you will take heed" [al-Nahl: 90].

Mafruq ibn 'Amr said: "You call to the noblest of character and the finest of deeds. Yet some people have come out against you and denied you." — As though he wished to draw Hani' ibn Qabisah into the conversation — he said: "This is Hani' ibn Qabisah, our elder and the custodian of our religion."

Hani' ibn Qabisah spoke and said: "O brother of Quraysh, we have heard your words, and we find that for us to abandon our religion and embrace yours in a single sitting — a sitting in which you have not fully set out your case — and without seeing what the outcome of what you invite us to will bring, would be a slip of judgment, a hasty conclusion, and a misfortune for those behind us who would hate to have a binding agreement made without their counsel. Let us return and let you return, and we will reflect and consider." As though he wished to draw al-Muthanna ibn Haritha into the conversation, he said: "This is al-Muthanna ibn Haritha, our elder, our chief, and the master of our wars." So al-Muthanna ibn Haritha spoke and said: "O brother of Quraysh, I have heard your words, and the answer — as for leaving our religion

[70/f]

and embracing yours — that would be a fine answer. But the answer is that

(1) Addition from Dala'il al-Nubuwwah.


<!-- page 169 -->
The Messenger of Allah said to him: "What are these two streams?" He replied: The waters of the Arabs and the rivers of Khusraw. As for what lies along the waters of the Arabs — the sin of one's companion there is forgiven and his excuse accepted; but as for what lies along the rivers of Khusraw — the fault rests entirely with its owner, who is neither forgiven nor excused. We went down there under a covenant we made with Khusraw: we cause no disturbance and we take no independent action. Yet what you call us to — that which kings despise — if you wish us to shelter you and support you in it, then after the waters of the Arabs, we will give you victory and aid.

The Prophet replied: "You have done no wrong in your answer, since you spoke truthfully. Only one who embraces the religion of Allah in all its dimensions truly fulfills it. But tell me — if Allah grants you their wealth, their homes, and their women, will you praise and glorify Him?" He said: That would be for you, O Messenger of Allah. The Messenger of Allah then said to them: "We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner, and a caller to Allah by His permission and a shining light" — [al-Ahzab: …].

He then rose and took my hand and said: "O Ali — these are the dreams of the pre-Islamic era in which Allah responds, one bolstering the other. And they will contend against it in this world?!"

<!-- page 170 -->
[Mention of the Death of Abu Talib]

Abu Talib died three years before the Hijra. During that time, the Prophet began to hear more harm from his people; he saw them holding back from accepting his message, keeping the Arabs away from it, and repelling them from following him.

[The Beginning of the Ansar's Islam]

When Allah willed to strengthen His religion and fulfill His promise, the Messenger of Allah would go out during some of the pilgrimage seasons and present himself to the Arab tribes, reciting to them the signs of his Lord, as he had done every season. While he was at al-Aqaba, he encountered a group from al-Khazraj, for whom Allah intended good. He said to them: "Who are you?" They said: "From the Khazraj." He said: "The allies of the Jews?" They said: "Yes." He said: "Will you not sit with me so we may speak?" They said: "Yes."

They sat with him and he called them to Allah Almighty and presented Islam to them, reciting the Quran. [71/f] There were Jews nearby, and they had told the Ansar that a prophet was about to arise at that time. When the Messenger of Allah spoke to these people and called them to Allah, they said to one another: "This is the very prophet those Jews told us of.

(1) Addition from "B."

<!-- page 171 -->
Let us not allow the Jews to reach him before us."

They believed in the Prophet and in Allah his Lord, then departed — the Messenger of Allah bidding them farewell — and returned to their land, where they brought others to belief.

They were six men from the Khazraj:

From Banu al-Najjar — who is the Tayyim of Allah — then from Banu Malik ibn al-Najjar ibn Tha'labah ibn 'Amr ibn al-Khazraj ibn Haritha ibn 'Amr ibn 'Amir: As'ad ibn Zurara ibn Adas ibn 'Ubayd ibn Tha'labah ibn Ghannam ibn Malik ibn al-Nahar, known as Abu Umama.

And 'Awf ibn al-Harith ibn Rifa'ah [ibn Sawad ibn Malik ibn Ghannam ibn Malik ibn Malik ibn al-Najjar] (1) — he is the son of Afra'.

And from Banu Zurayq ibn 'Amir ibn Zurayq ibn 'Abd al-Haritha ibn Malik ibn Ghadab ibn Hashm ibn al-Khazraj: Rafi' ibn Malik ibn al-'Ajlan ibn 'Amr ibn 'Amir ibn Zurayq.

And [from Banu Salamah ibn Sa'd ibn 'Ali ibn Sarda ibn Yazid ibn Jashim ibn al-Khazraj, then from Banu Sawad ibn Ghannam ibn Ka'b ibn Salamah:] (2) Qutbah ibn 'Amir ibn Hudaydah ibn 'Amr ibn Ghannam ibn Sawad.

(1) Addition from the Prophet's biography 429/2.
(2) Addition from the Prophet's biography 430/2.

<!-- page 172 -->
And [from Banu Haram ibn Ka'b ibn Ghannam ibn Ka'b ibn Salamah:] (1) 'Uqbah ibn 'Amir ibn Nabi [ibn Zayd ibn Haram] (2).

And from [Banu 'Ubayd ibn 'Adi ibn Ghannam ibn Ka'b ibn Salamah:] (3) Jabir ibn 'Abd Allah ibn Ri'ab ibn al-Nu'man [ibn Sinan ibn 'Ubayd] (4).

When they embraced Islam, they said: "O Messenger of Allah, we have left behind a people among whom there is no greater evil and enmity than that which exists between them. May Allah unite them through you; if Allah unites them around you, there is no man more honored than you."

[Mention of the First Pledge of Allegiance at al-Aqaba]

When the following year came, during the pilgrimage season, twelve men came to the Messenger of Allah at al-'Aqaba and pledged their allegiance to him after believing in Allah and accepting him. That was the first pledge of allegiance — likened to the pledge of women — in which fighting was not mentioned.

They included:

From Banu al-Najjar, then from Banu Malik ibn al-Najjar: As'ad ibn Zurara, and 'Awf and Mu'adh — the sons of al-Harith ibn Rifa'ah.

The Islam of As'ad ibn Zurara and 'Awf ibn al-Harith has already been mentioned.

And from Banu Zurayq ibn 'Amir: Rafi' ibn Malik ibn al-'Ajlan [ibn 'Amr ibn 'Amir ibn Zurayq.] (1)

Dhakwan ibn 'Abd Qays ibn Khaldah [ibn Mukhlad ibn 'Amir ibn Zurayq.] (2)

And from Banu 'Awf ibn al-Khazraj: 'Ubadah ibn al-Samit ibn Qays ibn Asram ibn Fahr [ibn Tha'labah ibn Ghannam.] (3)

Abu 'Abd al-Rahman, who is Yazid ibn Tha'labah ibn Khazmah ibn Asram ibn 'Amr ibn 'Amara, from Banu Ghusayna, from Bali, as their ally.

And from Banu Salim ibn 'Awf ibn 'Amr ibn al-Khazraj, [then from Banu al-'Ajlan ibn Zayd ibn Ghannam ibn Salim:] (4) al-'Abbas ibn 'Ubadah ibn Nadlah ibn Malik ibn al-'Ajlan.

And from Banu Salamah ibn 'Ali ibn Asad [72/f] ibn Sarda ibn Yazid ibn Jashim ibn al-Khazraj, then from Banu Haram ibn Ka'b ibn Ghannam ibn Salamah: 'Uqbah ibn 'Amir ibn Nabi — his particulars have already been given.

And from Banu Sawad ibn Ghannam ibn Ka'b ibn Salamah: Qutbah ibn 'Amir ibn Hudaydah ibn 'Amr ibn Ghannam ibn Sawad.

Their embrace of Islam has already been mentioned.
