*So the fate of al-Harith ibn Hisham came to pass,*
*And survived with the head of resolve and courage.*
*Blood passed by a reaper and a stone,*
*The scattering of neutrality in the desert.*
*And Thawa loved him as a noble man.*
*I filled the two hollows with him and cast him there,*
*The victory of Allah with the people of Islam.*
*Build a self and a brave man in battle —*
*Your heart stirred in a dream like musk*
*Mixed with the water of clouds.*
*If you are a liar, what has befallen me —*
*Leaving loved ones to fight without them.*

(1) See: Diwan, 1/29–30.


<!-- page 223 -->

A war whose fury rages without mercy —
I struck them down, and God will carry out His decree.
Were it not for God and her flight, I would have left him
At the Seven Islands and Dasna, mired in turmoil.
He falls silent when he finds tongues running hot
Among captives whose bonds are drawn tight,
Until the banners vanish from sight
And Majdal feels no shame at the call.
Al-Suf's egg-market, all in a stir —
Shame and disgrace laid bare when they see
The lineage of the short-robed: Smeida, Miqdam.
By my hand I will act if he belongs, and he shall not go unpunished,
Like lightning beneath the shadow of every cloud —
When it meets iron, it is forged.

Ka'b ibn Malik mourned 'Ubaydah ibn al-Harith (1):

Weep with your tears truly, and hold nothing back —
O eye of Judy, do not be sparing.
A scene of nobility and generosity,
Over the master of this fate now sealed.
A bold commander, steadfast, bearing arms —
Generous as tea, a crusher of enemies.
I would not deny our nakedness or our loss:
'Ubaydah, yesterday, and we have not yet rested from grieving him.
He guarded the courtyard with the mulberry grove,
Protecting the day after the battle of al-Qatta.

Hind bint Athathah ibn 'Abbad ibn al-Muttalib lamented 'Ubaydah ibn al-Harith (2):

A true vision that floods the heart and mind —
The yellow has made its pledge, and I will honor it.
A widow brought low, trembling like a fool —
Weep, O 'Ubaydah, and cry for those who die in exile.

(1) See: Diwan, 168.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 3/41–42.

<!-- page 224 -->

If the sky's horizons turn red from the blaze,
A shelter for nations through every winter.
And the youth of the pot, as long as it froths and boils,
And weeping for orphans while the wind howls.
He used to feed them with firewood,
But when the one who kindled the fire dies, his throat grows cold.
He wore a rosary of the messengers' trust,
Whether by night or calling on the villages.

Talib ibn Abi Talib praised the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — and mourned those among the Quraysh who had been wounded (1):

She weeps over a heel; and how quickly one grows rich —
Have my eyes not already shed their tears?
And the cat coveted them, and they offered a tail,
Yet in wars they were weakened.
Would that I knew — might I yet see closeness to them?
And 'Amir weeps over the blame of tomorrow.
They give promises, and their neighbor shall not be oppressed by force;
They are my brothers, whose tongue shall speak no more.
And the army of Abi Yaksum when they filled the people —
You have no squadron left.

Were it not for God's defense, there would be nothing at all
Except that our protector is better than the one who stepped down and faltered.
When we were born into Quraysh, greatness was already there —
He is generous, neither miserly nor mean.
A brother to trust, among the noble deputies, a support —
Believing in the sea, not the Zorah or the Serbs.
Persist until you believe, through the striking of Khazraj —
O our brothers of 'Abd Shams and Nawfal,
A ransom for you both — send no war between us;
These are hadiths in which all of you lament the catastrophe.
Do not, after friendship and familiarity,
Forget that war was not something noble men desired —
Good people guard his door from treachery.
I swear by God I will not keep my soul in sorrow.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 3/26–27.

<!-- page 225 -->

**The Battle of Bani Sulaym and Its Aftermath up to the Gathering of the Polytheists at Uhud**

**[The Battle of Bani Sulaym at al-Kadir]**

When the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — returned to Madinah, he remained there only seven nights before setting out to raid Bani Sulaym at al-Kadir. The Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — led the expedition himself, aiming for Bani Sulaym. He reached a watering place belonging to them known as al-Kadar. He camped there for three nights, then returned to Madinah without having engaged in any fighting. He remained in Madinah throughout Shawwal and Dhu al-Qa'dah, spending that time overseeing the ransom of the Qurayshi captives.

**Then came the Battle of al-Suwaiq:**

When the Quraysh returned to Mecca from Badr (1), Abu Sufyan ibn Harb swore an oath that he would not let water touch his head from ritual impurity until he had raided Muhammad — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family.

Abu Sufyan set out with two hundred horsemen from the Quraysh, bearing to the right as he traveled toward the Najdiyyah road. He descended at the mouth of a valley near a mountain called Tayb, which lay a post or thereabouts from the city. He departed by night until he reached Banu al-Nadir after dark. He went to Huyayy ibn Akhtab and knocked on his door, but Huyayy refused to open it, fearing him, and so Abu Sufyan left him. He then went to Sallam ibn Mishkam, who held the highest standing among Banu al-Nadir. Sallam granted him entry, received him hospitably, gave him water to drink, and informed him of the news of the people.

(1) Addition from the Biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 226 -->

Abu Sufyan then departed in the last part of the night and rejoined his companions. At dawn they rode to a place called al-'Arid, where they burned the palm-tree enclosures along its edges. They came upon two Ansari men working their land, killed them both, and then retreated.

The people set out in pursuit of them, and the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — went out to chase them until he reached Qarqarat al-Kudr, then turned back — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — having missed Abu Sufyan and his companions. The raiders had thrown down their provisions in their flight, and most of what they discarded was al-Suwaiq flour. Because of this, the expedition came to be called the Battle of al-Suwaiq.

**The Battle of Dhu Amr:**

The Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — marched out toward Najd intending to strike at Ghatafan. He camped at Najd al-Safra, then returned to Madinah without having encountered the enemy. The campaign occupied the whole of Rabi' al-Awwal, or most of it.

**Then came the Battle of Buhran:**

The Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — set out in search of the Quraysh until he reached the mines of Buhran in the Hijaz. He remained there through Jumada al-Akhirah and part of Jumada al-Ula, then returned to Madinah without having fought.

**Then came the affair of Banu Qaynuqa':**

They were Jews, and the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — had made a covenant with them. When the Battle of Badr took place, the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — gathered them in the market of Banu Qaynuqa' and said to them: "O assembly of Jews, beware lest God bring down upon you the like of what He brought down upon the Quraysh.

<!-- page 227 -->

Embrace Islam, for you know well that I am a prophet who has been sent — you will find this in your own scripture, and God has bound you to it."

They replied: "O Muhammad, do not be deceived by having defeated a people who had no knowledge of war and who gave you an opening against them. By God, if we were to fight you, you would know that we are truly a people."

**[The verses revealed concerning them]**

It was said that on account of them God Almighty revealed: *"Say to those who disbelieve: you will be overcome and gathered into Hell — and evil is the resting place. There was already a sign for you in the two forces that met: one fighting in the cause of God and another that disbelieved; they saw them with their own eyes as twice their number. And God strengthens with His victory whom He wills — in that is a lesson for those who have sight."* [Āl 'Imrān: 12–13]

**[The cause of the war between them and the Muslims]**

A woman from among them entered their marketplace and then suffered an indignity at the hands of a Jew. A Muslim man then leapt upon the Jew and killed him, and the Jews fell upon the Muslim and killed him. The Muslim community cried out, and the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — came forth and besieged them for fifteen nights. The Commander of the Faithful and master of the heroes, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him — said: "O Messenger of God, let us force our way in against them."

They were then compelled to submit to the judgment of the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — and God delivered them into his power. However, 'Abd Allah ibn Ubayy ibn Salul objected on their behalf, as he was one of the chiefs of

<!-- page 228 -->

the hypocrites. He stood before the Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — and said: "I ask you to spare them. They are four hundred men in armor and three hundred without, who have protected me against every foe, Arab and non-Arab alike." The Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — said: "They are yours."

**Then came the raid of Zayd ibn Haritha:**

The Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — dispatched him on a raiding mission and he struck the caravan of al-'Ayr. Abu Sufyan ibn Harb was in charge of it, and the caravan carried a great quantity of silver. The men fled and came to the Messenger of God in disgrace. The Quraysh had already been afraid to travel their usual route to the Levant after the events of Badr, so they had taken the road of Iraq — and it was along that road that Zayd ibn Haritha intercepted the caravan.

Hassan ibn Thabit reproached the Quraysh for choosing that road, saying (1):

Let the farmers of Damascus stand in their way —
A blow like the edge of a blade.
At the hands of men who migrated to their Lord,
With His supporters — truly — and the hands of angels.
If you walk in the valley of the sands of that land,
Tell them: there is no passage there.

**Then came the killing of Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf.**

He was a man from Tayyi', of the clan of Banu Nabhan, and his mother was from Banu al-Nadir. When the casualties of Badr reached him, he exclaimed: "Can it be that Muhammad has killed the noblest of the Arabs and the kings of men? By God, if Muhammad has truly struck down these people, the belly of the earth is better than its back."

(1) See: Diwan, 1/85.

<!-- page 229 -->

Like one wound following another —
Weep, for I have wept as a nursing slave weeps.
He insulted a people who fought him with relentless fury,
Yet the Most Merciful has healed our master.
He survived and emptied from his heart a passion
That had long lain cracked open by his fear.

**[Ka'b's harassment of Muslim women and the plot to kill him]**

Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf returned to Madinah and began composing poems designed to seduce the women of the Muslims. The Messenger of God — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — said: "Who will deal with Ibn al-Ashraf for me?"

Muhammad ibn Maslamah al-Ansari said: "I will, O Messenger of God — I will kill him." The Prophet — may Allah bless him and his family — replied: "Do so, if you are able." Muhammad ibn Maslamah said: "O Messenger of God, we will need to say things." The Prophet — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — replied: "Say whatever seems necessary to you, and I grant you leave in that."

He enlisted Salkan ibn Salama — one of the Banu 'Abd al-Ashhal, and Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf's milk-brother — together with 'Abbad ibn Bishr, al-Harith ibn Aws, others from Banu 'Abd al-Ashhal, and Abu 'Abs ibn Jabr of the Banu Haritha. They then approached the enemy of God, Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf. Before the group arrived, Salkan ibn Salama — Ka'b's milk-brother — went ahead alone, presented himself to Ka'b, and the two spoke for a while, reciting poetry back and forth. Then Salkan said: "O Ibn al-Ashraf, I have seen what this man has brought upon us" — meaning the Messenger of God, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — "and what a calamity it has been. The Arabs have grown hostile to us, our trade routes have been cut off, and even our children have gone without."

(1) Addition from the Biography of the Prophet.
(2) Addition from the Biography of the Prophet.
(3) Addition from the Biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 230 -->

When Ka'b became convinced of this, he grew alarmed. He had already gone to Mecca, where he stirred up hostility against the Messenger of God — may Allah bless him and his family — composing poems that moved the hearts of the wounded Qurayshis who had suffered loss at Badr. Among what he said:

The mill of Badr ground for your time and your kinsmen —
And for what Badr meant, you deserve to weep and mourn.
Do not stand apart while kings are struggling —
The one who rejoices is only he whom hardship has sheltered.
How many men of white-robed nobility were struck down —
A champion prevails, then falls.
The hands are loosened when the planets are left behind;
The son of the despised one lingered on, swallowing his humiliation.
He tells the people of the captivity, in their indignation —
She kept shaming her family and cracking open wounds.
They believed the earth would swallow them at that hour, slain,
Or they would live on blind, trembling, and unhearing.
The one who brought this report has become a stabbing grief;
They feared to kill Abu al-Hakim, and they held back —
All the sons of al-Mughirah,
And none came to the aid of the doomed,
Nor did the sons of Rabi'ah come to him at Manb.
I killed the secrets of peoples around their wounded.
Among the people He wills good things and gathers them;
She embraced al-Harith ibn Hisham,
Visiting Yathrib amid the crowds — yet protected by the noblest and most generous.

Hassan ibn Thabit al-Ansari replied to him:

From him — and he lived in a state of shock and unhearing —
I weep over a heel, and then over a lesson:
Dead men wipe their eyes of tears,
And I saw in its belly a full moon among them.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 3/52; al-Maghazi, 1/185–186.
(2) See: Diwan, 1/426–427.


<!-- page 231 -->
Ka'b said: "By Allah, I have told you, O Ibn Salamah, it will be exactly as I said." Silkan told him: "I want you to sell us food, and we will give you a pledge." He said: "Will you pledge your children?" Silkan replied: "You want to disgrace us among the Arabs. I have with me companions like myself, and I have brought them to you, so sell to them and do well by me. We pledge you in the transaction that there will be fulfillment" — though what they truly wanted was not to be denied their weapons. He said: "If that is the case, then it is a pledge of fulfillment."

So two men returned to their companions and ordered them to take up arms. They did so. Then Abu Nayla — who was Silkan — called out, and Ka'b was in his fortress. Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf came down. They spoke with him about what Silkan had arranged, and said: "Muhammad has become a source of hostility between us and the Arabs."

He listened to their conversation, and they said: "Can you go out with us for a walk among the people? We will talk through the rest of our night." He said: "If you wish." So they went out walking for an hour.

Then Abu Nayla put his hand on Ka'b's head, then sniffed his hand and said: "I have never smelled anything more fragrant." Ibn al-Ashraf had recently married and was still close to his wedding, and Abu Nayla moved away. Then he returned to do the same for a time until Ka'b was reassured. Then he came back once more, seized his head, and said

<!-- page 232 -->
to his companions: "Strike the enemy of God." They killed him. He cried out — the enemy of God let out a great cry — and not one of the Jewish fortresses was left without a fire being lit upon it. Al-Harith ibn Aws ibn Mu'adh was wounded when their swords struck him by mistake. They carried him and made it safely until they reached the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — while he was standing in prayer. When he finished and gave the salutation, he came out to them, and they informed him of the killing of the enemy of God. The Prophet praised them and commended them greatly, and from that point on the Jews were gripped by a severe and lasting fear.

Ka'b ibn Malik said in commemoration of the killing of Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf:

After his death, the harm was brought low,
And a piercing cry returned from them.
On the palms of hands his sickness was met
With our hands — a celebrated man.
For the heel of a brother, a heel that moves
By the command of Muhammad, who strikes at night,
So he is cunning, and I bring him down by cunning,
And Mahmoud, the brother of the trust, is the bridge.

[The Marriage of the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, to Fatima, peace be upon her]

After the event of Badr came the marriage of the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abu Talib — peace be upon him — to Fatimah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family. Both Abu Bakr and 'Umar had sought her hand, but the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — declined them both. He would hear mention of her virtue and would say: "Fatimah is a branch of me."

A group of the companions of the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — spoke among themselves and said: "Have you not noticed that the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — has refrained from

<!-- page 233 -->
marrying his daughter Fatimah to anyone?"

'Ammar ibn Yasir said: "We see no one more fitting for her than his cousin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him."

He — peace be upon him — had not yet married. 'Ammar, al-Zubayr ibn al-'Awwam, and Sa'd ibn 'Ubadah said: "Let us speak to 'Ali about this."

They came to him, and 'Ammar said: "We see no one who has emigrated to Medina who was not granted a child after his emigration — except you."

He said to him — 'Ammar: "If I prevent you from speaking, then tell me what is in your hand of wealth. That is my wealth, so take from it what you like." Ali — peace be upon him — said: "O my father! What Allah granted me at Badr is what I set aside for this matter." They said: "If you had mentioned to the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — his daughter Fatimah — peace be upon her — we believe he has refrained from marrying her to any of his companions, and we do not think he has left her for anyone else." Ali said — peace be upon him: "Fatimah belongs to the Imams of Allah, and her affair is in the hand of Allah. My tongue turns around wishing to mention it to the Messenger of Allah, yet I feel shy before him." They said to him: "And who is more deserving of it than you? We will mention it to the Messenger of Allah."

Then they returned to the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — and spoke with him about it. At that point Gabriel — peace be upon him — descended to the Prophet — may Allah bless him and his family — and said: "Allah the Almighty commands you to respond to what they have asked of you, for I have married Fatimah to Ali in

<!-- page 234 -->
the same hour that Eve was married to Adam, and this was in My judgment, and I had already made it known in what is better than My creation."

The Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — smiled at this and said: "Allah the Almighty has answered your request. Go, O 'Ammar, and bring me my son-in-law."

'Ammar said: So I came to Ali — peace be upon him. He was bowing and prostrating, making supplication to Allah concerning Fatimah — peace be upon her. I said: "O my lord, I bring you glad tidings that will heal your soul, delight your eyes, and fulfill the response of the Messenger of Allah."

So Ali — peace be upon him — came to the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family. When he entered upon him, he said: "O Ali, do you know what Sa'd and 'Ammar have said?" He said: "Yes, O Messenger of Allah."

He said: "O Ali, Gabriel has come and told me that Allah has given you Fatimah, and has given her to you, and has joined her to you in marriage, and has granted you a gift. What dowry do you have?"

The Messenger of Allah — peace be upon him — said: "What is that?"

He said: "I have set aside for that twenty ounces of gold and silver, two black slaves, a horse for battle, and a shield to face my enemy."

<!-- page 235 -->
The Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — said: "O Ali, do not let the horse go from your hand, for it is for riding and raiding, nor the two black slaves. Do not give up your horse, and do not surrender any of your weapons. But bring whatever gold and silver you have."

Then the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — contracted the marriage of Fatimah — peace be upon her — for him. He commanded — may Allah bless him and his family — and a ram was purchased, along with fat, flour, and 'Ajwa dates, and food was prepared. He then ordered a caller to announce throughout the quarters of the city that people should come to the Messenger of Allah. The people came to him.

Those who ate were counted and they numbered one thousand and six hundred, yet the food remained as it had been and was not diminished in the least, by the blessing of the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — and his grace.

The Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — heard the sound of a tambourine and said: "What is this?" Asma' bint Umays said: "O Messenger of Allah, I wanted to bring joy to Fatimah, for I feared she might say to herself: My mother has died and there is no one left to delight me." He said: "O Asma', what are you saying?" She said: "I do not know what to say, O Messenger of Allah!" He said: "We have come to you, we have come to you — so greet us." He said: "Say:

<!-- page 236 -->
the like, and do not say farewell."

Then he said: "May Allah bring you joy, O Asma', as I have brought joy to my son."

The Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — then instructed that Fatimah be brought to Ali — peace be upon him.

With the chain of transmission of al-Mufaddal ibn Muhammad, from Ibn 'Abbas, he said: "When Fatimah — peace be upon her — was brought to Ali — peace be upon him — the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — walked before her, Gabriel was on her right, and Michael on her left. Seventy thousand angels accompanied her from behind, glorifying Allah the Almighty and sanctifying Him until the break of dawn."

[From Ibn al-'Arabi, with his chain of transmission] Asma' bint Umays said: "I was among those who accompanied Fatimah — peace be upon her — to Ali — peace be upon him. When she entered her house, the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — came forward until he entered upon her. He called for water, the name of Allah was invoked over it, he drank from it, and she drank from it.

<!-- page 237 -->
He then sprinkled water between Fatimah's veil and her body, and likewise between Ali's garment and his body.

Then he said: 'O Allah, protect the people of this house, bless them, and bestow Your blessings upon them wherever they may be.'

Then he said: 'May Allah reward Asma' and those like her with good.'"

Ahmad ibn Muhammad al-Tabari, with his chain of transmission, from Anas ibn Malik, said: "I asked my mother about the description of Fatimah — peace be upon her — and she said: White as an egg, as though she were the moon on the night of its fullness, and as the sun when it emerges from behind clouds."

The virtues of Ali and Fatimah — peace be upon them both — are many and well known. It is narrated from the Prophet — may Allah bless him and his family: when Ali married Fatimah — peace be upon them — he said: "Today faith has been doubled and Islam has been made complete."

The marriage of Ali took place after the event of Badr; it has also been said it was after the event of Uhud, though the former is the majority view.

<!-- page 238 -->
The occurrence of the battle of Uhud on a Saturday in the month of Shawwal came about as follows:

When the Quraysh suffered their losses on the day of Badr, Abu Sufyan ibn Harb stirred the Quraysh against making war on the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — and commanded them to seek revenge. He said to them: "O assembly of Quraysh, Muhammad has left and killed your finest men, so support us with this wealth to make war against him" — meaning the wealth that had been rescued from the caravan on the day of Badr.

They agreed to this, and in response to it, Allah revealed:

*The disbelievers spend their wealth to turn people away from the path of Allah. They will continue to spend it, but then it will become a source of regret for them, and then they will be overcome. And those who disbelieve will be gathered to Hell.* [Al-Anfal: 36]

[The Quraysh War Council]

So the Quraysh gathered to fight the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — after Abu Sufyan had done this. The people of the caravan in its surrounding areas joined them, as did those from the tribes of Kinanah and the people of Tihama who obeyed their call. They set out with their cavalry, their weapons, and their allies, and they brought their women along with them to prevent the men from fleeing.

Abu Sufyan — who was the commander of the army — went out with Hind bint 'Utbah. 'Ikrimah ibn Abi Jahl went out with his mother Hakim bint al-Harith ibn Hisham ibn al-Mughirah. Al-Harith ibn Hisham ibn al-Mughirah went out with Fatimah bint al-Waleed ibn al-Mughirah.

<!-- page 239 -->
Hardly any of the women of Quraysh stayed behind. They marched until they descended at a place with springs, in a mountain in the belly of the Sabkhah, beside a canal on the edge of the valley, opposite the city.

[The Vision of the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family]

When the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — was informed of their approach, the Muslims had come down. When they had dismounted, the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — said to the Muslims: "I have seen a vision — I saw a cow, I saw something in the flat of my sword, and I saw that I put my hand into a fortified coat of mail, and I interpreted it as the city."

"As for the cow, it represents people from among my companions who will be killed. And what I saw in the flat of my sword is a man from my household who will be killed."

[The Prophet's Counsel to the People on Whether to Go Out or Remain]

"My view is that you remain in Medina and let them come to where they have encamped. If they hold their ground, they will be in an unfavorable position; and if they enter upon us, we will fight them within it."

The opinion of 'Abdullah ibn Ubayy ibn Salul aligned with the opinion of the Messenger of Allah — peace be upon him — not to go out to meet them, and he agreed with


<!-- page 240 -->
Some of them said: "We should march out and fight them where our women and children cannot see us and be terrified." They refused to heed the words of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), so he withdrew in anger and ordered them to march out. When they saw his displeasure, they said: "O Messenger of Allah, we fear we have angered you against your own judgment — return and do as you see fit." But he said, may Allah bless him and his family: "Once a prophet has armed himself for his community and taken up his weapon, he does not turn back until he has fought."

[The Defection of the Hypocrites]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) set out with a thousand of his companions. When they had reached the ground between Madinah and Uhud, 'Abdullah ibn Ubayy ibn Salul withdrew with a third of the force, saying: "He obeyed them and disobeyed me. I do not know why we are throwing ourselves to our deaths here, O people." He turned back, taking with him those of his people who were hypocrites and waverers.

[A Good Omen the Prophet Took on That Day]

The Messenger of Allah continued until he passed by the quarter of the Banu Haritha, where a horse struck its sword against something and bolted. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) took this as a good omen from the sword and said: "Draw your sword — I see that swords will delight you today."

<!-- page 241 -->
One of the heroes of the Ansar asked: "And what is his due, O Messenger of Allah?" He said: "That you stand by him at the end of the ranks and strike the enemy with it until it bends." The man said: "I will take it, O Messenger of Allah." Abu Dajanah al-Ansari — Sammak ibn Kharsha, brother of the Banu Sa'ida — then took it. He brought out a red headband and tied it around his head, and the Ansar said: "Abu Dajanah has put on his death-band — whenever he did that, this is what followed." Then he strode out swaggering between the two lines, reciting:

*I am the one my close friend made a promise to,*
*and we are at the foot of the palm trees —*
*shall I not rise in the days of battle?*
*Strike with the sword of Allah and the Prophet!*

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) saw Abu Dajanah al-Ansari striding in that manner, he said: "That is a gait Allah hates — except in a place such as this."

[What Abu Sufyan Said to Incite the Standard-Bearers]

Abu Sufyan said to the standard-bearers from Banu 'Abd al-Dar: "You abandoned the standard on the day of Badr, and you brought us what you have seen. Either you take full charge of our standard, or leave it to us and let us attend to our own affair." They took him at his word and pledged themselves, saying: "Tomorrow you will know whether we have fulfilled our obligation."

<!-- page 242 -->
[The Messenger of Allah Descends with the People and Arrays Them for Battle]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) advanced until the people came down at Uhud, positioning themselves in the valley with their backs to the mountain. He said: "Let no one among you fight until he is ordered to fight." The Quraysh spread their cavalry and infantry through the cultivated fields that ran alongside a canal. A man from the Ansar called out: "Are you going to let them trample the crops of the Banu Qila — why are we not fighting?" The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then arrayed seven hundred men for battle and placed 'Abdullah ibn Jubayr, brother of the Banu 'Amr ibn 'Awf, in command of the fifty archers. He said to them: "Keep the horses away from us — let them not come at us from behind, whether the battle goes in our favour or against us. Do not leave your positions." The Messenger of Allah appeared between two shields, and the Quraysh — three thousand strong, with two hundred horses — were drawn up in array.

[The Story of Abu Dajanah al-Ansari]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) raised his sword and said: "Who will take this sword and give it its right?" Al-Zubayr said: "I will, O Messenger of Allah." Abu Dajanah al-Ansari also said:

<!-- page 243 -->
"And what is its right, O Messenger of Allah?" — and he took it. Abu Sufyan meanwhile sought to rouse his men further.

Hind bint 'Utbah and the women with her beat their tambourines to incite the fighters.

[What Hind Did to Stir Up the Polytheists]

She cried out among the polytheists, saying:

*We are the daughters of Tariq,*
*we walk upon the cushioned litters —*
*if you advance, we embrace you,*
*and spread soft carpets beneath you;*
*but if you turn and flee,*
*we part from you, a bitter parting,*
*a parting that leaves no love behind,*
*and shame upon you ever after.*

She also said:

*Be steadfast, O Banu 'Abd al-Dar,*
*protectors of those who guard the rear —*
*strike with all the force of vengeance.*

<!-- page 244 -->
[The Battle-Cry of the Muslims]

The battle-cry of the companions of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) on that Sunday was: "O Mansur, put them to death!" — meaning: be relentless and press on.

[The Complete Account of Abu Dajanah]

The people fought until the battle was fully joined. Abu Dajanah fought until he was the wonder of all who looked on.

Al-Zubayr ibn al-'Awwam said: "I resented the fact that I had asked the Messenger of Allah for the sword and he gave it to Abu Dajanah instead of me — and I am the son of Safiyyah, his aunt, and from the Quraysh. By Allah, let me watch what Abu Dajanah does." So I followed him.

He met no one but he killed him. Among the polytheists there was a man who spared no wounded Muslim — he finished every one of them off. I prayed to Allah to bring the two of them together, and so they met. They traded two blows: the polytheist struck Abu Dajanah, who caught the blow on his shield, and the sword bit into it; then Abu Dajanah struck the polytheist and killed him. Then I saw him raise the sword over the head of Hind bint 'Utbah — but he held back and pulled away. Later Abu Dajanah said: "I deemed it beneath the honour of the sword of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) to use it on a woman."

<!-- page 245 -->
[The Affliction of Hamza]

Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib fought on, killing Arta'ah ibn 'Abd Sharhabil ibn Hashim ibn 'Abd Manaf ibn 'Abd al-Dar, who was one of the standard-bearers. Then Saba' ibn 'Abd al-'Azza al-Ghubshani — who was called Abu Nayyar — passed by him, and Hamza said: "Come to me, O son of the one who mutilates eyes!" — for his mother practiced such mutilation. When they met, Hamza struck him and killed him.

[The Intensity of the Fighting and the Exploits of Ali — Peace Be Upon Him]

The fighting raged fiercely, and Ali (peace be upon him) and Hamza wrought havoc among the polytheists. The standard-bearer of the polytheists, Talha ibn Abi Talha, called out: "O companions of Muhammad! You claim that whoever among us is killed goes to the Fire, and whoever among you is killed goes to Paradise. Which of you will come forward to hasten me to the Fire and hasten himself to Paradise?" 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) stepped forward and said: "By Allah, I will not leave you until I send you to the Fire, God willing." They clashed, and when the shield slipped from Talha's leg, 'Ali (peace be upon him) struck him with the sword, severing his leg, and stood over him intending to finish him — but Talha called upon Allah and pleaded for mercy. 'Ali (peace be upon him) said: "When he invoked Allah and pleaded for mercy, I was too ashamed to kill him, so I left him knowing he would not survive." Talha died from that blow.

After him the standard passed to Abu Sa'id ibn Abi Talha, and Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas — peace be upon him — killed him.

<!-- page 246 -->
Then 'Uthman ibn Abi Talha took the standard, and Hamza killed him. Then Musafi'a ibn Talha took it, and 'Asim ibn Thabit ibn Abi al-Aqlah killed him.

The Muslims were prevailing in the fighting; they struck the polytheists with killing blows and left them wounded and routed. But when the polytheists saw their defeat, those archers whom the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had ordered to hold their positions abandoned them, chasing after the enemy in pursuit of spoils and saying: "We missed the plunder on the day of Badr." When the archers had left the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) exposed, Abu Sufyan, Khalid ibn al-Walid, and the polytheists seized the moment.

[The Killing of Hamza — Peace Be Upon Him]

Wahshi, the slave of Jubayr ibn Mut'im, watched Hamza as he roared through the battle like a lion — no polytheist who came near him was left alive. Jubayr had told Wahshi: "If you kill Hamza, the uncle of Muhammad, in exchange for my uncle Tu'ayma ibn 'Adi, you are free." And Hind herself had promised Wahshi a reward for killing Hamza.

So Wahshi lay in wait for Hamza (peace be upon him) behind a tree. Just then Saba' ibn 'Abd al-'Azza came at him, and Hamza (peace be upon him) leapt upon him and killed him. At that moment Wahshi hurled his spear, which struck Hamza (peace be upon him) in the lower body. Hamza (peace be upon him) moved toward him but was overcome and fell.

The polytheists also killed Mus'ab ibn 'Umayr as he held the banner of the Ansar in the hands of the Messenger — may Allah bless him and his family.

<!-- page 247 -->
[The Washing of Hanzala by the Angels]

Hanzala ibn Abi 'Amir met Abu Sufyan and, as he was raising his sword to strike him down, Shaddad ibn al-Aswad — a man from the Shu'ub — killed him before he could reach Abu Sufyan. He was called "al-Ghusl," the washed one, because the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "I saw the angels washing Hanzala ibn Abi 'Amir" — for he had gone out to fight without having purified himself.

[The Exchange: "You Were Killed in Paradise; You Were Killed in the Fire"]

When Hanzala was killed, Abu Sufyan cried out: "Up, O Hubal!" — and called: Hanzala for Hanzala, meaning his own son, who had been killed at Badr by 'Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) replied: "Allah is most high and most mighty. Are our slain in Paradise while your slain are in the Fire?"

[The Courage of the Standard-Bearer and Hassan's Poetry on the Matter]

When the standard of the polytheists fell as the standard-bearer was killed, 'Umra bint 'Alqama al-Harithiyyah took it up and raised it again for the Quraysh. The standard then passed to a slave of the Quraysh who fought on with it until he was killed. He cried out: "O Allah, have I acquitted myself?" — meaning: have I been excused?

<!-- page 248 -->
In response to this, Hassan ibn Thabit al-Ansari said:

*A standard-bearer — when he returned to the right course,*
*you boasted of the standard, and how vile a boast —*
*and the pain of one whose soil is trampled underfoot.*
*You placed your pride in a slave,*
*and that is no right thing.*
*You supposed — and the fool has his suspicions —*
*that in Mecca you could sell red shame.*
*Yet our swords met you on the day we clashed,*
*and as soon as you grew fanatical about the dye.*
*He admitted that his hands were bound.*

[Hassan's Poetry on 'Umra bint 'Alqama]

Hassan ibn Thabit also said concerning 'Umra bint 'Alqama al-Harithiyyah when she raised the standard:

*If an arm were flung at us like the tie of a brow, stirring teachers,*
*and their grief from blows raining from every side —*
*we stabbed them and struck them, stab upon stab,*
*while they sold their milk in the markets.*
*Were it not for the brigade of the Haritha, they would have come to ruin.*

[The Archers Abandon Their Positions]

When the archers abandoned their positions and defied the command of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), the polytheists rallied, the Muslims were routed, and a voice cried out — it was the voice of Satan, from whom we seek refuge in Allah — "Muhammad has been killed!"

<!-- page 249 -->
[The Affair of Anas ibn al-Nadir]

When 'Amr was routed, Anas ibn al-Nadir encountered him and said to him that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had been killed. Anas said: "What use is life to you after him? Go back and die for what the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family died for." Then he turned toward the enemy and fought on until he was killed — may Allah have mercy on him.

[What the Prophet Met on That Day]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was left nearly alone. The polytheists struck down those around him, wounded him, broke his lower right tooth, cut his lip, shattered the egg of his helmet on his head, and landed upon him blows of great severity. The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had been standing between two shields at that time. 'Ali (peace be upon him) came to him and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) stumbled into a pit; 'Ali (peace be upon him) took his hand and helped him to his feet. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) stood upon a rock, and 'Ali (peace be upon him) remained alone at his side. When the people kept pressing in on him, the Messenger of Allah said: "Go forward, O 'Ali." He replied: "Where shall I go, O Messenger of Allah? I will not turn back a disbeliever after having become a Muslim." When the polytheists came at them, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) urged 'Ali against them, saying: "Bear down on these others, O 'Ali." And 'Ali would drive back those who came at them, inflicting losses upon them before they withdrew — and he was counted among the bravest of those present.


<!-- page 250 -->
Among the accounts narrated concerning that day, Abu Rafi' reported: On a Sunday, when the battle was at its fiercest and the polytheists were pressing hard against the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), Ali ibn Abi Talib stood beside him. The Messenger of Allah said, "Go, O Ali." Ali replied, "Where shall I go while I am with you?" At that moment Ali spotted a battalion advancing, and the Messenger of Allah said to him, "O Ali, charge that battalion." So he charged it and broke through it, killing Hisham ibn Ummah al-Makhzumi, and drove it off. Then a second battalion came forward, and the Prophet said, "Charge it." So he charged it and killed 'Amr ibn 'Abd Allah al-Jumahi and scattered it. Then a third battalion advanced, and the Prophet said, "Charge it." So he charged it as well.

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 286/1, where it is noted: "This is found in a second narration related by Ahmad ibn 'Ali al-Baghdadi, with his isnād from Abu Rafi', who said:"
(2) Addition in Sharh al-Akhbar.
(3) Addition in Sharh al-Akhbar.
(4) "Upon it" in Sharh al-Akhbar.
(5) Addition in Sharh al-Akhbar.
(6) "Upon it" in Sharh al-Akhbar.
(7) Addition in Sharh al-Akhbar.

<!-- page 251 -->
[Pg 147]

There was no young man like Ali [5/112] on the day of Uhud.

**The Battle of Uhud**

He killed Shaybah ibn Malik, the brother of Banu 'Amir ibn Lu'ayy, in that encounter.

When Gabriel (peace be upon him) witnessed what Ali (peace be upon him) was doing in defense of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), he said: "O Muhammad, this is true loyalty." The Prophet said: "He is of me, O Gabriel, and I am of him." Gabriel replied: "And I am of you both, O Muhammad." Thus the angels were called Hashimite.

A voice was then heard calling out from heaven:

*There is no sword but Dhu al-Fiqar,*
*and there is no young man but Ali.*

When Ali (peace be upon him) held firm, the Muslims rallied back around the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas also took a stand beneath him. Malik ibn Sinan, the father of Abu Sa'id al-Khudri, came and sucked the blood from the wound of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), then swallowed it. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Whoever's blood mingles with mine, the Fire shall not touch him."

The two rings of the helmet had driven into the cheek of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and Abu 'Ubaydah ibn al-Jarrah drew them out with his teeth; two of his own teeth fell out in doing so.

At that point the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had cast aside his bow until it was no longer of use.

(1) See: Sīra al-Nabawiyyah 100/3; al-Munammaq fī Akhbār Quraysh 411; Tārīkh al-Tabarī 514/2; Sharh al-Akhbār 94/1, 536/2; Amālī al-Tūsī 271.
(2) In copies (a) and (b): "Sa'd" — what has been established is from Sīra al-Nabawiyyah 80/3.
(3) See: Sīra al-Nabawiyyah 80/3.

<!-- page 252 -->
**[The Killing of Ubayy ibn Khalaf]**

Ubayy ibn Khalaf came forward, calling out: "Where is Muhammad? I shall not survive if he survives." Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) said: "O Messenger of Allah, this is Ubayy ibn Khalaf — shall I go to him?" The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "I will deal with him myself." Those around him tried to hold him back, but he broke free from their hands, snatched a spear from one of them, and went to meet Ubayy. He struck him such a blow that Ubayy nearly fell from his horse [112], and Ubayy fled.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had previously met him in Makkah, where Ubayy had said: "Muhammad, if you do not finish me off, I will kill you." The Messenger of Allah had replied: "I will kill you, God willing."

When Ubayy rejoined his companions, he was writhing in agony. They said to him: "There is nothing seriously wrong with you — why this state?" He replied: "He told me in Makkah that he would kill me. Had he but spat upon me, it would have killed me." And so the enemy of Allah died from that wound while they were carrying him back toward Makkah.

**[The Prophet's Prayer — Performed Sitting]**

It is related that on that day the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) prayed the noon prayer with his companions

[Pg 148]

sitting, because of the pain of his injuries and his inability to stand, and he ordered those praying behind him to pray sitting as well.

It is also said that the one who broke the facial bones of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family)

(1) Addition in Sīra al-Nabawiyyah.

<!-- page 253 -->
was 'Utbah ibn Abi Waqqas, who struck him with a stone and hit him. 'Abd Allah ibn Shihab al-Zuhri stabbed him in the forehead, and Ibn Qumi'ah al-Harithi inflicted a wound on his cheek. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) received these blows from the volley the polytheists unleashed against him. The polytheists were then repelled and driven back, but the Muslims had already retreated — it was a day of trial and severe testing.

**[The Martyrdom of Hamza — Peace Be Upon Him]**

Hamza (peace be upon him) was killed and then mutilated. Hind tore out his liver, chewed it, and found it would not go down. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) saw what had been done to him, he was deeply grieved and said: "If Allah grants me victory over them, I will mutilate seventy of them in return."

Allah (Glorified and Exalted be He) then revealed to him: ⟪ar:وَإِنْ عَاقَبْتُمْ فَعَاقِبُوا بِمِثْلِ مَا عُوقِبْتُمْ بِهِ وَلَئِن صَبَرْتُمْ لَهُوَ خَيْرٌ لِّلصَّابِرِينَ⟫ "And if you retaliate, retaliate with the like of that with which you were afflicted; but if you are patient, it is better for those who are patient" [al-Nahl: 126]. And the Muslims were also gladdened by what they had inflicted on the enemy. So Allah revealed: ⟪ar:أَوَلَمَّا أَصَابَتْكُم مُّصِيبَةٌ قَدْ أَصَبْتُم مِّثْلَيْهَا قُلْتُمْ أَنَّىٰ هَٰذَا ۖ قُلْ هُوَ مِنْ عِندِ أَنفُسِكُمْ ۗ إِنَّ اللَّهَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَيْءٍ قَدِيرٌ⟫ "When a disaster struck you, though you had struck twice as much, you said: 'How is this?' Say: 'It is from yourselves, for Allah is over all things competent'" [Al 'Imran: 165] — meaning: because they had disobeyed the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) when they left Madinah and then abandoned the archers' posts.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then ordered that the martyrs be buried where they had fallen.

(1) Addition in Sīra al-Nabawiyyah.

<!-- page 254 -->
Hind al-Khabiyyah said, in verse, regarding the killing of Hamza (peace be upon him) and what she did to him:

*I satisfied myself on Hamza*
*when his liver was drawn from his belly.*
*Then it left me — I could not keep it down —*
*from the piercing grief that had seized hold of me.*

She also said:

[Pg 149]

[Pg 113]

*We repaid you for the day of Badr,*
*for war follows war, blow for blow.*
*I could not bear the loss of my father,*
*nor my brother, my uncle, and my firstborn.*
*I have healed myself and fulfilled my vow,*
*and mended my fierce, unyielding heart.*
*A wild gratitude remains in my life*
*until my dearest joins me in the grave.*

Hind bint 'Utbah ibn 'Abbad ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib ibn 'Abd al-Manaf replied to her, saying:

(1) See: Sīra al-Nabawiyyah 92/3.
(2) See: Sīra al-Nabawiyyah 91/3.
(3) See: Sīra al-Nabawiyyah 91/3–92.

<!-- page 255 -->
**The Battle of Uhud**

[Pg 150]

*I was shamed by Badr and what came before Badr —*
*O woman, the depth of that disgrace is immense.*
*May Allah have blessed your morning after that dawn*
*at the hands of the towering Hashimites in full bloom.*
*In every quarter a Husam was running them through —*
*Hamza the lion, and Ali the hawk.*
*If your Rabb and your father were treacherous,*
*the outskirts of the sacrifice were shaken by it.*
*I warn you of the evil of the vow —*
*you were brutally given to a hardened conscience,*
*a brutal assault behind the veil of concealment.*
*The harlots have no harlotry left now.*

It is related that Hind would have mutilated herself as she mutilated Hamza, had he been taken alive. She was among those who had held the tambourines. Hassan ibn Thabit al-Ansari composed verses about her, saying:

*Hind of the long clitoris —*
*may Allah curse her and her husband with her.*
*She brought out a dancer for someone*
*— your father and your son — on the day of Badr.*
*The young men of Makkah are not those who cover,*
*and your uncle is at the level of deterrence.*

(1) See: Dīwānuhu 384/1.

<!-- page 256 -->
It is related that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) [114/f] had never stood in any position that filled him with more grief than when he saw what they had done to Hamza. He said: "I have never stood in a position that has made me angrier than this."

Gabriel (peace be upon him) then descended and said: "O Muhammad, it is written among the people of the seven heavens that Hamza is the lion of Allah and the lion of His Messenger."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then ordered that a cloak be brought, and he prayed over Hamza with seven takbīrs. The other martyrs were then brought and laid beside [Pg 114/f] Hamza (may Allah be pleased with him), and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) prayed over them — each group laid beside Hamza — until he had prayed over him seventy-two times in all.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was told: "Safiyyah bint 'Abd al-Muttalib has come to look upon her brother Hamza (peace be upon him)." He said to al-Zubayr: "Go to her and turn her back — do not let her see what has been done to her brother, for she will be greatly distressed." Al-Zubayr went to her and said: "O my mother, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) commands you to return." She replied: "Why? I have been told that my brother was mutilated, and that this was in the cause of Allah. I am content with it, and I shall be patient, God willing."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Let her go to him." So she came

[Pg 151]

looked upon him, prayed over him [114/z], sought forgiveness for him, and then returned.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then commanded that Hamza and the rest of the martyrs be buried.

(1) Addition in Sīra al-Nabawiyyah.
(2) Addition in Sīra al-Nabawiyyah.

<!-- page 257 -->
**The Battle of Uhud**

He also ordered that the martyrs be buried where they had fallen, each in the spot where he lay. He said: "I bear witness over these people. Every Muslim who is wounded in the path of Allah — his wound shall come on the Day of Resurrection as it was: its color the color of blood, its scent the scent of musk."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then returned to Madinah. As he entered the city and passed by the houses of the Ansar, he could hear their women weeping for their dead. Tears came to his own eyes, and he said: "But there is no one weeping for Hamza." The Ansar heard this and ordered their women to go to the mosque and weep for Hamza. They did so, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) came out, weeping for Hamza. Then he had the door of the mosque closed and said to them: "Return home — may Allah have mercy on you — for you have done what was right. But lamentation is forbidden."

**[The Washing of the Swords]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) entered his house, Fatimah (peace be upon her) came out to receive him. He handed her his sword and said: "Wash the blood from this, O my daughter — it has served me faithfully today."

And Ali (peace be upon him) gave her his sword, Dhu al-Fiqar [115/f], saying to her:

(1) Addition in Sīra al-Nabawiyyah.
(2) In copies (a) and (b): + "Uhud" — we have chosen to omit it.
(3) See: Dīwān, 129–130.

<!-- page 258 -->
*I have not been moved by wealth or any price,*
*but by obedience to a merciful and devoted servant.*
*For my life: she declared her love for Ahmad —*
*may he be rewarded from every rebel and hard of heart.*
*My sword alone, like a shooting star, sufficed —*
*I drove on until Allah broke up their ranks.*
*And even the breath of every clement soul found healing.*
*Fatimah, this sword bears no reproach.*

**[The Messenger of Allah Pursues the Enemy]**

On the following day — Sunday, the sixteenth of Shawwal — the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) called upon the people to pursue the enemy. He stipulated that only those who had gone out on the first day should march with him, and none who had stayed behind. He advanced with them — may Allah's blessings and peace be upon him and his family — as far as Hamra' al-Asad, which is eight miles from Madinah. He remained there on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday, then returned to the city.

Along the way he encountered the Banu Ibn Abi — a tribe divided between polytheists and Muslims — who served as advisors to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). On that day one of them said: "O Muhammad, by Allah, what has befallen your companions has grieved us deeply." They then marched alongside the Messenger of Allah in the wake of the enemy, until word reached Abu Sufyan ibn Harb and those with him. They had been unanimous in turning back against the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his companions, saying: "We have struck down their companions, their nobles, and their leaders — let us go back and finish them off before they recover." But when Abu Sufyan heard the account of Ma'bad, he asked: "What news do you bring, Ma'bad?" Ma'bad replied: "Muhammad has gone out with his companions to seek you, in a gathering the likes of which I have never seen — they burn with fury against you, and those who had stayed behind have now joined them."

[Pg 152]


<!-- page 259 -->

Abu Sufyan asked Ma'bad what lay behind him. Ma'bad replied that the Prophet had marched out with his companions to pursue them, assembling a force the like of which he had never seen — men burning with fury against them, joined now by those who had previously held back. He said: "Woe to you! What are you saying?" Ma'bad answered: "By Allah, I think you should not leave until you see the hooves of the horses." Abu Sufyan said: "By Allah, we had resolved to fall upon them again and finish off the rest of them." Ma'bad said: "I am warning you. By Allah, what I witnessed drove me to compose verses about it." Abu Sufyan said: "And what did you say?" He replied: "I said:

[0/116]
When the earth was filled with the clouds,
I almost lost my voice.
When you meet, and there is no lean of isolation
You will return with a generous lion, do not be fooled
So I remained an enemy, I think the earth was tilted / When they were called an undefeated president.
If Albatha is immersed in tricks
I said: Woe to Ibn Harb from meeting you.
For everyone who has a wife of them and is it possible
Yes ◌ to the people of Basil a suburb
And it is described that I was not excited by gossip
From Ahmed's army, no monster"

Abu Sufyan and those with him turned back toward Mecca. When word reached the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), he said — and the lion's red: "By the One in whose hand my breath rests, I had intended to pursue them; had they pressed forward, they would have been like yesterday."

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) encountered Mu'awiya ibn al-Mughirah ibn Abi al-'As ibn Umayya ibn 'Abd Shams — who was the grandfather of 'Abd al-Malik ibn Marwan through his father, and 'A'isha bint Mu'awiya — and said: "O Zubayr,

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 103/3.

<!-- page 260 -->

strike his neck." So al-Zubayr struck his neck. It was said that 'Uthman had wanted to grant him protection, whereupon the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) ordered two of his companions — Zayd ibn Haritha and 'Ammar ibn Yasir — to strike his neck. And Allah knows best.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) returned to Medina. Seventy men were martyred on that Sunday, four of them

[116/z] from the Muhajireen, among them Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib (peace be upon him) — "the Master of the Martyrs."

**[Mention of some of the polytheists killed on Sunday]**

Twenty-two men were killed among the polytheists:

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) slew 'Amr ibn 'Abdullah ibn Umayra ibn Wahb ibn Hudhaqah ibn Jumah Sabra.

He also killed — may Allah bless him and his family — Ubayy ibn Khalaf from Banu Jumah.

'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) killed: Talha ibn Abi Talha and Abu Sa'id ibn Abi Talha — though it was also said that Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas killed the latter — both of whom were from Banu 'Abd al-Dar. As for the right account: an Abyssinian slave was killed by 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him).

Abu Ummah ibn Abi Hudhayfah ibn al-Mughirah, from Banu Makhzum, was killed by 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him).

<!-- page 261 -->

'Abdullah ibn Humayd ibn Zuhayr, from Banu Asad ibn 'Abd al-'Uzza ibn Qusay, was killed by 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him).

Abu al-Hakam ibn al-Akhnas, from Banu Zuhra ibn Kilab, was killed by 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him).

'Uthman ibn Abi Talha, from Banu 'Abd al-Dar, was killed by Hamza (peace be upon him). Artah ibn 'Abd Sharhabil ibn Hashim ibn 'Abd Manaf ibn 'Abd al-Dar was likewise killed by Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib (peace be upon him). [Pg 117]

Saba' ibn 'Abd al-'Uzza, an ally of theirs from Khuza'a, was killed by Hamza (peace be upon him). [Pg 154]

Musafi' and al-Jallas, the sons of Talha ibn 'Abd al-Dar, were killed by 'Asim ibn Thabit ibn Abi al-Aqlah.

Among the rest of the slain polytheists:

Al-Harith ibn Talha was killed by a man from the Ansar.

Kilab ibn Talha, from Banu 'Abd al-Dar, was killed by 'Abd al-Rahman ibn 'Awf.

Abu Zayd ibn 'Umayr ibn Hashim was killed by Qarman — and Qarman was an ally of the Banu al-Najjar among the Ansar.

Al-Qasit ibn Shurayh, from Banu 'Abd al-Dar, was killed by Abu Dajanah al-Ansari.

Hisham ibn Abi Umayya ibn al-Mughirah was killed by Qarman.

<!-- page 262 -->

Khalid ibn al-A'lam was killed by Abu Dajanah al-Ansari.

'Ubaydah ibn Jabir was killed by 'Abdullah ibn Mas'ud.

Shayba ibn Malik ibn al-Mu'dabh was killed by Qarman.

**[The Prophet's verdict concerning Qarman as a hypocrite, and what came to pass]**

It is related in the biography of Ibn Hisham (1):

There was a man among us whose identity was uncertain; it was said his name was Qarman. (2) The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "Qarman is one of the people of the Fire." On that Sunday, Qarman fought the polytheists with extraordinary ferocity, killing eight or seven of them single-handedly. He was struck by many wounds, and was carried to the house of Banu Zafar. Some of the Muslims came to him and said: "By Allah, you have worn yourself out today, O Qarman — rejoice!" He said: "What should I rejoice about? I fought only for the sake of my people, and for no other reason." When his wounds grew severe, he drew an arrow from his quiver and killed himself with it.

**[Mention of poetry composed about the Battle of Uhud]**

'Ali ibn Abi Talib said concerning that Sunday (3):

Praise be to Allah, my Lord, the one who is steadfast, no one shares his wisdom

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 88/3.
(2) An addition not found in the Prophet's biography.
(3) See: Diwan, 59–60.

<!-- page 263 -->

He is the one who made the disbelievers known by their disbelief
Talha we left him hunched
And the person 'Uthman wanted us
And the plates have fire between us
So he brought his wife from his loss
They did not give up on menstruation when they came back
There are nine and a brigade between their backs
So the birds and the eagles kept devouring him, and he was carrying a few of them, and he was lying down.
From us, they have encountered good and they have been happy.
And who did you say about what was a wonder
And the believers will mourn them with what they have promised
If it was a country they had a sermon
Is it possible that he will see in her absence a rational one?
Victory and scheming against the disbelievers when they have
And may God help him from the God
Who included one of our brothers
If you pronounce proudly, I don't care.
Where are the noses, where is the branch and the number
They were the wolf from Fahr and honored her
Under the Ajaj Whoever is diligent
And Ahmad al-Khayr has replied to Ahal.
They are not covered by heat or screaming
They have madness from Paradise
So the scene is true before it they witnessed
God prayed on them whenever they mentioned
Smell the den of them is the lion's red
Stand up and be loyal to the Messenger of Allah and count
Until you get a fox body from him
And Mus'ab remained without him in a hurry
The fire of hell is on its doors monitoring
They are not like the dead of the infidels, let them in

He (peace be upon him) also said in this regard (1):

I saw the polytheists rebelling against us and resorting to temptation and prayer

Thin Limit accident with Saqqal

It is like an agate in the shadows.
As if salt is mixed with it if

If they sell and be proud of us

Preventing the blame for dinner worries and imagination if the worries are gone
He is sick, he is inside a muffled

It was said that Hassan ibn Thabit composed this poem one night during the 'Isha' hour, [157] calling his people together, and declaring: "I feared that my fate would overtake me before I could tell them." (3) It was said to be the most eloquent of his verse.

(1) An addition not found in the Prophet's biography.

Hassan also said, in elegy of Hamza ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib (1):

Oh May, go and find the magicians of the scarcity of lamentations
Like the bearers of reverence in the least of the salts
The silent pickaxes are free faces and right
It is as if the torrent of her tears is dyed with sacrifices
They shake their poems there in the desert / the tourists
As if they were the tails of horses in the sacrifice the sun of the lambs
Among the evil and the besieged he is afraid of the evils
They cry begging for the loots that the toilers toiled them
And her heart has been hit by a place that brings roaches
I mean the two events, who we were hoping for,
The friends of one of their dear ones have a pain that has wounds
Who was our knight and protector if he sent the messengers
Hamz, no, I swear I won't forget you, I didn't get the vaccines
For a climate of orphans, guests and widows
And when the time is on behalf of war for war and it is bad
Oh knight you are the one who is the one who is mad
We have very engaged women if they are beputed to them
He reminded me of the lion of the prophet and he was the source of the benefits

[Pg 119]

[Pg 158]

[0/120]

Here he was counting when he counted the shrapnels

Our condolences I say that our strength is a blessing in history

[Pg 159] [120/z] Al-Hajjaj ibn Alaat al-Sulami praised the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), commemorating his killing of Talha ibn Abi Talha, the standard-bearer of the polytheists (1):

I mean the son of Fatima al-Ma'am, the authoritarian
To Allah, which is a shofar, is forbidden
Your hands preceded him in a hurry
I left a patch for the forehead shaved
By dragging when they are dear, I turn my brothers
Bassel's intensity intensified and exposed them

Ka'b ibn Malik lamented Hamza (peace be upon him) and said (2):

And I was scared that the young people were armed
Your love is gone and you are upholstered

So let the temptation go too far.
And you have been obedient
Or wake up if the guide exhausts you
And I threatened to lose that redness

And if he scares Heraa like him
I knocked your worries, and sleeping is a mess
I bid farewell to your heart to love and my conscience

<!-- page 269 -->
At the Battle of Uhud, Kurm managed to penetrate the ranks of the Banu Hashim,
and the shame of it endures if it is remembered.

Where the barn, the dew, and the darkness —
a confusion from which the water nearly freezes.

The one who abandons the measured horn is brought low
on the day of the foul deed and the qana.

Dhu Labd, Chen al-Barastan, Irbid —
and you see him striking through iron as though

above the vein there surges a foaming torrent.

Or the sons of al-Mughirah — we have dealt them a blow
as the pigeon rose, and that wellspring ran dry.

Uncle of the Prophet Muhammad — Wasfiyya —
they stood by the Prophet, and among them was the martyr.

He passed on as a teacher within his family,
to die within a branch that does not offer shade.

And I took that as glad tidings —
a day when the happiest is absent from her joy.

From what we became, in the necklace of its people,
Jibril was under our banner and Muhammad before us,

and Badr cut them down as it turned their faces back.

Two fates: those He wills to slay, and those expelled —
seventy: at their threshold, and lions among them.

So he dwelt, perfumed with the fragrance of the gifted ones.
Wrath at the hands of the believing Muhannad.

The nation of al-Hamhi — the people of his devotion —
and the horses threw off their riders like ostriches.

So you are filled with polytheists as though they were
nothing — and whoever abides in tenderness is immortal.

What awaits him in Hell is Thawia.

Until I saw their secrets unveiled before the Prophet.

Hassan ibn Thabit said, weeping for Hamza (peace be upon him):

After you, toward the downward path one asks —
do you know the house, chaste and marked?

Between al-Saradeh and the well of the soul at Hail,
I asked her of it; she was bewildered, and did not know what the questioner sought.

(1) See: Diwana 321/1–322.

<!-- page 270 -->
And weep for Hamza, the one of noble gifts,
dust upon him in the age of the Sunnah.

He was one tempted by withered grief,
and the one who abandons the century stands at his horn.

And the horses — if you hold them back —
he is like Laith in his formidable forests.

Radiance at the summit of Hashim,
he never passed without upholding truth over falsehood.

I paralyzed the hand of a brutal killer —
there is no martyr among your swords.

Marna al-Amel, tender in spirit —
if a woman finds herself in straits

and the dark light of the moon fades,
the earth was plunged into darkness by his loss.

Exalted, honored, inward nobility —
may Allah bestow upon him His prayers in Paradise.

In every matter our youth go forth —
we used to look upon Hamza as our guardian.

He was neither weak nor among the losers,
and in Islam this stands as a landmark.

Tears and grief are a lesson to the bereaved.

Do not rejoice, O Hind, and be not pleased —
for the sword waits beneath the barrier.

And weep for him at his threshold when he hastened forth
with all his heart, heedless of ignorance.

If he should fall into eldership among you,
they walk beneath the virtuous throat.

Hamza wanted them within his family —
yes, he was the minister of the noble knight.

The following day, Jibril was his minister.

Leave behind a house whose drawing has been forgiven —
the Sherry Filler when the storm strikes.

[122/f]

<!-- page 271 -->
[Mention of the Day of Return, in the Third Year]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family were approached by a delegation from the continent — after the occurrence of Uhud — from the Adal and al-Qarah, who are of the Hunn ibn Khuzaymah ibn.

They said: "O Messenger of Allah, send with us some of your companions who will instruct us in religion, teach us the Quran, and acquaint us with the rulings of Islam."

So the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent six of his companions with them. They were: Murtad ibn Abi Murtad al-Ghanawi, an ally of Hamza ibn Abd al-Muttalib; Khalid ibn al-Bakir al-Laythi, an ally of Banu 'Adi ibn Ka'b; 'Asim ibn Thabit ibn Abi al-Aqlah, brother of Banu 'Amr ibn Awf [ibn Malik] ibn al-Aws; Zayd ibn al-Dinnah [ibn Mu'awiya], brother of Banu Bayadah ibn Jashim ibn al-Khazraj; Khubaib ibn 'Adi, brother of Banu Jahjaba ibn Kalfa; and Abdullah ibn Tariq, ally of Banu Zafar ibn al-Khazraj ibn 'Amr ibn Malik ibn al-Aws.

[The Treachery at al-Raji' against the Six]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) appointed Murtad ibn Abi Murtad al-Ghanawi over them as commander. They set out with the delegation, and when the party reached the Hijaz region, their hosts betrayed them. They raised the alarm against them, and the people of the surrounding area answered the call. The six companions, finding themselves on the march, were met by men with swords drawn. The armed men cornered them, and the companions drew their own swords to fight. Their assailants called out:

(1) Addition from the biography of the Prophet.
(2) Addition from the Prophet's biography.

<!-- page 272 -->
"We swear by Allah, we do not intend to kill you. We only wish to obtain something by delivering you to the people of Mecca, and you have Allah's covenant and pledge from us that we will not kill you."

As for Murtad ibn Abi Murtad, Khalid ibn al-Bakir, and 'Asim ibn Thabit, they replied: "By Allah, we will accept no covenant made with polytheists."

'Asim ibn Thabit then said:

I have no concern for you — I am the wielder of the strung bow,
and the bow holds its string within it,

less than the span of its stave.
Death is a certainty, and life is fleeting.

Every soul that Allah protects must one day descend —
one by one they fall like deer.

If I do not fight you, let my mother be shamed.

He also said:

Abu Sulayman — and the feathers of the quiver —
and straying like embers on the stove.

If the Nawaji were scattered, I would not be routed.

(1) Addition from the biography of the Prophet.
(2) Addition from the Prophet's biography.
(3) See: Biography of the Prophet, 3/170.
(4) See: Biography of the Prophet, 170/3.

<!-- page 273 -->
And a tender shield fashioned from the hide of a barren ox,
and he believed in what Muhammad brought.

Al-'Asim was known as Abu Sulayman. He fought the enemy until he was killed, and his companions were killed with him — may Allah have mercy on them all.

When 'Asim was slain, Hathil sought to sever his head in order to sell it to Sulafa bint Sa'd, [123/f] whose son had been killed, and she had vowed that if his head were brought to her, she would drink wine from his skull. But a swarm of bees protected his body and prevented them from reaching him.

'Asim had made a covenant with Allah that no polytheist, man or woman, would ever touch him.

It was said: "The bees protected him, and when night fell, we tried to take him, but Allah sent a flash flood that filled the valley and swept him away, carrying him off."

As for Zayd ibn al-Dinnah, Khubaib ibn 'Adi, and 'Abdullah ibn Tariq, they were reluctant and desired to live. They surrendered and were taken captive, and their captors set out with them toward Mecca to sell them there.

When they reached Dhahran, 'Abdullah ibn Tariq wrenched his hand free from his bonds and seized his sword. The people drew back from him and pelted him with stones until he was killed, and he was buried — may Allah have mercy on him — in Dhahran.

They arrived in Mecca with Khubaib ibn 'Adi and Zayd ibn al-Dinnah and sold them to the Quraysh.

(1) Addition from the Prophet's biography, 171/3.

<!-- page 274 -->
The Quraysh then killed them after crucifying them — may Allah have mercy on them. They roused their tribes and assembled every gathering against them.

Khubaib said, when they were about to kill him:

<!-- page 275 -->
I am in a trust that has been lost —
and I drew near to a tall, ancient trunk.

They gathered their sons and their wives,
and to Allah I complain of my grief, then more grief.

I do not watch the factions that surround me when I die —
they have cut my flesh and I have despaired of my longing.

O Throne, grant me patience for what You have willed.
Bless the dismemberments of a torn shell.

This is for the sake of Allah — and if He wills,
I will not close my eyes in fear.

They offered me disbelief, and I chose death over it —
but beware, Hell is a blazing fire.

I have no dread of death, for I am dead
on whatever side my death comes in Allah's path.

I hope that if I die a Muslim,
I need not fear, for to Allah is my return.

I am not a principle of enmity to be feared —
they gathered the factions around me and assembled,

and all of them strive from the principle of enmity.

[Pg 123]

Hassan ibn Thabit said, on the Day of Return — with honor and grief:

May Allah's prayers be upon those who followed.

Ibn al-Bakir led them, and Khubaib —
the head of the company was the renegade, yet their true prince.

He died, and then his pledge was written in light.

And the son of Tariq and the son of al-Dinnah among them,
gaining the exalted reward — what a gain.

And the slain ones rest at their resting place.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 176/3–177.
(2) See: Diwana 1/179.

<!-- page 275 -->
They held back the archers from reaching his back until he fought on and wailed in defense.

Hassan ibn Thabit also said:

They said on the Day of Return that they were free —
we have those who were killed treacherously yet remained loyal. [124/f]

May Allah bless our lives — it was not their blood
that was a trust broken at this and at Safa.

With this steadfastness, they were not found wanting.

If they were killed on the day of rest, all their families —
before the people of unbelief, manifest and harsh.

He killed his mother-in-law around their homes,
and sold a beloved companion after him with loyalty.

I lost — I said to Hayyan: more honorable than them
is their remembrance, in every remembrance, a lasting exemption.

So to live in every circumstance
beneath a dome of treachery and misfortune that boasts,

yet its evil never touched them in secret.

Yes — if the killing of those slain were my healing,
and if they were killed, their blood would not be withheld from those who shed it —

like the clouds of al-Jaham pressing forward.

If I do not die, I will be struck by a swift raid.
Life sleeps for the traitor, an anniversary of shame.

By the command of the Messenger of Allah — and command belongs to him —
winter ice does not warm.

So it is right to uphold what matters most.

[The Incident of Bi'r Ma'unah, in the Month of Safar, Year Four]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family spent the remainder of Shawwal, Dhu al-Qa'dah, and Dhu al-Hijjah in Medina — and the custodianship of that Hajj season was in the hands of the polytheists, who held the sacred months inviolable. Then the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) dispatched the companions of Bi'r Ma'unah in the month of Safar, four months after Uhud.

(1) See: Diwana 406/1.

<!-- page 276 -->
This came about because Abu Bara' [124/D] — 'Amir ibn Malik ibn Ja'far, known as the Player of Spears — had come to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) in Medina, and the Prophet presented Islam to him. He neither embraced Islam nor turned away from it outright. He said: "O Muhammad, if you were to send men from your companions to the people of Najd to call them to Allah, I hope they would respond to you." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) replied: "I fear for them the people of Najd." Abu Bara' said: "I am their neighbor — send them, and let them call the people to your message." [So send them.]

So the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent al-Mundhir ibn 'Amr, brother of Banu Sa'idah, in command of forty men. According to al-Bukhari, the number was seventy — from among [the finest] of the Muslims, his companions. They marched until they reached the well of Ma'unah, which lies between the land of Banu 'Amir and the lava fields of Banu Sulaym, closer to Hurrat Banu Sulaym.

When they encamped there, they dispatched one of their number — a man named Haram ibn Milhan — carrying a letter from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) to the enemy of Allah, 'Amir ibn Tufayl ibn Malik ibn Ja'far [165] ibn Kilab. When Haram arrived, 'Amir did not so much as glance at the letter before ordering a man to kill him. He was slain. 'Amir then called upon the Banu 'Amir to rise against the Muslim party, [125/f] but they refused to betray the protection Abu Bara' had extended.

(1) Addition from the biography of the Prophet.
(2) Addition from the Prophet's biography.
(3) Addition from the Prophet's biography.
(4) Addition from the Prophet's biography.

<!-- page 277 -->
He called upon them again, but they said: "We will not break the protection of Abu Bara'." So 'Amir roused the tribes of Banu Sulaym — 'Usayya, Ra'l, and Dhakwan — and they answered his call. They set out and closed in on the Muslim encampment, surrounding the companions while they were still at their halting place. When the companions saw them approaching, they took up their weapons and fought. They were martyred to the last man — may Allah have mercy on them — save for Ka'b ibn Zayd, brother of Banu Dinar ibn al-Najjar, whom the attackers left for dead among the fallen. He survived, remained among the slain until the enemy departed, and lived on until he was killed as a martyr on the Day of al-Khandaq.

'Amr ibn Umayya al-Damri and a man from the Ansar had been away from the camp, tending to their mounts. They were alerted to the fate of their companions only by the sight of birds circling overhead. They came to investigate and found their companions lying dead, with the enemy horsemen standing over them. The Ansari rushed forward and fought until he was killed, after declaring: "I will not report back about what has befallen my companions while still alive." 'Amr ibn Umayya was taken prisoner.
