When 'Amir ibn al-Tufayl learned that he was from the Mudar, he cut off his ear and released him, claiming that this fulfilled a vow [125/z] he had made to his mother.

'Amr ibn Umayya set out on his way back to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). Along the road, he came upon two men from Banu 'Amir and asked them about their tribe. They told him. They held a treaty of protection with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), though 'Amr did not know of it. He waited until they fell asleep, then attacked and killed them both.

When 'Amr arrived before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and told him what had happened,

(1) Addition from the biography of the Prophet.


<!-- page 278 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to him: "I have killed two men whose blood I must answer for." Then he added: "This is the work of Abu Bara'a — that is why I despised it."

Abu Bara' was deeply troubled by what 'Amir had done and by what had befallen the companions of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family.

Hassan ibn Thabit inciting Abu Bara' against 'Amir ibn al-Tufail:

He was one of the wolves of the people of Nahd —
Did the Banu Umm al-Banin not want you?
Let him fall silent — and what a deliberate wrong this is.

'Amir mocks Abu Bara':

What happened in the two events after me —
Shall I not inform Rabi'a of these endeavors?

[Pg 166]

Your uncle, the noble Hakam ibn Sa'd,
Your father Abu al-Hurub — Abu Bara'.

Hassan ibn Thabit said, weeping for the dead of Bi'r Ma'unah, may Allah have mercy on them:

[Pg 126/f]

At the beginning of help I began / with tears from the eye, and there was no holding back the tear.
Their wishes and their meeting are as they were —
On the horses of the Prophet, they were found the next day.
You betrayed their covenant treacherously —
Destruction struck them, bound by a people.
And I embrace in my grief patience —
What longing for Mundhir, if he were to tell me
From the light of the secret of 'Amr —
And one who was wounded on the day that followed.

(1) An increase from the biography of the Prophet.
(2) See: Diwan 232/1.
(3) See: Diwan 1/207.

<!-- page 279 -->
Then the evacuation of Banu al-Nadir took place in the fourth year of the Hijrah. This came about because the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) went to them seeking their assistance with the blood money owed for the two men slain from Banu 'Amir whom 'Amr ibn Umayyah al-Damri had killed — men who had a treaty with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). There existed between the Banu al-Nadir and Banu 'Amir a pact and an oath of allegiance. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) came to them with his family, they said: "Yes, O Abu al-Qasim, we will help you with what you have asked."

Then they turned to one another in secret and said: "You will never find a man like him in such a position again." The Messenger of Allah stood beside a wall of one of their houses. A man was stationed on top of the house, and they conspired: "Let him hurl a rock down upon him, and rid us of him." With the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family were the Commander of the Believers (peace be upon him) and a small group of his companions.

A man from among the Jews was assigned this task — it is said his name was 'Amr ibn Jahash ibn Ka'b — and he said: "I will kill him." He climbed up [126/z] to throw a rock down upon the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family from the top of a wall. But revelation came to the Prophet from heaven, informing him of what the people had planned. He rose and left, heading back toward the city, and his companions followed him. He then informed them of what the Jews had plotted in their treachery.

(1) An increase from the biography of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allaah be upon him) 190/3.
(2) An increase from the Prophet's biography 190/3.
(3) An increase from the Prophet's biography 190/3.

<!-- page 280 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) commanded his companions to prepare for war against them and to march out. They set out in the month of Rabi' al-Awwal, and he besieged Banu al-Nadir for six nights. They fortified themselves in their strongholds, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered the cutting down of their palm trees and the burning of them.

'Abdullah ibn Ubayy ibn Salul and a group of the hypocrites had sent word to them urging them to hold firm and resist. They had promised them: "We will not abandon you — if you are attacked, we will fight alongside you, and if you are expelled, we will go out with you."

[Pg 167]

Concerning the hypocrites, verses were then revealed from Surah al-Hashr. Allah cast terror into their hearts, and the hypocrites shrank from what they had promised. The Banu al-Nadir therefore asked the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) to grant them safe passage and to allow them to evacuate, and the Prophet granted them security on the condition that they could take whatever their camels could carry, except for weapons. They loaded up what they could of their wealth and departed for Khaybar, while some of them went to [127/f] al-Sham. They left with their women and children, going forth with tambourines and music, with trumpets sounding behind them — a spectacle the like of which had rarely been seen from any community of their time.

Their remaining property was left behind for the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, and it became his personal disposition to place as he saw fit. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then divided it

(1) An addition from the Biography of the Prophet, 191/3.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography 192/3.

<!-- page 281 -->
among the first Muhajireen, to the exclusion of the Ansar. However, Sahl ibn Hunayf and Abu Dajanah [Sammak ibn Kharshah] complained to him of poverty, and so he allotted shares to them as well. Only two men from among the Banu al-Nadir embraced Islam, and the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allaah be upon him) therefore spared them and left them to their affairs.

[What was said in poetry concerning Banu al-Nadir]

The Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), composed verses recalling the evacuation of Banu al-Nadir and the killing of Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf — the account of whose killing has been mentioned above. 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) distinguished himself in the campaign against Banu al-Nadir with a celebrated stand, a virtue recorded by many, and he said (peace be upon him):

I knew — and whoever is discerning knows —
The tight words from which there is no escape.

[Pg 168]

And truly I understood and did not encounter
Any other refuge but Allah, the Most Merciful, the Most Compassionate.

Letters taught among the believers —
Ahmad al-Mustafa chose them.

A noble station and a lofty standing —
Ahmad became dear to us.

He came not with injustice, nor did he pardon the guilty —
O you who broke your promise, you are the fool among them.

[Pg 127]

By Allah, one who has no fear of Him is faithless —
Do you not fear even the slightest torment?

To wrestle beneath his swords
As Abu Ashraf died, felled at the heel.

Wider than the most reckless of camels —
The next day God made manifest his tyranny.

(1) An addition from the Biography of the Prophet, 192/3.
(2) See: Diwan 101.

<!-- page 282 -->
Inspired against 'Abd al-Muttalib's line,
By a path so subtle and fine —
When does a heel shed blood for her?

His eyes were gouged out by pickaxes;
We have not recovered from that lamentation.

They said to Ahmad: "We spoke but little" —
A handful of grain despite opposition.

So he left them and then pronounced a curse upon them,
And they were dwelling in the finest of abodes.

The light was deferred to a stranger —
However that may be, I am steadfast.

To the arms of the threshing floor, though they are an illusion —
So Jibril descended to command his killing.

The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allaah be upon him) —

Ka'b ibn Malik said, recalling the evacuation of Banu al-Nadir:

Indeed, time has a turning that keeps revolving —
She was made sorrowful by her own treachery.

The mighty one is brought to a good end —
And there came to them from Allah, the All-Wise,

Illuminating verses built upon truth.
A truthful warner delivered the Book —

And you deny us, O overbearing one.
They said: "He has not come with a matter of truth."

Good understanding believes me —
He said: "Yes, I truly have,"

And whoever disbelieves in him is deceived by stubborn unbelief.
Whoever follows him is guided to every right course,

While they turn away from the truth seeking escape.
When they drank of treachery and disbelieved,

Allah ruled, and His ruling does not change —
And his supporter — what a supporter!

So His support and authority came over them,
For they had disbelieved in their Lord.

Together, they forfeited both understanding and knowledge.

[Pg 128]

I see Allah directing the Prophet by a true and clear vision —

(1) See: Id. at 168–170.

<!-- page 283 -->
[Pg 169]

So he was deceived by them and humiliated after his arrogance —
On the palm trees, and then he was brought low,

By our hands — a man of renown —
Felled at the heel, the heel of his own downfall,

By the command of Muhammad, when he moves at night,
And Mahmud, the brother of the trust of bridges.

He is cunning — so I shall undo him with cunning,
And I absolve myself of what they did, the instigators.

Those are the ones who spread harm in a wretched abode —
The Messenger of Allah, and He will deal with them.

The next day he came to them in triumph
Over the enemies, and He is their true Sovereign.

Ghassan al-Hama' — struck down —
And their command was nothing but falsehood and lies.

So he said peace, and he ruled; they were repelled —
Each of the three bearing his burden like a laden camel.

[128/z]

So they tasted the folly of their own affairs and their misfortune,
Deceived, and they turned away.

And they evacuated, the deliberate people of Qithqa'.

Then came the Battle of Dhu al-Riqa' in year four. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) remained in Medina following the campaign against Banu al-Nadir through the month of Rabi' al-Akhir and part of Jumada al-Awwal, then he set out into Najd, intent on the Banu Muharib and the Banu Tha'labah of Ghatafan.

The battle was called the "Battle of Dhu al-Riqa'" because in it they planted their banners. "Dhu al-Riqa'" is a tree in that locality, also known as "the place of the snake." A company of Ghatafan advanced toward it, and the two sides drew close to one another, but no fighting broke out between them. The two forces were wary of each other, until the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) led the people in the Prayer of Fear, after which the people dispersed.

<!-- page 284 -->
[The Prayer of Fear]

On the authority of Ja'far ibn Muhammad, [from his father, from his forebears]:

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) led the Prayer of Fear with his companions during the Battle of Dhu al-Riqa'. His companions divided into two groups: one group he stationed to face the enemy,

[Pg 170]

and the other group stood in prayer behind him. He stood and they stood, he recited and they listened, he bowed and they bowed, he prostrated and they prostrated. Then the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family remained standing while those who had prayed with him completed a rak'ah on their own, then gave the salutation to one another. They then went out to relieve their companions and stood against the enemy, while their companions came and took their place behind the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family. These too stood and stood, recited and listened, bowed and bowed, prostrated and prostrated. He sat and they sat, then he gave the salutation, and they rose and completed a rak'ah on their own, then gave the salutation to one another and departed."

[The Account of Ghurth ibn al-Harith]

It is reported that a man from Banu Muharib — said to be Ghurth — said to his people, namely Ghatafan and Muharib: "Shall I not kill Muhammad for you?" They said: "Yes — but how will you do it?" He said: "I will kill him."

He came close to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) while the Prophet was seated, with the sword of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) in his lap. The man said: "O Muhammad, look —

(1) See: Pillars of Islam 202/1.
(2) An increase from the Prophet's biography 205/3.

<!-- page 285 -->
Is this your sword?" He said: "Yes." The man took hold of it, drew it from its sheath, and then began to threaten the Prophet with it. He shook it menacingly, but Allah restrained him. He then said: "O Muhammad, are you not afraid of me, with your sword in my hand?" The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) replied: "No — Allah protects me from you." The man sheathed the sword. Concerning this, Allah revealed: "O you who believe, remember the blessing of Allah upon you when a people resolved to stretch out their hands against you, but He restrained their hands from you. And fear Allah — and in Allah let the believers place their trust" [al-Ma'idah: 11].

The Messenger of Allah then returned to Medina, where he remained for the rest of Jumada al-Awwal and Jumada al-Akhirah.

Then came the Battle of Badr al-Akhirah in Sha'ban of the fourth year. Abu Sufyan and the disbelievers of Quraysh had appointed Badr as the place for a rematch. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family set out in Sha'ban in answer to Abu Sufyan's challenge and reached the market of Badr, where he remained for eight nights waiting for him. Abu Sufyan marched out with the people of Makkah until he halted at Dhahran — some say at 'Asfan. He looked at his companions and saw in them a lack of resolve, and so he turned back. He said: "O people of Quraysh, nothing will serve you but a year of plenty, in which you can graze your animals on the trees and drink their milk. This year of yours is a lean one, and I am turning back — so turn back." The people of Makkah turned back, and they were thereafter called "the army of Suwaiq." It was said of them: "You went out only to drink your Suwaiq."

(1) An increase from the Prophet's biography 205/3.
(2) An increase from the Prophet's biography 205/3.
(3) See: Dictionary of Countries, 673/1.
(4) An increase from "B".

<!-- page 286 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) waited for Abu Sufyan until it became clear he had turned back, and then the Messenger of Allah returned to Medina.

'Abdullah ibn [130/f] Rawahah said of this:

[Pg 171]

He gave his word honestly, yet did not keep it —
Abu Sufyan promised us Badr, and we did not fall short.

He clung to the daughter of Dhimima and abandoned the loyal —
I swear, if you meet us, you will find us ready.

We left him the limbs of 'Utbah and his son,
And 'Umar — Abu Jahl we left him at Thawiyya.

You disobeyed the Messenger of God, following your own religion,
And your wretched counsel led you astray.

A ransom for the Messenger of God — my family and my wealth —
Yes, and if you admit it to me, I will say it plainly.

A blazing star in the darkness of night —
We obeyed him; nothing in us was altered but through him.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had previously concluded a treaty with Banu al-Nadir from among the Jews. Islam had spread and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had established himself in Medina after his departure from Badr for the appointed meeting with Abu Sufyan — a full year had passed and another had begun.

[The Battle of Dumat al-Jandal]

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) returned to Medina, he remained there for some months until the month of Dhu al-Hijjah had passed. The polytheists were the guardians of the Sacred House that year. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then set out but turned back before reaching it, and he received no rebuke for doing so.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allaah be upon him) 210/3–211, and attributed to Ka'b ibn Malik; see: Diwan 222. Maghazi al-Waqidi 389/1–390.


<!-- page 287 -->
[Death of Fatima bint Asad]

In the fourth year of the Hijra, Fatima bint Asad, the mother of the Commander of the Faithful (peace be upon him) — she being Fatima bint Asad ibn Hashim ibn Abd Manaf — passed away.

Al-Waqidi said: She was the first woman to embrace Islam after Khadijah al-Kubra (peace be upon her).

Al-Waqidi said: She was the first woman to migrate from Mecca to Medina on foot.

Ibn Abbas (may Allah be pleased with him) said: The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) [172] used to visit her and rest in her home, and she was devoted to him.

As Ali ibn Ahmad ibn al-Husayn al-Bayhaqi narrated — with his isnad — from his father, from Abdullah, who said:

"I heard Fatima bint Asad say that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) once remarked: 'On the Day of Resurrection, people will be gathered naked.' She said: 'How shameful that would be.' The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: 'I ask Allah, exalted be He, to clothe you.' He said: I heard him mention the torment of the grave, and she said: 'How feeble I am before that.' The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: 'I ask Allah, glorified and exalted be He, to spare you that.'"

(1) An addition by the editor.
(2) See: al-Kafi 453/1, Characteristics of the Imams 64–65, Warning the Heedless 190–191.

<!-- page 288 -->
Mujahid (1) said: She was the first Hashimite woman to be born to a Hashimite father and mother.

'Ikrimah said: She used to circumambulate the Ka'ba while pregnant with the Commander of the Faithful (peace be upon him). Her labor overtook her, the door of the Ka'ba was opened for her, and she gave birth inside it. We have mentioned this.

Al-Waqidi said: The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) attended her funeral. He ordered that she be shrouded in his own shirt, and he (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) descended into her grave, lying down in it. He said: "I wanted to ease this for her, and so that it might benefit her as it benefited Abu Talib."

It was also narrated — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — that when he saw her in her grave [131/f], he said: "Your son, your son — 'Ali, 'Ali." He was asked: "Pray to Allah." He said: "The two angels asked her, 'Who is your Lord?' She said, 'Allah.' They said, 'Who is your prophet?' She said, 'Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.' They said to her, 'Who is your Imam?' She hesitated, and I said to her, 'Your son, your son.' She said: ''Aqeel.' It was said to her: ''Ali, 'Ali.'"

[The Battle of the Trench fell in the month of Shawwal in the fifth year of the Hijra]

This is because some of the Jews had grown embittered — among them Salam ibn Abi al-Haqiq al-Nadhari, and

(1) See: The Statement in the History of the Zaydi Imams, 35.
(2) See: Da'im al-Islam 361/2, Sharh al-Akhbar 201/3.
(3) See: Kashf al-Yaqeen 194.

<!-- page 289 -->
Ibn Akhtab al-Nadari, Kanana ibn al-Rabi' [ibn Abi al-Haqiq] (1) al-Nahri, and Hudha ibn Qays al-Wa'ili, and Abu 'Ammar al-Wa'ili, along with a company of Banu al-Nadir and Banu Wa'il.

They set out until they reached the Quraysh in Mecca and called them to war against the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), telling them: "We will stand with you in this until we uproot him."

The Quraysh said to them: "O community of Jews, you are the people of the First Book, and you know well what we and Muhammad are at odds over — tell us: are we on the side of right, or is he?" They said: "Your religion is better than his, and you are more deserving than he."

[Jewish incitement of Ghatafan]

When they had said this to the Quraysh and secretly secured their agreement to make war on the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) — telling them, "Gather yourselves for this and make ready" — these Jewish leaders then went out and came to Ghatafan of Qays 'Aylan, and invited them too to fight the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family). They told them that they would stand with them, and that the Quraysh had already agreed to join. Accordingly, Ghatafan fell in with them as well.

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 214/3.

<!-- page 290 -->
[The departure of the confederate forces]

The Quraysh marched out under their commander Abu Sufyan ibn Harb, and Ghatafan marched out with their leaders: 'Uyayna ibn Husn ibn Hudhayfah ibn Badr, leading Banu Fazara; al-Harith ibn 'Awf ibn Abi Haritha al-Marri, leading Banu Murrah; and Muqarr ibn Rukhilah, leading those who followed him from among the people of Ashja' ibn Rayth ibn Ghatafan.

[The digging of the trench]

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) received word of their advance and the command came to him, he ordered a trench to be dug around the city. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) worked on it himself — alongside his God-given duty — in order to encourage the Muslims to exert themselves. The Muslims worked alongside him. 'Ali (peace be upon him) and his followers were among the most energetic and hardworking, while the hypocrites were slack and negligent in their efforts.

[174]

[A miracle manifested: the miracle of the rock]

The workers were severely hampered in the trench and complained of it to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family). He called for a vessel containing water, spat into it, supplicated to whatever Allah willed, and then sprinkled that water upon the rock.

Jabir ibn 'Abd Allah al-Ansari said, in what has been narrated from him: the Prophet struck it, and it crumbled away like a dune — neither axe nor pick would turn back from it thereafter.

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 217/3.

<!-- page 291 -->
[The blessing in the dates — the story of the sister of al-Nu'man ibn Bashir]

It was narrated from the sister of al-Nu'man ibn Bashir that she said:

"My mother called me, gave me a handful of dates, and said: 'My daughter, take this to your father and your uncle 'Abd Allah ibn Rawaha as their lunch.' As she passed by the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), he said to her: '[Come here, O daughter,](2) what do you have with you?' So she poured the dates into his palms, and they did not even fill them.

He called for a garment to be spread out, then called for the dates to be scattered upon it, and then he commanded his caller to announce to all who were in the trench that they should come and eat. They came and ate until they had had their fill, and yet the dates only increased — they were spilling over the edges of the garment."

[The blessing in Jabir ibn 'Abd Allah's food]

Jabir ibn 'Abd Allah said:

[175]

"I ordered my wife to slaughter a sheep and prepare some food, and then I went to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) to invite him to come with me alone. He came, however, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) arrived with the food and the sheep [132/z]. He blessed it — and the blessing of Allah, glorified and exalted be He, came upon it — then he ate, and the people came to it. No sooner had one group finished and departed than another arrived, until all the people of the trench had eaten their fill."

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) 218/3.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography 218/3.
(3) See: Biography of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) 218/3–219.

<!-- page 292 -->
[What Allah, the Almighty, showed His Messenger (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) of future conquests]

Salman al-Farisi (may Allah be pleased with him) said:

"I struck the pickaxe against a section of the trench and a rock resisted me. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) took the pickaxe from my hand and struck it three times. I saw three flashes of light within it, and I said: 'What is this, O Messenger of Allah?' He said — and I confirmed, 'Yes, by my father and my mother' — He said: 'As for the first flash, Allah has granted me the opening of Yemen; as for the second, Allah has granted me the opening of the Levant and the Maghreb; and as for the third, Allah has granted me the opening of the East.'"

[The Quraysh descend upon Medina]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had not yet finished the trench when the Quraysh descended and camped at the settlement of al-Asiyyals near Ruma, between the escarpment and al-Ghaba, with ten thousand of their forces together with their allies from Banu Kinana and the people of Tihama. Ghatafan and the people of Najd who were with them likewise advanced, until they descended [with overwhelming numbers] (3) near Uhud.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) marched out with three thousand Muslims [133/f], positioned them with their backs to the heights, placed his army there, and set the trench between himself and the enemy. He commanded — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — that they encamp and dig in.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 219/3.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography 219/3.
(3) An addition from the Prophet's biography 220/3.

<!-- page 293 -->
They settled into their positions.

[Huyayy ibn Akhtab persuades Banu Qurayza to break their covenant with the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family)]

Huyayy ibn Akhtab, the hostile Jew, went out until he came to Ka'b ibn Asad al-Qurazi, the holder of the compact and covenant of Banu Qurayza — a man who had kept faith with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) [toward his people and toward the covenant he had made with him] (1).

Huyayy said to him: "I have come to you with glory undying." He would not relent until Ka'b answered him, [176] and Banu Qurayza broke the treaty they had with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and with the protected community.

[The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) is informed of the breach]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then summoned Sa'd ibn Mu'adh, master of al-Aws; Sa'd ibn 'Ubada of al-Khazraj; 'Abd Allah ibn Rawaha; and Khawwat ibn Jubayr, of the brothers of Banu 'Amr ibn 'Awf. He said to them: "Go and see — is what has been reported to us about these people true or not?"

They went out and went to them, and found them in the worst of states. Banu Qurayza said: "Who is the Messenger of Allah? There is no covenant between us and Muhammad [and no compact.] (2)" They responded with abuse toward the Messenger of Allah, and Sa'd ibn Mu'adh cursed them. (3)

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 220/3.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography 222/3.
(3) An addition from the Prophet's biography 222/3.

<!-- page 294 -->
The companions of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) returned and reported the news to him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Allahu Akbar — give glad tidings, O community of Muslims."

[The fear that gripped the Muslims and the exposure of the hypocrites]

The calamity grew greater, fear intensified, and the enemy pressed upon them from above and below, and the stars of the sky appeared. Some of the hypocrites said: "Muhammad used to promise us that we would eat the treasures of Chosroes and Caesar, yet now not one of us can be sure he will even make it to the latrine."

Some of them went to the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "Our houses are exposed [to the enemy]." (1)

The believers were shaken by a violent trembling, as Allah, exalted be He, describes in Surat al-Ahzab (2). The polytheists laid siege for more than twenty nights, and the fighting between the two sides was only skirmishing and archery across the trench. The polytheists looked at the trench and said: "This is a trick the Arabs have never known before."

[A group of the polytheists crosses the trench]

A bold warrior was dispatched from among the polytheists — 'Amr ibn 'Abd Wudd ibn Abi Qays, of the Banu 'Amir ibn Lu'ayy. He was one of the most formidable fighters and one of the greatest of them, a man renowned for his prowess.

(1) An addition from the biography of the Prophet.
(2) A reference to verse 11: "There the believers were afflicted and a severe earthquake struck."

<!-- page 295 -->
He had fought at Badr on the side of the polytheists, and had received wounds so severe that he was counted among the dead, yet he survived. From that time he had not fought in any battle, and now he wished to make a show of himself before the Quraysh and demonstrate his valor — so he put on a distinguishing mark so that he might be recognized. With him went 'Ikrima ibn Abi Jahl, Hubayra ibn Abi Wahab al-Makhzumi, and Dirar ibn al-Khattab ibn Mardas, of the brothers of Banu Muharib ibn Fihr.

They armed themselves, prepared for battle, and set out on horseback until they drew up before Banu Kinana and called out: "Prepare to fight, O Banu Kinana — today you will see true horsemen!"

Then they urged their horses forward until they reached a narrow point of the trench. Their horses leapt across it, and the riders spread out in the salt flat between the goods-storage and the trench.

'Amr ibn 'Abd Wudd was the mightiest of them, and he rode ahead of them as their leader, calling out for single combat. He said:

Calling them to gather — is there any swordsman?
I have escaped from the frost of age
At the station of great deeds across the ages,
And I stood when they called me
Rushing toward the shaker.
Yes, I am still undefeated,
And valor is among the finest of traits —
Courage in a man.

[Ali (peace be upon him) kills 'Amr ibn 'Abd Wudd, and his poetry on that occasion]

'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) rose [134/z] and sought permission from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) to go out to him. Permission was granted, and he went out to face him, saying:

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 323/1, Zahr al-Adab 29/1, from his name among the poets 105. Al-Irshad by Shaykh al-Mufid 1/100, Letters of Sharif al-Murtada 122/4, Explanation of Nahj al-Balagha 291/13–292.


<!-- page 296 -->
When the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family) sent Ali toward him, he said: "He is Amr ibn 'Abd al-Wad." Ali (peace be upon him) replied: I gladly accept. By Allah, O Messenger of Allah. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) granted him permission and handed him his sword. He then raised his hands toward the sky and said: "O Allah, protect him from what is before him and behind him, from his right and his left, from above him and from below him." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) then said: "All of faith has gone forth to meet all of disbelief."

Ali (peace be upon him) advanced, reciting:

*When you called the lover, your voice was never helpless,*
*And sincerity clears a path past every rival.*
*You must mourn, you criminals,*
*For the sting of the bees will reach you.*
*I hope you will prove strong.*

'Amr said to him, "Who are you?" He replied: I am Ali ibn Abi Talib. 'Amr said: A worthy man of noble rank — yet I have no wish to kill you. Ali (peace be upon him) said to him: But I have been told about you. O 'Amr, I put to you two choices and you must answer one of them. He said: Then put them to me. He said: I invite you to Allah, to His Messenger, and to Islam. He said: I want none of that. He said:

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 323/1, Sharh Nahj al-Balagha 292/13, 64/19, Tafsir al-Qummi 183/2, Al-Manaqib by al-Khwarizmi 169, 'Uyun al-Athar 2/93, Sabal al-Huda wa al-Rashad 378/4.

<!-- page 297 -->
Then I invite you to single combat. 'Amr said: Your father Abu Talib was a dear friend to me, and I hate to kill you. Ali (peace be upon him) said: As for me, I would gladly kill you, since you have refused Islam. 'Amr then dismounted from his horse and charged at him, striking at his face. He came at Ali (peace be upon him), who gave ground and sidestepped. A fierce struggle broke out between them, and no one could see them through the dust, and the Muslims feared for Ali. 'Amr struck Ali on the head (peace be upon him), splitting his helmet. He then struck at his flank, but Ali (peace be upon him) delivered a blow that severed his head from his body.

The dust settled to reveal Ali (peace be upon him) wiping his sword on his garment and saying:

*Against them I did not leave my friends without support,*
*While the knights broke apart before me like this.*
*I upheld the Lord of Muhammad with my righteousness —*
*A victory wrested from the folly of his arrogance.*
*Like the bond between your brother and my narrator,*
*Issued the moment I left him boasting.*
*I was the one whom he called to challenge —*
*And I shamed his deputies, though I did so alone.*
*And O Prophet, O gathering of the confederates —*
*Do not imagine that Allah would abandon His religion.*

'Amr's cavalry fled in rout until they plunged back across the trench. 'Ikrimah ibn Abi Jahl hurled his spear as he fled, and Hassan ibn Thabit said:

*Was it not you, Ikrimah, who fell short?*
*You barely escaped from the ditch.*
*He fled and left his spear behind us.*

And it was said:

*Would you not count yourself among the enemy of darkness?*

(1) See: Diwan, 32–33.
(2) See: Diwan, 509/1.

<!-- page 298 -->
*Or has your back grown tame — is your back like the back of a snake?*

Ali (peace be upon him) killed, together with 'Amr ibn 'Abd Wad, his son Hasal ibn 'Amr and Nuqal ibn 'Abd Allah al-Makhrumi, who had crossed the trench alongside 'Amr and become entangled in the fighting with him. The polytheists asked the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) to let them ransom 'Amr's body. He said: "We have no need of his body or its price." And he left them to it.

Because of what Ali (peace be upon him) achieved that day, Allah opened the way for the Muslims. When He opened it, their hearts were strengthened, and they knew that the polytheists had lost all hope of forcing the trench. Despair and dread descended upon the polytheists.

[Recitation of 'Abdullah ibn Mas'ud: "And Allah has sufficed the believers in fighting."]

In the recitation of 'Abdullah ibn Mas'ud (1): "And Allah has sufficed the believers in fighting" — with Ali (peace be upon him) — "And Allah is Strong and Mighty." [Al-Ahzab: 25]

This was narrated by Fadl ibn al-Qasim [from Sufyan al-Thawri] (2) from Zubayd, from Murrah, from 'Abdullah. It was mentioned by Ibn Makula in his work "Al-Ikmal" (3) in the chapter "Al-Fadl."

(1) See: Interpretation of what was revealed from the Qur'an concerning the Prophet and his family, by Ibn al-Jahham, 260–261; Evidence of ta'zil 7/2; History of the City of Damascus 361/42.
(2) An addition from the interpretation of what was revealed from the Qur'an concerning the Prophet and his family, by Ibn al-Jahham.
(3) See: Al-Ikmal fi Raf' al-Irtiyab 67/7.

<!-- page 299 -->
[Martyrdom of Sa'd ibn Mu'adh]

Sa'd ibn Mu'adh al-Ansari (may Allah have mercy on him) was struck by an arrow that severed the vein of his arm. The arrow was shot by a man from Banu 'Amir ibn Lu'ay, and Sa'd died after the Battle of the Trench and the affair of the Banu Qurayzah from that wound.

[Safiyyah and Hassan ibn Thabit, and an account of his cowardice]

Safiyyah bint 'Abd al-Muttalib was in the fortress of Fara', where she had taken refuge with the women and children, and Hassan ibn Thabit was with them. A Jewish man came to the fortress, circled it, and began to probe its defenses, for the Banu Qurayza had cut off communication between the fortress and the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) and the Muslims, who were occupied facing their enemy. Safiyyah (may Allah have mercy on her) said: I said to Hassan: This Jew — as you can see — is prowling around the fortress, and I fear that he will expose our position from behind us to the Jews, while the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) and the Muslims are unable to reach us. Go down and deal with him. He said: O daughter of 'Abd al-Muttalib, you know I am not capable of this.

She said: When he told me that, and I saw nothing of use in him, I took a tent pole, climbed down from the fortress, and struck him with it until I had killed him. When I finished, I returned and said, "O Hassan, go down and strip him of his weapons, for it is not seemly for me to strip a man." He said: I fear there may still be fight left in him.

When 'Amr ibn 'Abd Wad was killed, the feet of the polytheists faltered and their resolve collapsed.

<!-- page 300 -->
[The Account of Na'im ibn Mas'ud]

Na'im ibn Mas'ud ibn 'Amir ibn Anif, a man from Ghatafan who had been among the polytheists, came to the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) and said: O Messenger of Allah, I have accepted Islam and my people do not yet know of it. I have come to you, so employ me however you see fit. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said: "You are but one man among us — a single voice — but go to your people and sow discord among them if you can, for war is deception."

He went out on this mission. He was a friend of the Banu Qurayza, so he went to them as a visitor. They welcomed him and drew him close. After he had greeted them, he said: "You know my affection for you, and I have come to give you counsel, if you will accept it from me." They said: May Allah reward you well — we do not suspect you, and we trust your affection; give us your advice.

He said: "You have taken a course of action without thinking carefully about yourselves. You have given your backing to Muhammad's enemies — the Quraysh and Ghatafan — yet your situation is not like theirs. The Quraysh and Ghatafan came only to fight Muhammad and his companions on their own soil. If they gain what they want from him, well and good; but if the tide turns, they will ride away and leave you behind with him — and you know you cannot stand against Muhammad and his companions alone if he turns on you. Your confederates have grown worn down by failure and disagreement; their momentum has slackened and their resolve diminished. You have seen what became of 'Amr ibn 'Abd Wad and his companions — you heard what happened, and you understood the full weight of it. He was the one they depended on, and you saw what came of him when he stormed the trench. Now, with despair gripping people's hearts, a few more days and any shock will send them home. When they go, they will return to their lands and leave you to face Muhammad alone."

<!-- page 301 -->
They said: You have spoken truthfully and counseled us well. But what stratagem is there after all this? He said: "The stratagem is not to fight alongside these people until you take hostages from them — nobles of their men held in your hands — so that they cannot simply abandon you while you hold their kinsmen." They said: You have pointed us to sound counsel and have done well. May Allah reward you.

Then 'Uyaynah ibn Husn and Abu Sufyan came separately to the Banu Qurayza and said: Between me and the Banu Qurayza there is something you have heard of, and I have met with them and learned their position. They said: And what is it? He said: "The people regret having broken their covenant with Muhammad now that they see where we stand. They have looked at the outcome of 'Amr ibn 'Abd Wad and his companions and fear that we may withdraw and leave them behind — and then Muhammad will reach out to them, and they will send to him wishing to reconcile, expressing their regret for what they did. They are proposing to demand hostages from both tribes — nobles from among our men — which they would then hand over to Muhammad to have their necks struck, or to deal with as he sees fit. After that they would join him against whatever remains of us. Do not be taken in by the Jews, lest they hand one of you over to the enemy."

So Abu Sufyan sent to the Banu Qurayza 'Uyaynah ibn Husn and 'Ikrimah ibn Abi Jahl, along with a contingent of Quraysh and Ghatafan, to inquire into this and summon them to battle. They said: We cannot remain here any longer — the horses are exhausted, the provisions have run out, and Abu Muhammad and his companions are blocking us with that trench of theirs. You know the terrain, so come out with your forces and let us finish off Muhammad and his companions, and storm the trench together.

When the leaders of the Banu Qurayza received this appeal, they replied: We were bound to Muhammad by a covenant, and we did not break what stood between us and him without reason. We fear that when the tide of war turns against us, you will withdraw,

<!-- page 302 -->
and the war will have moved away from us — we will not fight alongside you unless you give us hostages from among your leading men to hold as a guarantee, so that Muhammad and his companions can be dealt with.

When word of this reached Abu Sufyan and 'Uyaynah, they recognized that it was exactly what Na'im ibn Mas'ud had engineered, and they refused to give anyone as a hostage. The Banu Qurayza said: This confirms precisely what Na'im ibn Mas'ud told us.

The confederates then held their positions, each faction distrustful of the other, their hearts estranged, and they had no course left but to withdraw to their homelands. Then Allah sent a fierce wind upon them that left them with no vessel upright, no cooking pot standing, and no shelter intact. Abu Sufyan rose when the night had grown long and said: O people of Quraysh — by Allah, this is no place to remain. The horses and camels are perishing, and we have left the Banu Qurayza behind us having gained nothing. Add to that the ferocity of this wind you see — so depart. And they departed.

He went to his own mount, which had been hobbled and was kneeling, and he sat upon it and spurred it, then struck it and set it off at a trot, and he did not release its headband until he was standing in the open. The Quraysh and Ghatafan departed each to their own lands, and not one of them reached Medina. Allah delivered the Muslims through the blessing of His Messenger (peace be upon him and his family) and the jihad of Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him).

[What Hudhayfah ibn al-Yaman said about the virtues of the Commander of the Faithful (peace be upon him)]

It was narrated from Abu Harun al-'Abdi from Rabi'ah al-Sa'di, who said: (2)

(1) An addition from "B".
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 299/1–300.

<!-- page 303 -->
"I came to Hudhayfah ibn al-Yaman and said: O Abu 'Abdullah, relate to us something about Ali (peace be upon him) and his virtues, for the people of Basra say to us that we exaggerate concerning Ali (peace be upon him) and his merits. Will you not tell me a hadith about Ali (peace be upon him)?

[He said:] (2) Hudhayfah ibn al-Yaman said to me: 'O Rabi'ah, what do you ask me about a man who — if the deeds of all the companions of Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family) were placed together on one pan of the scale, and the deeds of Ali (peace be upon him) were placed on the other — his deeds would outweigh theirs?' Rabi'ah said: I said: Even Abu Bakr and 'Umar? He said: Yes, and all the rest. I said: Even Abu Bakr and 'Umar? He said: (3) O you — how could it be otherwise? Where were Abu Bakr and 'Umar — may your mother mourn you — and all the companions of Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family) on the day of 'Amr ibn 'Abd al-Wad, when he stormed the trench and called for a champion? Every single one of them held back — except Ali, who stepped forward and killed him with his own hand, and thereby caused all the confederate forces to scatter. By Him in Whose hand my soul rests, his deed on that day outweighs in reward the deeds of all the followers of Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family)

until the Day of Judgment.'"

[The Battle of the Banu Qurayza in the Fifth Year]

When the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) returned after the departure of

(1) An addition from "B".
(2) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(3) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.


<!-- page 304 -->
When the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) returned from the trench to the city, with the Muslims alongside him, and had set down his arms, Gabriel (peace be upon him) came to him at midday.

Gabriel said: "Have you laid down your weapons, O Muhammad?"

He said, "Yes." Gabriel replied: "The angels have not yet laid down their weapons. You have come back only from the direction of the people who sought you out. Allah commands you to march against the Banu Qurayzah — I am heading toward them now, and I will shake their strongholds."

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) then ordered his muezzin to call out to the people:

[Pg 183]

"Hear and obey — no one shall pray the afternoon prayer except at Banu Qurayzah."

[Ali (peace be upon him) advances and reports to the Messenger (peace be upon him) what he heard from their fools]

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) sent Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) ahead to the Banu Qurayzah. Ali (peace be upon him) carried the standard forward, and the people followed. As he drew near to their fortresses, he heard them speaking about the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) in vile terms. When the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) himself approached their fortresses, Ali (peace be upon him) went out to meet him and urged him to turn back, wishing to spare him from hearing what they had said. But the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) pressed on, and when he reached them he said: "O brothers of apes — shall Allah not shame you and bring His wrath down upon you?"

Al-Qasim: I was not ignorant of this.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) encamped near a well from among their wells.

(1) The property of the Banu Qurayzah — the well is referred to by a particular name; it was also called by another name.

(1) An addition from the biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 305 -->
[Their siege, and what Ka'b ibn Asad said to them]

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) besieged them for twenty-five nights, and Allah cast terror into their hearts.

Hayy ibn Akhtab had entered their fortress alongside them after the Quraysh and Ghatafan had turned away — fulfilling to Ka'b ibn Asad what he had promised him. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) did not relent until their situation had been decided. Ka'b ibn Asad then said to them: "O community of Jews, you see the situation you are in. I put three options before you — take whichever you will." They said: "And what are they?" He said: [Follow this man and his command, for by Allah you know that he is a prophet sent to you — he is the one you find described in your scripture — and so protect your lives, your wealth, your children, and your women."] They said: "We will never do that. We will never abandon the ruling of the Torah."

He said: "Then let us kill our own sons and women, and go out to Muhammad and his companions with our swords, leaving nothing behind us — either the victory is ours or it is his. If we prevail, we can find women and children again." They said: "Shall we kill these poor souls? What is life worth after them?"

He said: "If you refuse that too, then tonight is the Sabbath night, and perhaps Muhammad and his companions have relaxed their guard — let us descend upon them and perhaps catch them off guard." They said: "Shall we violate our Sabbath and bring upon ourselves what came upon those before us, who transgressed on the Sabbath?" He said: "Not one man among you has had a night's firm resolve since the day his mother bore him."

(1) A necessary addition, omitted in its place: "And he warned them and said, 'O Banu Qurayzah.'" See: Biography of the Prophet, 235/3.

(2) An addition from the Biography of the Prophet, 236/3.

<!-- page 306 -->
[The surrender of the Banu Qurayzah to the ruling of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and the arbitration of Sa'd]

When the siege had worn them down and Allah had cast terror into their hearts, they asked to be placed under the judgment of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family). They said: "O Messenger of Allah, make us loyal allies as you did with the Khazraj — as you did with them yesterday, and as I understand it." They meant the Banu Qaynuqa', in whose case Abdullah ibn Ubayy ibn Salul had interceded with the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him).

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "O community of the Aws — do you agree that a man from among you should rule over them?" They said yes. He said (peace and blessings be upon him and his family): "Then let it be Sa'd ibn Mu'adh."

His people came to him and carried him on a donkey, over which they had placed a leather cushion, for he was a large and handsome man who was suffering from the arrow wound he had received in the trench. They brought Sa'd to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and to the Muslims, and the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "Rise for your master."

They rose for him, and said to him: "O Abu Amr, your mawali have placed their fate in your hands." Sa'd said: "O community of the Ansar — do you covenant by Allah and His pledge to accept my judgment?" They said: "Yes." He said: "O Messenger of Allah, I decree that the men be killed, the women and children taken captive, and the property divided."

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said to Sa'd: "You have judged among them by the judgment of Allah from above seven heavens."

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 240/3; Pillars of Islam, 385/1.

<!-- page 307 -->
[The reason the Banu Qurayzah surrendered to Sa'd's ruling]

Ibn Hisham said: Some of those I trust among the people of knowledge told me: The Banu Qurayzah did not surrender until Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) approached their fortresses carrying the standard and called out: "O battalion of faith — advance!" And he said: "By Allah, I will not turn back until Allah opens this for His Messenger, or until I taste what my uncle Hamza tasted. [Or until their fortress is opened."] When they saw this, they said: "O Muhammad, we surrender to the ruling of Sa'd ibn Mu'adh."

When that had come to pass and Sa'd ibn Mu'adh had delivered his ruling, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had them confined in Medina, in the house of a woman from the Banu al-Najjar.

[The killing of the Banu Qurayzah]

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) then went out to the market of Medina and had trenches dug in it. The men were brought out and their necks were struck over those trenches. Among those brought out was the enemy of Allah, Hayy ibn Akhtab, and Ka'b ibn Asad, the chieftain of the people. They numbered six hundred or seven hundred, and it was also said they were between eight hundred and nine hundred.

When Hayy ibn Akhtab was brought forward, he was wearing a patterned garment that had been torn all around its edges, each tear about a hand's breadth, so that it could not be taken as spoils — his hands were bound to his neck with a rope. When he looked upon the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family), he said: "By Allah, I do not blame myself for having opposed you — but whoever forsakes Allah shall himself be forsaken."

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 240/3.
(2) An addition from the biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 308 -->
Then he turned to the people and said: "O people — the command of Allah is a decree and a fate inscribed for the Children of Israel." Then he sat down, and his neck was struck.

Jabal ibn Jawwal al-Tha'labi said:

And whoever forsakes him, may Allah forsake him —
You never blamed a son who gave himself
And worry seeks shame in every anxious one
To strive until the soul finds its excuse

[The death of Sa'd ibn Mu'adh (may Allah be pleased with him)]

It is related that when the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had finished the campaign against the Banu Qurayzah, the wound of Sa'd ibn Mu'adh burst open and he died a martyr — may Allah have mercy on him.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "The gates of heaven were opened for Sa'd, and the Throne trembled for him."

When Sa'd was buried, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) praised him. The people then noticed that he was walking and moving more briskly than before, and they said: "O Messenger of Allah, why did you walk like that?" He said: "The angels had crowded around him so thickly that I feared being jostled, until Allah granted him relief."

Sa'd (may Allah be pleased with him) was a man of great dignity and high standing. In his honor, one of the Ansar said:

[Pg 141]

(1) An addition from the biography of the Prophet.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 241/3.
(3) See: Biography of the Prophet, 252/3; attributed to Hassan ibn Thabit (see al-Kamil fi al-Adab, 1470/3; Diwan Hassan, 311/1).

<!-- page 309 -->
Did the Throne of Allah tremble at the death of a mortal — such is what we heard, by the Lord of Sa'd ibn Abi Amr. [186]

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) sent Sa'd ibn Zayd, the brother of the Banu Abd al-Ashhal, with some of the captives of the Banu Qurayzah to purchase horses and weapons. For himself, the Prophet chose from among the captive women Rayhana bint Amr ibn Hanafa, one of the women of the Banu Qurayzah.

[Poetry composed about the Battle of the Trench and the Banu Qurayzah]

Ka'b ibn Malik, the brother of the Banu Salamah, said — remembering the day of the trench and the killing of the Banu Qurayzah:

And if you witnessed our patience
In what we endured — then trust us
We all plunged into it together
While they watched us with enmity

With blows delivered in haste
Like the ears of Mulah that streamed upon us
With them we healed every sore
And in our right hands were gleaming blades

Their lips guarded our honor
At the gate of the two trenches, like lions
Against the enemies — our bold teachers

Our knights, when they rode out early,
Were loyal servants of our deliverer

I swear by al-Nasr Ahmad, even
When the parties came in their sorrow
And the people of Makkah know how they marched

Sa'd ibn Mu'adh said [142/f]:

And a questioner asks what we have found —
Our patience sees no injustice before Allah

And the Prophet was a true leader for us
They fought a tenth, oppressive and defiant

Hasten them, if they rise against us —
You will see us bring ruin upon them

(1) See: His Diwan, 215–216.

<!-- page 310 -->
And Allah is the guardian of the believers —
Allah is the best of those who judge

Either you kill Sa'd the fierce
And he enters paradise with good deeds
Or grant our good a place to stand

With your anger, shame our traitors
You were close to destroying us —
Shame that you did not receive that, and then good came

[Pg 142]

And so they were buried beneath it.

A speaker, to one who questions, with an answer
And every view of the encampment dispersed
White faces, accounting — piercing through

White Miss — the hadith of Ka'b
From a time when they wronged the prophet's anger
And I entrust my worries to Allah — and what you see

The people of the villages and the Arab valleys
All marched toward him and gave ear

They are in the arena of the confederates
The army of their fathers, and the son of war among them
Seeking to kill the Prophet — such is the meaning of the slabs

They turned back on their heels in anger
Yet were met with hands capable of more

The winds of a storm that scattered their gathering
And the soldiers of your Lord are the masters

For Allah has no partner —
As your answer was given, there is no wandering
A windstorm will strike you

Hassan ibn Thabit replied:

Is the drawing of the encampment's station at a door
The desolation of chastity — Rahm al-Sahab's markings
And I saw the departing ones adorned with them

So leave the house and remember every —

[Pg 187]

Even when they came to Medina and made it tremble

(1) See: Diwan, 80/1. [It appears the author omitted quoting Ibn al-Za'bari's poem, which Hassan replied to in the Sirah, 282/3–283, as noted: the verses of wear and wandering and ranged caps.] M.S.

The neighborhood's dwellings — erased the knowledge they once drew

<!-- page 311 -->
And reward them with the finest reward —
Send down the text of our King, al-Wahab
And humble every mounted liar

[Pg 143/f]

In unbelief there is no purity of soul
The heart suffered at the site of the banner
In unbelief — the last of these punishments
Misery clung to his heart and core

Allah suffices for the believers as their protector
After they had despaired, He gathered them
And He confirmed the eye of Muhammad and his companions

Musafa' ibn Abd Manaf ibn Wahab ibn Hudhafah ibn Jumah said — weeping for Amr ibn Abd Wad and lamenting his killing at the hands of Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him):

Amr ibn Abd was the foremost knight

[Pg 188]

I would buy other goods, no longer serviceable
Among goods of the south — would that he had not descended

May Allah gladden the heart of that prevailing horseman
The contest was over, and the knight was taken

He sought to fight with a sword that troubled him not
The noble gave himself freely, once for all

For a slave's son among them could not have done it
And you know well, when they turned away from you

He wanted his words — yet found no welcome in my abode
Or had the six been knighted

At dawn — I found no dilemma like it
So come to me, for I will not find his equal

In the bath of death, he did not dissolve
Would that I had given myself for that prevailing horseman

So that I might see a companion who was not let down
I mean the one who sealed the contest with his bridal gift

Until the truffles were enough — all of them

Hubayrah ibn Abi Wahb said — weeping for Amr ibn Abd Wad and lamenting his killing by Ali (peace be upon him):

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 266/3–267; Commentary on Nahj al-Balagha, 288/13.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 268/3.


<!-- page 312 -->
Hassan ibn Thabit said about Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf — whose killing we have already mentioned — in reference to the killing of Ibn Abi al-Haqiq:

*O son of al-Haqiq, and you son of al-Ashraf,*
*I found a band*
*Bold as lions in their den*
*They pressed upon you*
*They struck you*
*Even in the heart of your own land*
*Contemptuous of every trifling thing*
*Clairvoyants, upholding the religion of their prophet*

The conquest of Banu Qurayza took place in Dhu al-Qa'dah and the opening days of Dhu al-Hijjah. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) returned to Madinah in Dhu al-Hijjah.

**[The Battle of Banu Lahyan]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) remained in Madinah through Dhu al-Hijjah, Muharram, Safar, and the two months of Rabi'. He then launched his campaign against Banu Lahyan in Jumada al-Ula, six months after the conquest of Banu Qurayza. He sought to avenge the companions of Khubaib ibn 'Adi and those who had died with him — may Allah have mercy on them — and he let it be known that he intended to head toward the Levant in order to take the people by surprise. He moved swiftly until he descended upon Gharran, which was the territory

<!-- page 313 -->
of Banu Lahyan. He found them forewarned and sheltering in the heights of the mountains. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) descended and found that he had missed the opportunity he had hoped for, he said: "Had I descended upon 'Usfan, the people of Mecca would have seen it." So he marched out with two hundred of his companions until he reached 'Usfan, then sent two horsemen ahead until they reached the level grazing ground of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and then returned.

**[The supplication of the Prophet upon his return]**

Jabir ibn 'Abd Allah said: I heard the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) when he returned say:

*"We are returning, repentant, if Allah wills — our Lord is praiseworthy. I seek refuge in Allah from the hardships of travel, from distressing sights along the way, and from finding family and wealth in a bad state."*

**[The poetry of Ka'b in the Battle of Banu Lahyan]**

Ka'b ibn Malik said concerning the Battle of Banu Lahyan:

*They found among them a believer of steadfast nerve*
*Had the sons of Lahyan been made to answer*
*Before them a host like a rushing galaxy*
*They quickly found the squadron tireless and wondrous*
*The strongholds of the Hijaz yield not in agreement*
*But they were righteous and followed.*
(1) See: Biography of the Prophet 280/3.
(2) See: Dictionary of Countries, 247/4.
(3) See: Diwan, 294.

<!-- page 314 -->
**[The Battle of Dhu Qarad]**

The Battle of Dhu Qarad came about because 'Uyaynah ibn Husn ibn Hudhayfah ibn Badr al-Fazari raided with cavalry from Ghatafan and struck the herds of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) in the forest. Among those present were a man from Banu Ghifar and a woman of his household. They killed the man, took the woman captive, and drove off the camels.

**[The pursuit of Ibn al-Akwa' in this raid]**

The first Muslim to give chase was Salamah ibn 'Amr ibn al-Akwa'. He was heading toward the forest, carrying his bow and quiver. He shouted at the raiders, drove them into opposition against one another, and shot at them, saying as he loosed his arrows:

*Take that — I am the son of al-Akwa'*
*Today is a day of slaughter for the young!*

**[The Messenger of Allah reaches Dhu Qarad]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) heard the cry of Ibn al-Akwa' and commanded

<!-- page 315 -->
the call to be made throughout the city. The horsemen rode out toward the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and the first of the knights to reach him was al-Miqdad ibn al-Aswad. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then ordered al-Miqdad to lead eight horsemen in pursuit of the raiders, and he placed Sa'd ibn Zayd in command of them.
(1) See: Biography of the Prophet 282/3.
(2) Addition from "B".

<!-- page 316 -->
The rest of the people set out after them, accompanied by 'Ali ibn Abi Talib and the other Muslims.

**[Those Who Were Slain]**

The first Muslim to overtake the raiders was Muhriz ibn Nadhlah, of the Banu Asad ibn Khuzaymah, who was also called al-Akhram. He confronted them and called out: "Stand, O sons of al-Lakiya, until those behind you of the Muhajirun and the Ansar can reach you!" He was then overwhelmed. A man from Ghatafan killed him. The Persians scattered but he could not be saved, and the people returned to Madinah. It was said that Waqqas ibn Mujazz al-Madlahi was also killed alongside him.

**[The Polytheist Casualties]**

When the Muslim horsemen gave chase, Abu Qatadah al-Harith ibn Rabi'i, of the Banu Salamah, caught up with Habib ibn 'Uyaynah ibn Husn al-Fazari, killed him, and beheaded him, then rejoined the people. When the Muslims arrived with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), they found that Habib al-Fazari had been smeared with the blood of Abu Qatadah's camel, and they assumed that Abu Qatadah had been killed. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "It is not Abu Qatadah — he is the slain enemy of Abu Qatadah, covered in the camel's blood so that it might be known who its owner is." 'Ukasha ibn Muhsin, of the Banu Asad ibn Khuzaymah, caught up with 'Ubara and his son 'Amr

<!-- page 317 -->
ibn 'Ubara of Ghatafan, pinned them both with a single spear, killed them both, and recovered some of the Muslims' camels.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) marched on until he descended from the heights of Dhu Qarad. The people urged him on, saying: "O Messenger of Allah, if only we could strike the people!" He replied: "By now they are already in Ghatafan."

**[Dividing the Camels Among the Muslims]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) distributed the recovered camels among his companions, allotting a share to every hundred men. Then the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and those with him departed in a caravan until they reached the outskirts of Madinah.

**[The Woman of al-Ghifari and Her Vow to the Prophet]**

The wife of al-Ghifari, whom Ghatafan had taken along with the camels, seized one of the camels belonging to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and rode it to him. She told him what had happened and said: "O Messenger of Allah, I vowed to Allah that if He brought me safely through on it, I would slaughter it." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) smiled and then said: "That is a poor reward — Allah carried you to safety on it, and then you slaughter it! A vow made in disobedience to Allah is not binding, and you have no right over what is not yours. It is a camel of mine, so return home in the blessing of Allah."
(1) Addition from the Biography of the Prophet.
(2) Addition from the Biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 318 -->
**[Hassan's Poetry on the Battle of Dhu Qarad]**

Hassan ibn Thabit said concerning the matter of Dhu al-Qarad:

*In the south of Saya, yesterday, in glory*
*Had it not been for those who found and touched its eagles*
*To meet you bearing every heavy burden*
*The protectors of truth, noble in lineage*
*And the secret of the sons of the free is that we*
*Found peace in the aftermath thanks to the knights of al-Miqdad*
*Out of zeal, they leveled their spears at a father*
*We were eight, and they were confounded.*
*We were among those who drove them back and offered the charging of every horse*

*They cut across the breadth of the battlefield*
*Nay, by the Lord of the pilgrims, not one of them*
*Will return with queens and children*
*Until the arrows of fate reach them on your fields*
*In every battle there is valor and a valley*
*Rejoice with every swift and stalwart warrior*

*A day to lead and a day to be driven*
*I destroyed its wheels and its planks*
*And war is kindled by the wind of Allah*
*So our resolve remains unshaken*
*The clash of iron and the weight of repeated blows*
*Our swords are the finest of iron*
*And to the glory of the Most Merciful in recompense.*
*Allah took them away, though they were forbidden*
*The days of Dhu Qarad revealed the faces of servants*
*They dwelt in ease, then their fortune changed*

**[Poetry of Ka'b ibn Malik on the Battle of Dhu Qarad]**

Ka'b ibn Malik said on the day of Dhu al-Qarad:

*Do you imagine, O sons of the free, that we on horseback are not their match in cavalry?*
(1) See: Diwan 276/1–277.
(2) See: Diwan 176–177.

<!-- page 319 -->
*We do not flinch at the trampled shafts of spears*
*We are a people who do not regard killing as a reproach*
*We strike the head of the despicable informer*
*We honor the guest and ward off every oppression*
*By striking it we amuse the idle*
*The lessons of teachers return when chosen*
*A generous, pure-blooded warrior at the morning meal*
*And every young man who is the champion of truth, noble*
*Inspired by the notching of the bows*
*They defend their rights and their children*
*I asked Banu Badr if I met them — what al-Ahwan did on the day of the assembly*
*And keep your news secret in the councils*
*If you do not go out, trust whoever you find*
*With it, and free in the breast unless tested*
*And say: release us from the snares of al-Khadir*

**[The Battle of Banu al-Mustaliq at al-Muraysi']**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had spent part of Jumada in Madinah. Then Banu al-Mustaliq of Khuza'ah mounted their campaign in Sha'ban of the sixth year of the Hijra. Word had reached the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that they were gathering to make war against him. Their commander was al-Harith ibn Abi Dirar, the father of Juwayriyah bint al-Harith, who later became a wife of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) heard this, he marched out from Madinah toward them and engaged them at a watering place that was one of their own sources of water, known as al-Muraysi', lying toward the coast in the direction of Qadid. He struck them there; many were killed and many others taken captive.

**[The Battle Cry of the Muslims]**

The battle cry of the Muslims on the day of Banu al-Mustaliq was: *"O Mansur, slay!"*

<!-- page 320 -->
**[The Slaying of Banu al-Mustaliq's Leaders]**

'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) charged against them, broke their ranks, and killed Malik and his son, striking off their heads. The Muslims then pursued the rest, killing many of them. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) distributed a great number of the captives among the Muslims.

**[The Story of Juwayriyah bint al-Harith]**

Juwayriyah was the daughter of al-Harith ibn Abi Dirar. She became a wife of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). She had fallen by lot to Thabit ibn al-Dahdah, or to his cousin, and she drew up a contract of emancipation with him and went to seek the help of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). When she had finished settling her contract of emancipation, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) married her. News of the marriage spread among the people that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had taken her as a wife, and the Muslims said: "She is now the in-law of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)." So they released those captives who were in their hands. The narrators said: "A hundred people from the households of Banu al-Mustaliq were freed because of her." She was thus the most blessed of women for her own people.

**[The Death of Ibn Sababa]**

On the day of Banu al-Mustaliq, a Muslim man from Banu Kalb ibn 'Awf ibn 'Amir ibn Laith ibn Bakr was killed. He was known as Hisham ibn Sababa. A man of the Ansar who worshipped alongside Ibn al-Samt struck him, mistaking him for a polytheist, and killed him in error.


<!-- page 321 -->
[Jahjah, Sinan, and the Affair of Ibn Abi]

**293. The Battle of the Parties (The Trench)**

At the watering place of Bani al-Mustaliq, when the people were returning, a hired hand of 'Umar ibn al-Khattab from Banu Ghifar — known as Jahjah ibn Mas'ud — was leading his horse. He jostled a man from Juhayna who was an ally of Banu Auf ibn al-Khazraj at the water, and a quarrel broke out. Al-Juhani called out: "O people of the Ansar!" and Jahjah called out: "O people of the Muhajireen!" This ignited a heated exchange between 'Umar and 'Abdullah ibn Abi ibn Salul, along with a faction of hypocrites, and they came close to driving a wedge between the Muhajireen and the Ansar.

[The Messenger of Allah leads the people onward to avert fitna]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) then set out with the people without halting. He kept them marching through the day and into the night until they were exhausted from the long march and worn down by the heat of the sun, and only then did he give the order to make camp. When they reached the ground they fell asleep at once — and this was precisely the intention of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family): to distract the people from the dispute that had erupted among them.

[The Prophet, Usayd ibn Hudayr, and the words of Ibn Abi ibn Salul]

'Abdullah ibn Abi ibn Salul had said: "When we return to Madinah, the mightier will drive out the humbler — they have come into our own land." When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) finished the march and dismounted, Usayd ibn Hudayr came to greet him, and his tone conveyed displeasure. He said: "O Messenger of Allah, you set out at an hour

<!-- page 322 -->
I cannot think you would normally have chosen." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said to him: "Did you not hear what Ibn Abi ibn Salul said?" He replied: "What did he say, O Messenger of Allah?" He said: "He claimed that when he returns to Madinah, the mightier will drive out the humbler." Usayd said: "O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, it is you who are the mighty and he who is the humble — and if you will it, Allah will drive him out. O Messenger of Allah, deal gently with him, for Allah has brought him to you. His own people had been preparing beads to crown him as their king, and he now sees that you have supplanted him in that."

[Muqees ibn Sababa and his treachery in avenging his brother's blood]

As for Muqees ibn Sababa: after his brother was killed by a Muslim who mistakenly believed him to be a polytheist, Muqees came from Mecca pretending to have embraced Islam.

He said: "O Messenger of Allah, I have come to seek the blood money for my brother." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) ordered the blood money to be paid to him. He then stayed with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) for a few days before turning on his brother's killer and slaying him, after which he fled to Mecca as an apostate. Muqees said in verse:

Muqees ibn Sababa said:

He was struck by a blow that reached her and left her paralyzed,
From the soaking of the blades that rise above it and prevail.
Do not trust the sons of Bakr if they wrong you —
I said it, and death is sustained by those who seek it.

[Al-Walid ibn 'Uqbah and Banu al-Mustaliq, and what was revealed in the Quran about this]

In the days after that, Banu al-Mustaliq accepted Islam, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) sent a messenger to them —

<!-- page 323 -->
al-Walid ibn 'Uqbah. When Banu al-Mustaliq heard of his coming, they rode out to receive him and welcome him with good tidings of their Islam, just as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had dispatched him. However, al-Walid turned back to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and claimed that the tribe had rebelled, were planning to kill him, and had refused to give him the charity due from them — to the point that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) was on the verge of sending an expedition against them. While this was being prepared, their delegation arrived and said: "O Messenger of Allah, we remain firm in our Islam. We heard that your messenger was coming, so we rode out to receive and honor him and to hand over to him whatever charity we owed. He turned back and then claimed we had gone out to kill him — by God, we intended no such thing."

Allah then revealed concerning al-Walid ibn 'Uqbah: *"O you who believe, if a wicked person comes to you with news, verify it, lest you harm a people out of ignorance and then regret what you have done, and know that among you is the Messenger of Allah; were he to obey you in many matters, you would surely suffer"* [al-Hujurat: 6] and what follows.

**The Battle of the Parties (The Trench)**

[The account of Banu al-Mustaliq]

'A'isha bint Abi Bakr was with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) on this journey. She fell behind, and it was said that she had gone out to relieve herself; the litter was lifted and she was not in it, and what was said was said. It is reported that a verse was revealed to the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) declaring her innocence.

[Hadith: "I liken people to the Messiah"]

It is narrated from Salman al-Farisi (may Allah be pleased with him) that he said:

<!-- page 324 -->
When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) departed from the battle of Banu al-Mustaliq, he placed himself at the head of the people and commanded 'Ali (peace be upon him) to bring up the rear. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) reached Madinah and came to the door of the mosque, he sat and waited for 'Ali (peace be upon him). He did not enter his house. He wiped the sweat from his forehead, then said — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — "In this very hour, from among these people" — and he gestured with his hand toward some of the ranks — "there will emerge a man who resembles the Messiah (peace be upon him) in his standing among people. He is the best of people after me on the Day of Resurrection, and the first to enter Paradise."

He then had us watch the people, and the first to emerge was 'Ali ibn Abi Talib. When he came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), <إليه> he rose and embraced him, kissed him between the eyes, and they entered together (peace be upon them). Some of the hypocrites said: "He likens his cousin to the Messiah and equates him with him — are our gods, whom we used to worship, better, or is 'Ali?" Allah (may He be glorified and exalted) then revealed: *"And when the son of Mary was cited as an example, your people immediately raised an uproar ﴾ and they said, 'Are our gods better, or is he?' They cited him only to argue — indeed, they are a contentious people"* [al-Zukhruf: 57–58].

<!-- page 325 -->
Then came the 'Umrah of al-Hudaybiyah:

**'Umrah al-Hudaybiyah**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) spent the months of Ramadan and Shawwal in Madinah, then set out in Dhu al-Qi'dah as one intending 'Umrah and not war.
