Abu Sufyan, do you not yet know that there is no god but Allah?" He replied: "By my father and mother, how I honor you, revere you, and follow your guidance — yet I confess, by God, that something still lingers in my soul regarding this."

Al-Abbas said to him: "Submit! Accept Islam and testify that there is no god but Allah, and that he is the Messenger of Allah — before the sword reaches your neck."

Abu Sufyan pronounced the testimony, finding before him nothing but faith or the sword.

Al-Abbas said: I then said, "O Messenger of Allah, Abu Sufyan is a man who loves distinction — grant him something." He said: "Yes. Whoever enters the house of Abu Sufyan is safe, whoever closes his door is safe, and whoever enters the mosque is safe."

When Abu Sufyan was about to leave, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to al-Abbas: "Detain him at the narrow mouth of the valley, at the bend of the pass, until the armies of God have filed past him."

Al-Abbas (may Allah be pleased with him) said: I carried out what the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had commanded. The tribes marched past with their banners, and not one tribe passed without

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 403/4.

<!-- page 376 -->
Abu Sufyan asking about them. Each time I named a tribe, he would say: "What have they to do with me? What have I to do with them?" Then the next tribe would come, and he would ask again: "Who are these?" I would say: "Such-and-such tribe." He would say: "What have they to do with me?" — and so it continued, tribe after tribe, each one he asked about, and when I told him, he said: "What are they to me and mine?" Until the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) himself passed at the head of his Green Battalion, comprising the Emigrants and the Helpers, clad in iron so that nothing could be seen of them but their eyes. Abu Sufyan said: "Glory be to Allah, O Abbas — who are these?" I said: "This is the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) with the Muhajireen and the Ansar." He said: "No one before has ever commanded power like this. By God, O Abu al-Fadl, your nephew's kingship has grown mighty today!"

I said: "It is prophethood, O Abu Sufyan." He said: "Yes — then it is prophethood." I then said to him: "Go quickly and warn your people."

[Abu Sufyan returns to the people of Mecca to warn them]

When Abu Sufyan came back to them, he called out at the top of his voice: "O assembly of Quraysh! Muhammad has come upon you with something you cannot withstand. Whoever enters the house of Abu Sufyan is safe." His wife Hind bint 'Utbah leapt up, seized him by his moustache, and cried: "Kill this foul fat traitor — what a wretched chieftain of a people!" He said: "Woe to you, do not let her deceive you. Whoever enters the house of Abu Sufyan is safe."

They said: "What use is your house to us?"

(1) An addition from "B."
(2) See: al-Kamil 322/1–323.

<!-- page 377 -->
He said: "Whoever closes his door is safe, and whoever enters the mosque is safe."

So the people dispersed to their homes and to the mosque.

[The Prophet reaches Dhi Tawa]

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) arrived at Dhi Tawa, he halted there, bowing his head in humility to Allah for the honour Allah had bestowed upon him through this conquest — bowing so low that his head nearly touched the saddle of his mount.

[The Muslim armies enter Mecca]

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) marshalled the multitude of Muslims, Allah had strengthened their victory and made Mecca within their grasp. Its people looked upon what they could not resist and had no power to oppose, and so they submitted, sought pardon, and entered into peace.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) entered Mecca that day at the head of an army the Arabs had never seen the like of — so great in number and so formidable in arms that no man could stare at another from the glare of iron. He placed the Ansar on the right, with their banner carried by Sa'd ibn 'Ubadah, and the Muhajireen on the left, with their banner carried by al-Zubayr ibn al-'Awwam. He said to each of them: "Enter from the place of sorrow."

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 4/405.
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 305/1.
(3) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.

<!-- page 378 -->
[The Muhajireen's concern over what Sa'd ibn 'Ubadah might do to Quraysh]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was in the midst of the Muhajireen, the Ansar, and all the other people, with each Arab tribe in great numbers. 'Umar ibn al-Khattab heard Sa'd ibn 'Ubadah, banner in hand, as he was about to enter Mecca, saying:

*Today is the day of the great battle,*
*Today the sanctuary shall be made lawful.*

'Umar went immediately to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and reported this to him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then said to Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him): "Go and take the banner from him, and be the one who enters with it." So Ali did so. And in this way Ali (peace be upon him) was the bearer of the Prophet's banner in war and the standard of his peace.

[Those whom the Prophet ordered to be killed]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) commanded the leaders of the battalions not to kill anyone except those who fought them — with certain exceptions he named — and he ordered that those exceptions be killed even if they were found sheltering beneath

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 305/1–306.
(2) See: al-Sira al-Nabawiyya 406/4.
(3) "B": "Hatq," as it also appears in Sharh al-Akhbar; what has been established comes from the Prophet's biography.
(4) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 306/1–307.
(5) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(6) "They found" in Sharh al-Akhbar.

<!-- page 379 -->
the curtains of the Kaaba, on account of the grave crimes they had committed. Many of them, however, were spared by those who encountered them — men who had prior knowledge of them, who cared for them, or who had given and received pledges between themselves — and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) accepted their intercession and returned his command concerning those men to them.

[The account of 'Abdullah ibn Sa'd]

Among those condemned was 'Abdullah ibn Sa'd, the client-brother of Banu 'Amir ibn Lu'ay. His offense was the gravest of all, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was more wrathful with him than with any other. He had been the first whose blood was formally declared forfeit, and the Prophet had said: "Kill him even if you find him clinging to the curtains of the Kaaba." This was because the man had been a Muslim, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had employed him to write down the Revelation. The Prophet would dictate to him, saying: "Forgiving, Merciful," and he would write instead: "'Aziz, Hakim" — and the like of that.

He then apostatized and joined the polytheists, saying to them: "I used to turn our Quran however I wished, and I made it serve myself!"

In response, Allah revealed: *And who is more unjust than one who invents a lie about Allah or says, "It has been revealed to me," while nothing has been revealed to him* [al-An'am: 93].

'Abdullah then took refuge with 'Uthman ibn 'Affan, who brought him secretly before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)

(1) See: Ansab al-Ashraf 429/1–430, Futuh al-Buldan 582/3, al-Manaqib wa al-Muqalab [folio 55/f].
(2) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(3) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(4) See: Tafsir al-Tabari 533/11–534.

<!-- page 380 -->
and interceded for him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) remained silent and gave no answer. 'Uthman pressed him again and again.

At last the Prophet released him, saying: "Go — your way is free."

Then he said to the Muslims who were present: "I stayed silent for so long so that one of you might rise and strike his neck, as I had commanded — yet none of you did."

They said: "O Messenger of Allah, why did you not signal to us?"

He said: "A prophet does not kill by signal."

[The account of al-Huwayrith ibn Naqidh]

Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) encountered al-Huwayrith ibn Naqidh, who was one of those whose blood the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had declared forfeit that day. Al-Huwayrith recognized Ali (peace be upon him), and Ali (peace be upon him) said to him: "Enemy of God — you are here!" Al-Huwayrith said to him: "Wait for me, O son of Abi Talib." Ali (peace be upon him) replied: "I will not keep you waiting for God's sake." Ali killed him, peace be upon him.

[Umm Hani' shelters two men]

Ali (peace be upon him) then went to his sister Umm Hani' bint Abi Talib, and he was still carrying his sword.

(1) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(2) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(3) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 307/1.
(4) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 307/1–308.

<!-- page 381 -->
There were with her two men whose blood the Messenger of Allah had declared forfeit, from Banu Makhzum, and they were under her protection. When Ali (peace be upon him) saw them, he drew his sword and moved to kill them. Umm Hani' stood between him and them and said: "My brother, I have given them my protection." He said: "The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) has ordered them to be killed even if they are found beneath the curtains of the Kaaba." She seized his hand — and her grip was strong — and pulled the sword from it, then commanded the two men to enter a house, which they did, closing it upon themselves.

She then went to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and when he saw her, he welcomed her warmly and asked how she was and what had brought her to him. She told him what had happened, and he laughed, and said: "We have honored those you have taken under your protection, O Umm Hani'."

He then sent for Ali (peace be upon him), who came, and the Prophet laughed with him and said: "Umm Hani' has prevailed over you." Ali (peace be upon him) said: "O Messenger of Allah, by Him who sent you with the truth — she did not cease struggling until she wrested the sword from my hand." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) laughed and said: "If Abu Talib had filled the world with his children, they would all have been courageous and strong."

[The Prophet restores the key of the Ka'ba to 'Uthman ibn Talha and the office of al-Sadana]

Ali (peace be upon him) had taken the keys of the Ka'ba at the time of entry and brought them to

(1) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(2) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(3) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(4) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(5) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 308/1.

<!-- page 382 -->
the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). He said: "O Messenger of Allah, here is the key of the Ka'ba. If you wish to unite in our hands both the watering of the pilgrims and the custodianship of the sanctuary, then do so." The Prophet said: "O Ali, I give you something better than that — what Allah has given us today is a day of righteousness and faithfulness. But I shall give you what you seek, not what you desire. Return the keys to 'Uthman ibn Talha, the brother of Banu Shayba ibn 'Abd al-Dar ibn 'Abd Manaf." He handed them back to him and said: "O Messenger of Allah, we are content with whatever you are content with, and we are with you, O Messenger of Allah."

[Ali's pride in being the first to believe and the first to strive in the way of Allah]

The Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), said:

"Al-Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib, 'Uthman ibn Talha — the brother of Banu Shayba — and I were once in the Sacred Mosque, each boasting of his distinction. 'Uthman ibn Talha said: 'The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) gave me al-Sadana — that is, the keys of the Ka'ba.' Al-Abbas said: 'And the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) gave me the watering of the pilgrims — that is, the keys of Zamzam.' They said: 'And what did he give you, Ali?' So Allah (Exalted be He) revealed: *Do you consider the providing of water to the pilgrim and the maintenance of the Sacred Mosque equal to the deeds of one who believes in Allah and the Last Day and strives in the cause of*

(1) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 19/1, Sharh al-Akhbar 2/342, Manaqib al-Imam 134/1–135, Kitab al-Munazarat 18, Tafsir Furat al-Kufi 165–169, Tafsir al-Qummi 283/1, Virtues of Ali by Ibn Mardawayh 256–257, Dala'il al-Tanzil 1/324, Manaqib Ali by Ibn al-Maghazali 321, Fada'il al-Sa'ada 82, Dala'il al-Sidq 320/1–330.


<!-- page 383 -->
These are the Quranic verses revealed in that context: those who believed and emigrated and strove in the way of Allah with their wealth and their lives hold the greatest rank in the sight of Allah — *"and those are the triumphant. Their Lord gives them glad tidings of mercy from Him, and of His pleasure, and of gardens where they shall have enduring bliss. They will abide therein forever, for Allah has a great reward"* (Quran 9:19–22).

**[The sermon of the Messenger of Allah at the door of the Ka'bah]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) stood and addressed the people, saying:

"There is no god but Allah alone, with no partner. He has fulfilled His promise, granted victory to His servant, and routed the confederates by Himself alone. Every claim of blood, wealth, or feudal privilege is hereby placed under my feet — except the custodianship of the Ka'bah and the watering of the pilgrims. Know that unintentional killing carried out with a whip or stick is treated as quasi-deliberate manslaughter, for which the blood-money is severe: forty camels, including those pregnant in their bellies.

O assembly of Quraysh, Allah has stripped from you the arrogance of the pre-Islamic era and its glorification of ancestry. All people descend from Adam, and Adam was created from dust."

He then recited the verse: *"O mankind, We have created you from a male and a female and made you peoples and tribes so that you may know one another. Truly, the most honourable of you in the sight of Allah is the most righteous"* (Quran 49:13).

He then said: "O assembly of Quraysh, what do you think I am about to do with you?" They said: "Good — a generous brother and the son of a generous brother." He said: "Go, for you are free."

**[A sermon of the Prophet at the Conquest]**

He then said: "O people, Allah declared Mecca sacred on the day He created the heavens

<!-- page 384 -->
and the earth — sacred until the Day of Resurrection. It is therefore not lawful for anyone who believes in Allah and the Last Day to shed blood in it or to uproot a tree from it. It was not made lawful to anyone before me, and it shall not be made lawful to anyone after me. It was made lawful to me only for this hour, out of anger against its people. Now it has returned to the same sanctity it held yesterday. Let those of you who are present convey this to those who are absent. And if anyone claims that the Messenger of Allah fought here, say to him: 'Allah made it lawful to His Messenger and did not make it lawful to you.'"

**[The march of Khalid ibn al-Walid to Bani Judhimah, and the march of Ali (peace be upon him) to remedy Khalid's error]**

After the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had conquered Mecca and its people had submitted, he sent out several detachments to call the surrounding Arab tribes to Allah and His Messenger — not to wage war. Among those he sent was Khalid ibn al-Walid.

Bani Judhimah ibn 'Amir [ibn 'Abd Mana'ah] came out to meet Khalid's force in arms. He told them: "Lay down your weapons, for the people have surrendered and the fighting is over. The Messenger of Allah has commanded us only to call people to Islam, not to fight anyone." They put down their weapons, but one of them called out defiantly. Khalid replied in kind, and then — by Allah — once they had laid down their weapons and he had taken them captive, he ordered their necks to be struck. He had them bound and executed.

<!-- page 385 -->
The survivors protested: "O Khalid, do you wish to shed our blood? The people have already converted and laid down their arms." He said: "By Allah, I will not lay down my weapon." They were overpowered, their weapons taken from them, and after they had disarmed, Khalid commanded them to halt — then put a group of them to the sword.

When the news reached the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), he rose and lifted his hands toward the sky, saying: "O Allah, I am innocent before You of what Khalid has done."

He then summoned Ali (peace be upon him) and said: "O Ali, go out to these people, assess their situation, and put the ways of the pre-Islamic era under your feet." He provided him with funds and said: "Compensate those who were killed, pay the blood-money, and pay for everything that was taken from them and more."

Ali (peace be upon him) set out and paid the blood-money for those who had been killed and paid compensation for every loss suffered — even, it is said, paying the value of a dog's bowl — so that nothing remained outstanding, neither in blood nor in property. He then asked them: "Is there any remaining claim of blood or property that has not been settled?" They said: "No." He said: "Then I shall distribute among you what remains of the funds entrusted to me by the Messenger of Allah, as a precaution on his behalf and on behalf of his family." He distributed to them everything that remained, and they received it, thanked him, and bade him farewell.

<!-- page 386 -->
He then returned to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and reported what had happened. The Prophet said: "Well done, Ali — well done."

Then he — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — turned toward the qibla, stood upright, and raised his hands toward the sky until the whiteness of his armpits was visible, and said three times: "O Allah, I am innocent before You of what Khalid ibn al-Walid has done."

The reason Khalid acted as he did was that Bani Judhimah had killed his uncle al-Fakkah ibn al-Mughirah during the pre-Islamic era.

**[The poetry of Hassan ibn Thabit on the day of the Conquest of Mecca]**

Hassan ibn Thabit composed the following verses on the day of the Conquest — though some say he composed them before that day:

*To a virgin dwelling whose house stands empty*
*Refined, with gentle fingers in the open air*
*Its heights and the sky above offer relief*
*A home among the sons of al-Hashas, lying waste*
*Through its meadows — yes, and willingly*
*It haunts me when the evening passes*
*Let this go, yet it has its beauty*
*His heart finds no cure for it.*
*Its spring flows with honey and water*
*For the benefit of those who seek redemption*
*Whether moth or bark*
*We lay blame on her when our pain*
*Had its companion still within.*
*For the grief that has left him orphaned*
*It is as though hiding from a house of refuge.*

[Pg 178] *If the scarves were mentioned one day,*

<!-- page 387 -->
*And drink — it leaves us as kings and lions, and we bring the gathering to an end.*
*We have lost our horses if you cannot see them*
*Contesting the lead*

[Pg 239]
*It avails when it is a disease*
*Upon her shoulders the thirsty throng*
*Our allegiance remains firm through every downpour*
*He strikes them with crimson warriors*
*The opening and the lifting of the veil —*
*Either they offered us the 'Umrah*
*And Allah aids whom He wills*
*Or else be patient for the day of reckoning*
*And the Holy Spirit shall not fall short*
*And Jibril is the Messenger of Allah among us*
*Truth speaks, and when calamity descends,*
*Allah said: "I have sent a servant."*
*And you said: "We will not rise, and we will not*
*Bear witness to him — so believe him."*
*The Ansar brought it before the assembly*
*And Allah said: "I have marched a host."*
*For us each day comes ready*
*With insult, battle, or satire*
*So we judge through our verses from afar*
*And we strike when blood runs mingled*
*The confusion remains concealed.*
*Should I not inform Abu Sufyan about me?*
*The servants of the House were its masters*

[Pg 179/f]
*That our swords leave you as a slave*
*And with Allah in that is reward*
*Your evil is not equal to redemption*
*I satirize him, though I am not adequate for him*
*Amen — Allah is the object of loyalty*
*A blessed mission, an honest and fair undertaking*
*And praise him and support him alike*
*Who mocks the Messenger of Allah among you?*
*To offer Muhammad from you protection —*
*My father, his father, and my offering.*
*And a sea whose buckets are never satisfied*
*My tongue is firm and flawless*
*I came to Muhammad and answered him*

<!-- page 388 -->
**[The poetry of Buhayr ibn Zuhayr on the day of the Conquest]**

Buhayr ibn Zuhayr ibn Abi Salma said on the day of the Conquest:

*Adorned with valor and bright sons*
*The people of al-Hablaq were driven out on a fierce day*
*We struck them in Mecca on the day of the Conquest*
*Dealt with well — with pumice-smooth strokes*
*And a thousand of Banu 'Uthman, steadfast*
*And a cast with the nimble archer*
*We see between the ranks that she bears a smile*
*As the lances obeyed from the paving stones*
*With the spears that are the pillar of discipline*
*We were gladdened and the horses surged into them*
*Their forebears regretted the dispute*
*We are enriched with what we sought.*
*Our compact rests on honourable clearance*
*And we rendered to the Messenger of Allah from among us*

[Pg 179/I] *Those who heard our word and understood — / The terror of that day departed from us by the next morning.*

*We came upon you at dawn with seven from Salim*

[Pg 240] *Who struck and thrust into their shoulders.*

**[The poetry of al-'Abbas ibn Mardas on the Conquest of Mecca]**

Al-'Abbas ibn Mardas said concerning the Conquest of Mecca:

*A thousand streams with which the plain was soaked*
*From us in Mecca on the day of Muhammad's Conquest*
*And their war-cry on the day of the encounter rang out:*
*"Support the Prophet and witness his days!"*
*It was as though inspiration poured tenderness upon it*
*In a house whose feet stood firm*
*Until the Hijaz guided her people to listen*
*She set her forces running before her*
*The rule of swords belongs to us, and it was crowded —*
*Allah enabled him and humiliated his enemy*

<!-- page 389 -->
*The leading staff raised high and bare, outwitting the noble Khudram.*

**[The Islam of Ibn al-Zuba'ari and his poetry on that occasion]**

Ibn al-Zuba'ari had taken refuge in Najran. Hassan ibn Thabit composed verses against him in which he said:

*Do not make a journey darker with the hatred of Najran, in the life of the most wretched of footwear.*

Hearing this, Ibn al-Zuba'ari went out to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and embraced Islam. He then said:

*And the night is sick, the hallway is foolish,*
*preventing sleep — nightingale and worries alike.*
*What came to me was that Ahmad blamed me,*
*and at that I became as though I had lost my mind.*
*The hands are soiled before us —*
*O best of those who ever bore a burden on her dismemberment.*
*I offer my apology to you, who —*

[Pg 241]
*— I lied when I stood in prayer.*
*An arrow — and you command me to follow a path*
*in days that urge me toward temptation and scheme.*

[Pg 180/f]
*The affair of the seducers and their matter is ominous,*
*and the causes of ruin are extended and lead me astray.*
*My heart — and the wrongdoer is deprived.*
*Today Muhammad has accepted my faith;*
*the enmity has passed and its causes are gone.*
*So forgive both my parents*
*and the bonds between us now made whole.*
*Slept — for you are merciful to the departed.*
*Light endures and destiny is inescapable.*
*And you have a sign from the banner of kings —*
*honour and promise — Allah is great.*
*He gave you after love a proof*

<!-- page 390 -->
*of truth — and you are earnest in the service of mankind,*
*a future among the righteous, generous.*
*Firmly rooted in al-Dara and Arum.*

**[Ali (peace be upon him) brought down the idols from the roof of the Ka'bah and smashed them]**

The next morning, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) approached the Ka'bah, which was ringed with idols that he began to break and destroy.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) saw idols mounted atop the Ka'bah and said to Ali ibn Abi Talib: "O Ali, place me upon your shoulders so that I may climb up and break those idols on the roof of the Ka'bah."

Ali replied: "No, O Messenger of Allah — rather, you mount my shoulders." The Prophet said: "O Ali, even if all the people of the earth were gathered and tried to carry me for as long as they could, they could not do what I command you to do."

Ali (peace be upon him) said: "I mounted the shoulders of the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), and he raised me up to such a height that, had I wished, I could have reached the sky and touched its stars — and I did what I was commanded."

When Ali (peace be upon him) climbed onto the roof of the Ka'bah, he hurled the idols down to the earth and they shattered. Then, feeling abashed before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family),

<!-- page 391 -->
he leapt from the roof to the ground and landed upright. The Messenger of Allah smiled at him.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Why do you smile, O Ali?" [Pg 242]

He said: "O Messenger of Allah, I did not expect to land so well from such a height!"

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "You stood upon the shoulder of Muhammad, and I descended upon the shoulder of Jibril."

**[The Prophet ordered the demolition of al-'Uzza]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered that every idol in the vicinity of Mecca be destroyed. Among them was a shrine held in great veneration by the Quraysh and Banu Kinana [and all who were associated with it]. Its custodians and its veil belonged to the Bani Shayban of Banu Sulaym, who were allies of Banu Hashim. When the guardian of the shrine heard what was coming, he hung his sword upon it and leaned against the mountain in which it stood, saying:

*O al-'Uzza, however fierce the trial that Khalid brings upon you — I cast off my cloak and throw it aside.*
*My father rushes forward, or you are a Christian.*
*Oh — if you do not kill a man, you live forever.*

Khalid ibn al-Walid was among those sent by the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) to demolish it, and when they arrived they tore it down.

The Conquest of Mecca took place with ten nights remaining in the month of Ramadan, in the eighth year of the Hijrah.


<!-- page 392 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) stayed in Makkah for fifteen nights.

**The Day of Hunayn after the Conquest — Eighth Year of the Hijra**

When the Hawazin tribes heard what had happened to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family — and what Allah had granted him in the conquest of Makkah — Malik ibn 'Awf al-Nasri rallied them. He gathered together the Hawazin, all of Thaqif, all of Nasr and Jushm, Sa'd ibn Bakr, and a few from Banu Hilal. Of all the clans of Qays 'Aylan, only these few took part; the rest were absent. The Hawazin brought with them Ka'b and Kilab, though none of those clans distinguished themselves by name. Among Banu Jushm was a great elder, Duraid ibn al-Sammah,

[Pg 243]

a man whose only remaining value was his judgment and experience in warfare; he was carried in a litter. Among Banu Thaqif there were Qarib ibn al-Aswad ibn Mas'ud ibn Mu'athib, and among Banu Malik there were Dhu al-Khimar Subay' ibn al-Harith ibn Malik and his brother Ahmar ibn al-Harith. Overall command of the people fell to Malik ibn 'Awf al-Nasri.

When Malik resolved to march against the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, he led the people out along with their wealth, their wives, and their children. When he descended upon Awtas, the people gathered around him, including Duraid ibn al-Sammah, who was carried in his litter to that place. Duraid asked: "In what valley are you camped?" They said: "In Awtas." He said: "Good terrain for horses — neither rocky enough to injure hooves nor so soft as to swallow them. But what is this I do not hear? No lowing of camels, no braying of

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography.
(3) See: Dictionary of Countries, 1/405.

<!-- page 393 -->
donkeys, no crying of children, no wailing of women?" They told him: Malik ibn 'Awf had led the people out with their wealth, their wives, and their children.

He said: "Bring him to me." When Malik came before him, Duraid said: "O Malik, you have become the leader of your people, and this day will have days after it. Why do I hear no lowing of camels, no braying of donkeys, no crying of children, no wailing of women?" Malik replied: "I brought the people along with their wealth, their children, and their wives." Duraid said: "And why is that?" Malik said: "I wanted every man to feel that his family and his wealth were behind him, so that he would fight to defend them."

Duraid said: "By God, you think like a shepherd of sheep! Does a routed man ever save anything? In the end, nothing helps a man but his own sword and spear. If the battle goes against you, you will have exposed your family and your wealth."

[182/f]

Then he asked: "And what of the Kilab and Ka'b — did they witness this gathering?" They said: "None of them came." He said: "The men of resolve and seriousness were absent. Had this been the day of al-'Ala' and Rifa'a, neither Kilab nor Ka'b would have stayed away. Who from Amir's people is with you?" They said: "'Amr ibn 'Amir and 'Awf ibn 'Amir." He said: "Those two branches of Amir will neither help nor harm. O Malik, you have accomplished nothing by bringing the women and wealth before the horses — by 'egg' he meant property and women. Send them back to the highlands and put the fighting men on horseback. If you win, what lies behind you is yours; if you lose, at least you have preserved your family and your wealth."

Malik said: "By God, I will not do that. You have grown old and your mind has weakened with your age."

[Pg 244]

<!-- page 394 -->
"Obey me, O assembly of Hawazin!" Malik called out. "Or I will fall upon this sword until it comes out through my back."

They disliked that Duraid ibn al-Sammah should have any say or influence in the matter, and so they said: "We obey you."

Duraid ibn al-Sammah said: "This is a day I have not witnessed before, and it has not stirred wonder in me."

He said: (1) (2)

*I wish it had a trunk*
*I would hide within it*
*I would lead and extinguish the impulse*
*It is like a crack*

Then Malik said to the people: "When you see them, break off the hilts of your swords, then charge them as one man."

**[The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) Borrows Arms and Weapons from Safwan]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was preparing to march against Hawazin, he was reminded that Safwan ibn Umayyah possessed shields and weapons. He sent a messenger (3) to Safwan ibn Umayyah, who was at that time still a polytheist. He said to him: "O Safwan, lend us what you have of weapons so that we may meet our enemy tomorrow." (4)

(1) An addition from "B".
(2) See: Diwan, 128.
(3) An addition from the Prophet's biography, 4/440.
(4) An addition from the Prophet's biography, 4/440.

<!-- page 395 -->
Safwan said: "O Muhammad, is this by compulsion?" The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "It is a guaranteed loan, to be returned to you in full." Safwan said: "In that case, there is no objection." It is said that the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) borrowed from him a hundred shields, along with the weapons and equipment to carry them.

**[The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) Marches with His Army against Hawazin]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) received word of Malik ibn 'Awf's movements,

[Pg 245]

he departed Makkah on the twelfth — with ten thousand men, plus two thousand from the people of Makkah and ten thousand of his companions who had marched with him to the conquest of Makkah.

**[A Poem by 'Abbas ibn Mirdas al-Sulami]**

As the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) marched with the Muslims, 'Abbas ibn Mirdas al-Sulami said: (1)

[Pg 183/f]

*From me, a letter of counsel, plainly set forth —*
*Carry it to Hawazin, to great and small alike.*
*His army fills the breadth of earth, its farthest corners:*
*I say the Messenger of God shall meet you in the morning.*
*And the Muslims are the garment of God — Ghassan among them —*
*In them I shield you whole, yet you have turned away.*
*And the Ajraban: Banu 'Abs and Dhubyan are with him,*
*And on his right, the support of Banu Asad,*
*In their vanguard, Aws and 'Uthman.*
*The earth near trembled beneath his awe.*

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography, 4/440.
(2) See: Diwan, 155.

<!-- page 396 -->
**[The Halt at Dhat Anwat]**

When the Muslims reached a place called Dhat Anwat — a tree — it was a tree the Quraysh had venerated in the time of ignorance. They would come to it every year, hang their weapons upon it, and slaughter animals beside it, staying near it for a day. Some of those who had recently embraced Islam called out to the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "O Messenger of Allah, make for us a Dhat Anwat as they have one."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Allah is greater! By the One in whose hand is the soul of Muhammad, you have said what the people of Moses said to Moses: 'Make for us a god as they have gods.' He said: 'You are a people who are ignorant'" [Al-A'raf: 138]. "It is the same pattern — you would follow the ways of those who came before you."

<!-- page 397 -->
**[The Battle of Hawazin on the Day of Hunayn and the Steadfastness of the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) descended into the valley of Hunayn, he moved through a wide, hollow valley of the Tihama region in the darkness before dawn. The Hawazin and those who had joined them had arrived before him and lay in ambush in the ravines, bends, and narrow passages of the valley. They had gathered, drawn up their ranks, and made ready. The Muslims, advancing through that valley in loose order, were suddenly overwhelmed and broke into flight as one man; the men turned and retreated, and no one could be blamed.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family turned to the right and called out: "Come to me! Where are you going? I am the Messenger of Allah. I am Muhammad ibn 'Abdullah."

The people fled in defeat. Some of the hypocrites revealed what lay in their hearts. Abu Sufyan said: "This is a rout that will not be stopped until it reaches the sea." And another cried aloud: "Is not the magic defeated today?"

**[Those Who Remained with the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and His Family]**

Five men of the Banu 'Abd al-Muttalib stood firm with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), sword in hand, shielding and striking for him;

(1) An addition from "B".
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/313–314.

<!-- page 398 -->
al-'Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib, who held the bridle of the Messenger of Allah's mule — and the Prophet was riding his white mule — Abu Sufyan ibn al-Harith ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib, al-Fadl ibn al-'Abbas, and al-'Abbas himself.

It was also said that Qatham was not among them, but rather Ja'far ibn

[Pg 184]

Abu Sufyan ibn al-Harith.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to al-'Abbas — who was a man with a powerful voice — "Call to the people and let them know where I am."

The people had been routed, as Allah the Almighty has described in His words: *Indeed, Allah has given you victory on many fields — and on the day of Hunayn, when your great numbers pleased you but availed you nothing, and the earth, vast as it is, seemed to close in upon you; then you turned back in flight. Then Allah sent down His tranquility upon His Messenger and upon the believers* [Al-Tawbah: 25–26] — meaning those who remained steadfast with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) when the people fled.

**[Shayba's Failed Attempt to Kill the Prophet during the Battle]**

Shayba ibn 'Uthman ibn Abi Talha had resolved to kill the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him).

[Pg 247]

He said: "Today I shall avenge my father." His father had been killed on the day of Uhud. He said: "I drew close to the Messenger of Allah, but my heart was overwhelmed — it was as though something closed over me and held me back."

(1) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/313.
(2) An addition from "B".
(3) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/313.

<!-- page 399 -->
**['Ali (peace be upon him) Strikes Down the Enemy Standard-Bearer]**

'Ali ibn Abi Talib looked and saw the man carrying the polytheists' standard — mounted on a camel, banner in hand — driving into the Muslims and pressing them hard as they fell back before Hawazin, with the enemy behind him. 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) charged him, struck the camel's leg with his sword,

[Pg 184]

felled the beast, and split the man's head open. The fallen camel became a barrier between the Muslims and the polytheists.

**[The Return of the Fleeing Men at al-'Abbas's Call, and Victory after Defeat]**

Al-'Abbas called out at the top of his voice: "O assembly of Muslims! O assembly of the Muhajirun! O assembly of the Ansar! O people of the Tree! O people of the Pledge of al-Ridwan — come to your Prophet!"

From every direction came the reply: "Labbaik! Labbaik!" Those who had fled were already beginning to realize that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had not been killed and had not abandoned those who had turned back. Each man who wished to wheel his horse or camel around found he could not do so in the press and crush of the crowd, so he would take his weapons, cast himself from his mount, and make his way on foot to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family.

When the polytheist standard-bearer was struck down, the enemy could no longer hold their position.

(1) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/314.
(2) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/314.
(3) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/314.

<!-- page 400 -->
Their formation collapsed, their order dissolved, and their affairs fell into disarray. Allah Almighty struck them in their faces and supported His Messenger (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) with a humiliation of the enemy the like of which had not been seen before, just as He has described in His Book. The last of those who had fled did not return until the captives were already sitting in the hands of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, and the spoils had been collected and were present.

'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) had on that day of tribulation (1) a role that no one else matched. When the Ansar returned to the Messenger of Allah

[Pg 185/f]

they stood well; and the Muhajirun were patient. The Messenger of Allah oversaw the men from his mount, watching as they fought, and said: "Now the furnace burns hot."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) turned to Abu Sufyan ibn al-Harith ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib during the rout, while he was holding the Prophet's mule, and asked: "Who is this?" He said: "I am your cousin, O Messenger of Allah." And he held firm at that hour, and his Islam proved good when he had embraced it.

[Pg 248]

**[Umm Sulaym bint Milhan]**

He saw Umm Sulaym bint Milhan. She was with her husband Abu Talha, standing firm in the midst of the battle. She was pregnant with 'Abdullah ibn Abi Talhah, and Abu Talha's mount was with her. She was afraid the camel might throw her off, so she gripped its head, plunged her hand into the halter near the stern, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) spoke to her:

(1) An addition from Sharh al-Akhbar, 1/314.

<!-- page 401 -->
She said: "Yes, by my father and mother, O Messenger of Allah — kill those who flee from you, as you kill those who fight you, for they deserve it."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "O mother, Allah is sufficient." It is said she had a dagger with her. Abu Talha asked her: "What is that dagger for, O Umm Sulaym?" She said: "I took it so that if any polytheist came near me, I would plunge it into him." Abu

[Pg 185/z]

Talha said: "Do you not hear, O Messenger of Allah, what Umm Sulaym al-Rumaysa' is saying?" — "Rumaysa'" referring to dirt that collects in the corner of the eye.

**[The Poetry of Malik ibn 'Awf upon the Defeat of His People]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was advancing toward Hunayn, (1) Banu Sulaym joined him under al-Dahhak ibn Sufyan al-Kilabi, and they were with him. When the people were routed, Malik ibn 'Awf said, struck by what had befallen him: (2)

*The oldest proof is that this was a day of denial —*
*a man like me, a man like you: shield and shield.*
*If the company loses one day, and the burden falls,*

[Pg 249]

*Then I drove back ranks upon ranks.*
*Battalions in full sight of eye —*
*I may yet drive the thrust to its resounding mark.*

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography, 4/447.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/447.


<!-- page 402 -->
When the poor outlast the prosperous,
And the nobles howl and flee from the thrust of the spear,
A falling machine gun sweeps from the flanks,
Tarat screams while we erupt forward,
And the fox working within it is broken.
O Zayd, son of their concern — where do you flee?
The tooth has worn down and the age has grown long;
The white banners have yielded to the long red lances.
I have known nothing like her except immersion,
When the Guards emerge from beneath the shadow.

[News from Abu Qatadah]

Abu Qatadah al-Ansari said: On the day of Hunayn, I saw two men fighting — one a Muslim and the other a polytheist — and a man from among the polytheists was trying to get his polytheist companion the upper hand over the Muslim. So I went at him, struck his hand, and cut it off. He then seized me with his other hand and held me so tightly that I feared he would kill me, but the bleeding weakened him until he fell, and I struck him and killed him.

[Defeat of the polytheists]

When Allah defeated the polytheists and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was able to

<!-- page 403 -->
I swear by God, the plant deserves it —
I have been absent from God's horses, the horses of Allat.

When Hawazin was defeated, Thaqif suffered losses at Banu Malik, with seventy men killed under their banner, among them 'Uthman ibn 'Abd-Allah ibn Rabi'ah ibn al-Harith ibn Habib. Their banner had been carried by Dhu al-Khimar, and when he was killed, 'Uthman ibn 'Abd-Allah took it up and fought under it until he too was killed. When he fell, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "May God keep him away — he hated the Quraysh."

One of the Muslim women remarked on this.

The banner of the allied forces was held by the boat of Ibn al-Aswad. When the people were routed, he propped it against a tree, and then he, his cousins, and his kinsmen fled from the alliance. Of the allied forces, only two men were killed: a man from Jealousy, known as Wahb, and another from Banu Kubbah, known as al-Jalah. When word reached the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that al-Jalah had been killed, he said: "Today the master of the youth of Thaqif was killed, [186/z] except for the son of Hneida" — meaning Ibn Hunaida, al-Harith ibn Uwais.

[The killing of Duraid ibn al-Samma]

When the polytheists were routed, they fell back to al-Ta'if with Malik ibn Auf. Some fled in haste while others headed toward a valley; those who made for the valley were none but Banu Jalah of Thaqif. The cavalry of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) pursued them.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet (PBUH) 449/4.

<!-- page 404 -->
He pressed through the palm groves among the people and did not follow the well-worn path of the passes.

Rabi'ah ibn Rafi' al-Sulami caught up with Duraid ibn al-Samma, taking hold of the halter of his camel. He mistook Duraid for a woman, since Duraid was riding in a litter, and Rabi'ah intended to take him captive. But the man was an aged elder, and Rabi'ah did not recognize him.

Duraid said to him, "What do you want, young man?" He replied, "I will kill you." Duraid said, "And who are you?" He said, "I am Rabi'ah ibn Rafi' al-Sulami." He struck him with his sword, but it did nothing. Duraid told him, "Your mother has given you a poor weapon! Take that sword from behind the litter, strike with it, raise it above the bones, and bring it down on the brain — for that is how I used to strike men. But if you have lost your wits, then go back to your mother and tell her you have killed Duraid ibn al-Samma, and there will come a day when you will have kept your women from harm."

When Duraid fell and was exposed, his thighs were like parchment from a lifetime in the saddle.

[Ara'a] (1)

When Rabi'ah returned to his mother and told her he had killed Duraid, she said, "By Allah, he freed three of your mothers."

[251] / [Killing of Abu Amir al-Ash'ari]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent, in pursuit of those who had fled toward Awtaas, Abu 'Amir al-Ash'ari. He caught up with some of the defeated fighters and they battled him. Abu Amir was struck by an arrow and killed. Abu Musa al-Ash'ari then took up the banner and fought them, and they feared he would kill the Banu Nasr from Banu Riab.

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 453/4.

<!-- page 405 -->
[The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) forbade the killing of the vulnerable]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) passed by a woman who had been killed by Khalid ibn al-Walid, and a crowd had gathered around her. He said, "Who killed this?" They said: Khalid ibn al-Walid. He sent word to Khalid through one of those with him, saying: "The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) forbids the killing of a child, a woman, or a hired laborer." And "al-Asif" means the hired man.

Al-Zubayr ibn al-'Awwam fought fiercely that day. Malik ibn 'Awf positioned himself and those with him at a mountain pass to cover the retreat of their vulnerable so that (1) they might get away safely.

[Concerning Bajad and Shayma']

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said regarding Bajad: "If you are able to find him, do not let him escape you." Bajad was a man from Banu Sa'd ibn Bakr. The Muslims captured him along with his family, and with them was al-Shayma' bint al-Harith ibn 'Abd al-'Ari, the foster sister of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family). Those who captured her treated her roughly during the march, but she said: "I am your companion's foster sister." They did not believe her, and when she was brought before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), she said: "I am your sister from breastfeeding." He said: "And what is the sign of that?" She said: "A bite you gave me on my back when I was carrying you." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)

(1) "B": "To prevent."

<!-- page 406 -->
[252] spread out his robe for her, seated her upon it, [and treated her with kindness] (1) and said: "If you wish, you may stay with me in honor and love, [and I shall hold you dear,] (2) or if you wish, I will provide for you and return you to your people." She said: "[Rather,] (3) provide for me and return me to my people." (4) And she chose that. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) returned her to her people.

[The names of the martyrs on the day of Hunayn from among the Muslims]

From the Quraysh:

[Ayman ibn Ubayd.] (5)
Yazid ibn Zama'ah ibn al-Aswad ibn al-Muttalib ibn Asad ibn 'Abd al-'Uzza — he had a mare called "The Wing," and he was killed by her.

From the Ansar:

Saraqa ibn al-Harith [ibn 'Adi, from Banu al-'Ajlan.] (6)

From the Ash'aris:

Abu 'Amir al-Ash'ari.

(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography 458/4.
(3) An addition from the Prophet's biography 458/4.
(4) An addition from "B."
(5) An addition from the Prophet's biography.

<!-- page 407 -->
[The gathering of the captives of Hunayn]

The captives of Hunayn were brought together before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). He ordered that the captives and the spoils (1) be held at al-Ji'ranah, and they were kept there.

[The poetry of al-Abbas ibn Mardas in satirizing the boat of Ibn al-Aswad and his people]

Al-'Abbas ibn Mardas al-Sulami spoke verses recalling the boat of Ibn al-Aswad and his flight on the day of Hunayn, and al-Khimar — and the imprisonment of his people that led to their ruin (3):

Except for the measure of Ghaylan from me, for I am the one with knowledge.

[Pg 253]
[Pg 188/f]

And a saying other than what you say is plausible,
And a reply — yet I give an answer.
By God, who neither strays nor leads astray,
Muhammad is the slave and the Messenger.
Every youth calls on him for good,
We found him a prophet like Moses.
Glory when things are apportioned,
And the matter is made clear by the command of Banu Fassi.
A prince, and the circles may turn —
They lost their standing, and to every people
The soldiers of God are a suburb on the march.
So we found for them a lion of the forest.
We were right — we almost took flight —
The gathering of Banu Fassi, the gathering of Banu Fassi.
They were joined by soldiers and did not hold their ground.
I swear, if they had not been killed before us,

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 459/4.
(2) An addition from the Prophet's biography 459/4.
(3) See: Diwan, 68–71.

<!-- page 408 -->
We would have embraced it, and the victories were handed over.
So why were we lions — and then made even?
He rode off, and the blood in it was like dates,
And the day before I had that longing.
No male people have heard the like of it —
You have not heard of it for a day.
On its banners the horses were forged;
We said in the dust: Banu Hitit —
They have a mind that punishes, or cunning.
And Dhu al-Khimar was no leader of a people;
Things became plain to whoever beheld them.
He drove them according to the way of the Munaya,
And many of them were slain.
So I said: those who survived among them were wounded,
Or shut within the narrow confines, creaking.
It does not befit matters to be kin to delay —
[188] They held sway and ruled their affairs, and the falcons escaped.
I revile her — the peacocks and the barley —
And Abu Auf stands apart from them.
I divided the houses and palaces:
If it were not for the boat and its sons,
On Yemen, referred to by the Field Marshal,
But the presidency spread wide,
And dreams of glory grew,
They obeyed a settled chieftain though they had grandfathers —
The noses of the people were not tanned by the samir.
In God's war they have no supporter;
The Banu Ghazia fled in every direction.
As the Banu Sa'd ruled and turned,
To Islam is a deception —
It is as though Banu Mu'awiyah ibn Bakr
Had been absolved of the tenderness of the breast.
Awaken us — surrender, I am your protector —

[Pg 254]
From some after the ladder.
It is as though the people, when they came to us,
If they embrace Islam, they will turn away,
And if they do not surrender, the call to prayer is made.

<!-- page 409 -->
[Another poem by al-Abbas ibn Mardas on the day of Hunayn]

Al-'Abbas ibn Mardas al-Sulami also spoke on the day of Hunayn (1):

So I see you — the workshops have been dissolved.
Pardon a man from his family, and he is glad.
Rest, and spend the time at the neighborhood mosque —
A house of no — O lamb — for most of our lives.
Milk, the past of a living sustenance returning,
A grain touched by the alienation of the kernels.
If you seek the unbelievers, you are not to blame.
I am a minister of the Prophet, and he continued — [189/f]
Bundle and the bitterness of them, and wide.
We summoned the finest delegation to them,
A gift for them of the weave of David — wondrous.
So we are with a thousand of Sulaym upon them;
The hand of God between the two woods is pledged —
Pledged to the beloved, yet
With our swords and bright, fallen caps.
So well done, O Mahdi — Mecca taken by force,
Intimate and now soaked in the blood of the unbelievers.
Openly, and the horses are yielding their bodies to us,
Narrowed with the broken souls.
And a day of longing when Hawazin came —
The striking of the enemies among them, and the realities.
Our patience with laughter does not weary us;
A brilliant cloud, a thunderous brigade,
Before the Messenger of God, who towers above us,
With the sword of the Messenger of God — and death is like it —
On the eve of the laugh of Ibn Sufyan Mu'attab.
We were the closest to following.
Defend us from our brother, even if we see —
We accepted it with guidance and laws.
But the religion of God is the religion of Muhammad,
And God's command has no hindrance.
He abided in it after the prayer and commanded us.

(1) See: Diwan, 107–109.

<!-- page 410 -->
[Poetry of Bujayr ibn Zuhayr on the day of Hunayn]

Buhayr ibn Zuhayr ibn Abi Salma said on the day of Hunayn (1):

When terror diminished every coward —
The lad is God's, and His slave, and orphaned —
On the day of love for our peers,
And swimmers pouring forth to the ears,
And distilled with your fingers and gum,
From the shape of his garment in his palm.

[Pg 189]

And He honored us through worship of the Most Merciful.
God honored us and made our religion plain,
And humiliated them through worship of Satan.
I swear by God, He destroyed them and scattered their gathering.

[Ibn Hisham said: Some narrators reported the following:] (2)
When the uncle of your Prophet and his guardian stood and called: "O battalion of faith! Where are those who answered their God on the day of al-Ara'id and al-Ridwan?"

[Malik ibn 'Awf's poetry apologizing for his flight]

[Pg 255]
Malik ibn 'Awf said, excusing his flight:

Yes, along the stretches of the road,
Preventing sleep — I do not close an eye for an hour.
Did Hawazin harm its enemy?
And the eyes of one who loves her — if he is penalized —
Two of them are Hasif and Blame,
And a battalion I clothed in armor.

(1) See: Sira al-Nabawiyyah 459/4–460; and verses [1–5] attributed to al-'Abbas ibn Mardas, see: ibid., 159.
(2) See: Addition from the Prophet's biography 460/4.

<!-- page 411 -->
I presented it, and the witnesses of the nation know better,
And the forerunner of the affliction of souls at his distress —
They want to flood him and drown him in blood.
I forgave him and left our brothers to his account.
The glory of life — and no sheep is divided.
If his floods recede, he will reveal himself to me,
And God knows best who held back and who wronged.
You cost me the destruction of the family of Muhammad,
And you abandoned me as you fight,
And you abandoned me when I fight — alone.
One and another are not equal. [190/f]
In the world he grows for the highest,
And the winter hastens.
Sahma was presented with the spear of Saljam —
I despised it as a machine of fornication.
And she says: not so-and-so,
And I let his place reply — and why?
Like the durry — impossible and shameful —
And I braced myself for the weight of the spears.

[Poetry of Hawazin on the Islam of their people]

[Pg 256]

And if the House of the boundary destroys one another —

It is said regarding Hawazin that they also recounted their journey to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family — with Malik ibn Auf after his embrace of Islam (1):

And your wealth above them — the banners fluttering —
I remember their path to the people when they gathered.
A day of longing — the crown shines,
And Malik, Malik — none surpassed him.
They had strength, bodies, and resolve.
Until they met hardship when hardship met them —
Before the Prophet, until the dusk of paradise.
So they scattered the people until none could be seen;
From the heavens — defeated and embraced.
Then Jibril descended with their victory.

(1) See: Sira al-Nabawiyyah 475/4, attributed to Madrak ibn al-Harith ibn Habib ibn Dahman, (8 verses). See: al-Isabah (translation no. 6427).

From us — and even if it were not Jibril fighting beside us —

With a thrust, or even the saddle of leeches,
'Umar al-Faruq saw them broken and defeated.

Then came the Battle of Ta'if:

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) marched to Ta'if after returning from Hunayn. Thaqif, upon the arrival of the Prophet at Ta'if, shut the city gates against him and prepared their fortifications for battle.

[Poetry of Ka'b ibn Malik]

Ka'b ibn Malik composed the following verses when the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) assembled his forces for the march to Ta'if:

We have swept all doubt from Tihamah,
and from Khaybar — then we drew our swords.
Choose your course; and if she were to speak,
she would say: step forward, or thrust.
In the courtyard of your house stand thousands of us,
making your home a place of departure.
Thrones are seized with courage and resolve,
and swift horses come to you.
Leaving behind a multitude in their wake,
she has what I have made her feel assured of.
The terminologists ring out with the hatufa;
India's fortunes did not strike Kativa.
Like the truths I have preserved as proverbs —
the day after the relief of Jad'a Medua.
I am no nurse if you do not see it:
when they descend into your space, you will hear —
[257] / in their hands, delicate squirrels.
Imagine the earnestness of heroes within it.

(1) See: Id. at 188–190.

<!-- page 413 -->
385
Battle of Hunayn and Ta'if

[Pg 191]

And a resolve that was no light twist,
wise and knowing, rightly guided.
Among the nations, we had a corporal —
do I find they have no counsel?
He told them that we had assembled,
mounted on horses, eager for battle,
and I brought them crawling forward,
surrounding the lined wall of their share.
Pure of heart and fragrant in abstinence,
their leader was Albi, who was crucified.
He was shown mercy toward us.
Obey us and obey God,
and make yourself a support and a refuge for us.
If they offer us peace, we will accept,
and we show no weakness or trembling.
But if you refuse, we will fight you with patience
until you submit to Islam.
We fight until we are slain or prophesy —
did we hope for the thalad or the spectrum?
We strive, caring not whom we encounter,
the core of the afflicted among them and the ally.
How many people turned away from us —
so we braved ears and noses.
They did not see them as capable,
yet he drives them with force,
with every supple, heavy Muhannad blade.
The religion is moderate and true,
the command of God and Islam supreme.
And rob her of necklaces and adornments,
and leave behind the exposure —
and whoever does not abstain will face eclipse.
So they accepted and were reassured.

[The poetry of Shaddad ibn 'Arid al-Jashmi on the march to Ta'if]

Shaddad ibn 'Arid al-Jashmi said in commemoration of the journey of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family — to Ta'if:

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 481/4–482.

<!-- page 414 -->
386 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities - by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

How can he be aided who finds no victory,
and did not fight alongside her stones in vain?
They are not human beings from among her people —
do not support al-Lat; God will destroy it.
The one that burned in the dam caught fire —
when will the Prophet come down to your land?

[Pg 258] [191/p.m] / [The Road to Ta'if]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) marched along the Palm of Yemen, then along a pass, then along the salt flats, then across the sea of foam from Layyah, where he built a mosque — one that remains a seasonal landmark.

At that time, a man from Banu Layth who had killed a man from Hudhayl was brought before him — and this was the first blood case adjudicated in Islam.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered the fortress of Malik ibn Auf to be demolished, and the march continued until the army descended near a Sidra tree. It is said that it was forbidden to approach the property of a man who was close to the wealth of a man of Thaqif, and so the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent word to him: either surrender, or we will tear down your wall. He refused to accept Islam, and so the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered his wall to be destroyed.

[Fighting begins at the Ta'if wall]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then advanced until he camped near Ta'if. He positioned his army there, and men accompanying him fell, because Thaqif's soldiers descended from the wall of Ta'if and shot arrows that reached them. When the fallen were among them, he moved his army to where his mosque in Ta'if stands today.

<!-- page 415 -->
387
Battle of Hunayn and Ta'if

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) besieged them for twenty nights — some say seventeen.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had two of his wives with him. One of them [192/f], Umm Salamah, was sheltered between two tent poles, and he prayed between the two intervals. When Thaqif later embraced Islam, they built a mosque over the prayer site of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family — [the mosque of] 'Amr ibn Ummah ibn Wahb ibn Mu'tab ibn Malik.(1)

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) fought fierce battles against them throughout the days of the siege, and they found themselves worn down by it.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered a catapult to be constructed and stones were hurled at the people of Ta'if. When the stones began to pile up and break through part of the wall, a group of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) crept beneath a protective tank and pushed it toward the wall of Ta'if in order to breach it. Thaqif responded by hurling upon them an iron beam wrapped in fire. The men scrambled out from beneath the tank, and Thaqif killed a number of them. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then commanded — alongside his forces — that the grape vineyards of Thaqif be cut down, and the Muslims carried this out.

[The withdrawal of the Muslims and the reason for it]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said, while the siege was still heavy upon him: "Permission has not been granted to conquer Ta'if." When this became known to the Muslims, they questioned him about it, and he confirmed it. They said: "O Messenger of Allah, then give the order to withdraw." And so the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) commanded

[Pg 259]

(1) An addition from the biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 416 -->
388 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities - by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

the herald to call it out, and the call for withdrawal was made.

[The remark of 'Uyaynah and what lay behind it]

'Uyaynah ibn Husn al-Fazari said: Thaqif is a glorious and honorable people. Some Muslims said to him [192/z]: "You praise the polytheists, yet you came to support the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family!" He replied: "I did not come to fight Thaqif alongside you. What I wanted was for Muhammad to open Ta'if, so that Thaqif might capture a slave girl [whom I would acquire], that she might bear me a son — for they are a people of noble stock."

[The liberation of the enslaved]

While the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family were besieging Ta'if, some enslaved people came down to him, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) freed them.

[The private counsel of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) with 'Ali (peace be upon him)]

Allah and His Messenger" — until Abu Bakr and 'Umar said: "O Messenger of Allah, you have prolonged [since](2) today your private counsel with 'Ali." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "It was not I who chose him — it was God who chose him."

All the people came to know the distinction that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and [260] his God had accorded to his guardian 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), drawing him close above all other Companions — such that none among them had been granted his favor during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah.

In the isnad of Abu Ghassan, from 'Ali (peace be upon him), he said:(3)

"When Allah (Exalted be He) revealed: 'O you who believe, when you consult the Messenger in private, offer charity before your consultation' [al-Mujadilah: 12] — [193/f] 'Ali (peace be upon him) said: I possessed a dinar, which I exchanged for ten dirhams. Whenever I wished to consult the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, I would give a dirham in charity(4) before doing so, until it was spent. No other Muslim did this. Then Allah (Exalted be He) revealed: 'Are you fearful of having to give charity before your consultation? Then when you do not, and Allah has pardoned you' [al-Mujadilah: 12] — to the end of the verse. No one fulfilled the verse of al-Najwa other than me."

This is agreed upon among Muslims, and both al-Zamakhshari and al-Tha'labi mentioned in their commentary on this verse that no one among the Muslims acted upon the verse of al-Najwa except 'Ali ibn Abi

(1) Addition from the explanation of the news.
(2) Addition from the explanation of the news.
(3) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 828/2.
(4) Explanation of the news: "I gave charity in dirhams."

<!-- page 418 -->
390 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities - by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

Talib (peace be upon him).

His virtues are many and well-known, and those who deny his virtue — "associated with it"(1) — have rejected most of them. Neither Abu Bakr, nor 'Umar, nor any other Companion is cited in connection with the verse of al-Najwa, nor is there any mention of them in it.

'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) said:

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) placed his mouth to my ear and opened to me a thousand doors of knowledge, and from each of those doors a thousand more doors were opened to me.

None of the Companions received anything like this. Yet Abu Bakr said: "I am your guardian, and I am not the best among you." And among you is the one who is "he"(3) whose horizon is mine.

'Umar said: "Do not exaggerate [193/z] in the dowries of women — the most the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) ever gave to any of his wives was ten ounces."

[Pg 261]

A woman who was not among the most learned stood up and said to him: "O Commander of the Faithful, have you not prevented us from a right that Allah has obligated for us? He said: 'And if you wish to take one wife in place of another, and you have given one of them a heap of gold, take nothing from it' [al-Nisa': 20]" — to the end of the verse. 'Umar ibn al-Khattab felt ashamed and turned to those around him, saying: "You hear such a thing from me and do not object — until a woman who knows little corrects me?"

(1) An addition from "B".
(2) See: Basa'ir al-Dara'at 324; al-Khisal 643–648; biography of Imam 'Ali by Ibn 'Asakir 483/2–485; Fara'id al-Samtayn 101/1; al-Tara'if by Ibn Tawus 518.
(3) An addition from "B".
(4) See: Tabaqat Ibn Sa'd 8/161; Da'a'im al-Islam 85/1.

Women.

Many such instances could be cited had we not already exceeded what we set out to recount — but we have mentioned them here in connection with the private counsel of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) with his cousin, and his distinction above others. The denial of those who deny this amounts to a challenge to the Prophet, and they dare to prefer over him one who belongs to the people of preference.

[Muslim martyrs on the day of Ta'if]

Those who were martyred at Ta'if numbered twelve Muslims: seven from Quraysh, four from the Ansar, and one from Banu Layth.

[The poetry of Bujayr in Hunayn and Ta'if]

After the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) departed from Ta'if following the fighting and siege, Bujayr ibn Zuhayr composed verses recalling Hunayn and Ta'if:(1) [194/f]

Tomorrow — Awtaas, and the day of lightning —
it was a wound in the heart of Hunayn.
Gathered by the seduction of Hawazin, they were gathered,
then scattered like a torn bird.
Except their wall and the belly of the trench —
they did not hold us back from a single position.
So they fortified themselves behind a closed door,
and we were left to draw them out.
You may divide leadership and meet —
the screaming moved across the harass as though I
see Hasran bounding back to Rahraja.
Shahba' shines with the hopes of the Legion —
we embraced a shadow as though it had never been created,
green Lumma, when they hurl it.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 487/4–488.

<!-- page 420 -->
392 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Archaeology - by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

Like the prohibition — the smell of the taraq drifts —
in every vanguard, should you fail to be caught.
Hadal touches their longing, our sandals —

[262] [The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, regarding Hawazin]

From the weaving of David and the family of Muharraq.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) departed from Ta'if, and it was said to him: "Pray against Thaqif, O Messenger of Allah." He said: "O Allah, guide Thaqif and bring them to us."

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) reached al-Ji'ranah, the people of Hawazin came to him and asked him to return their wives and children. They said: "O Messenger of Allah, among the captives are your aunts, your maternal relations, and the women who nursed and raised you. Even if we were like al-Harith ibn Abi Shimr [194/z] or al-Nu'man ibn al-Mundhir, and he then came down to us with what he had received, we would beg for his generosity — and you are the best of those who are generous. We have come to you as Muslims."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Which is dearer to you — your wealth, or your women and children?"

They replied: "O Messenger of Allah, you have given us a choice between our wealth and our lineage. Our wives and our children are dearer to us."

Hawazin had been taken as captives — six thousand in all, comprising women, camels, and sheep beyond counting.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "As for what belongs to me and to the family of 'Abd al-Muttalib, it is yours. And when I lead the people in the Dhuhr prayer, stand and say: We are


<!-- page 421 -->
The people stood and said what he had commanded them. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Whatever belonged to me and to the sons of Abd al-Muttalib is yours." The Muhajiroon and the Ansar said: "Whatever belonged to us is for the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family." Al-Aqra' ibn Habis said: "As for me and Banu Tamim, no." Uyaynah ibn Husn said: "As for me and Banu Fazarah, no." Abbas ibn Mardas said — [195/f] — "As for me and Banu Sulaym, no." But the Banu Sulaym said: "Yes — whatever was ours belongs to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family." At that, Abbas ibn Mardas said to his own people of Banu Sulaym: "And you are content with this?"

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Whoever holds fast to his share of these captives shall receive six shares from the first spoils I acquire as compensation." The people had no wives or children among the captives except Uyaynah ibn Husn, who [263] had captured an old woman. He said: "I count her of noble lineage in her clan, and perhaps a great ransom will come for her." Abu Surad Zuhayr ibn Surad said to him: "Give her up — by Allah, her breast gives no milk, her belly bears no child, her husband is gone, and her youth is spent." So he returned her in exchange for six shares of compensation, once Zuhayr had said what he said. It is claimed that Uyaynah later regretted it.

(1) This is additional detail from the biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 422 -->
Al-Aqra' ibn Habis complained to the Prophet about this and said: "By Allah, you have not given to a man of standing, nor to one of influence."

**[The Islam of Malik ibn Awf al-Nasri]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Tell Malik that if he comes to me as a Muslim, his family and his wealth will be returned to him, and I will give him a hundred camels." Malik did so and came out from [195/z] Ta'if. He had feared that the people of Ta'if would learn of this and detain him, so he slipped away under cover of night. He then came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and met him at al-Ji'ranah or Makkah, and there he submitted to Islam. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) restored his family and his wealth to him and gave him a hundred camels.

Malik ibn Awf said, upon becoming Muslim: (1)

Who among all people is like Muhammad?
Never have I seen or heard of anyone like him.
When you seek to know what tomorrow will bring,
He delivers and gives to the lowly when imitated.
With the polished spear he strikes every keen blade,
And if scribes were to count their ranks,
In the midst of confusion, he stands watch like a sentinel —
As though he stands guard over his cubs.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then appointed Malik over those of his people who had accepted Islam, from the surrounding tribes. These tribes had caused persistent trouble, and he fought them hard, never ceasing to raid them until they were subdued. Abu Muhajin ibn Habib ibn Amr

(1) See: Sirat al-Nabawiyyah 4/491; al-Maghazi 3/956.

<!-- page 423 -->
ibn Umayr al-Thaqafi (1) said:

Then Banu Salamah brought us comfort —
the enemies press in on every side.
They broke their covenant and violated what was sacred,
yet I have no concern for them.
We were the first to rebuke them,
and still they came against our homes.

[Pg 264]

**[The Division of the Spoils]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) finished returning the captives [196/f], he rode on. The people called out: "O Messenger of Allah, divide among us the spoils — the camels and the sheep!" They pressed him so urgently that his robe was pulled from him as he was backed against a tree. He said: "Return my robe to me, O people! By Allah, if you had as many camels as the trees of Tihamah, I would divide them all among you, and you would not find me miserly, cowardly, or a liar." He then went to a camel, took a tuft of hair from its hump, held it up between his fingers and said: "O people, by Allah, I have nothing of your spoils — not even this thread — that is not returned to you. So hand over even the needle and thread, for those who steal from the spoils will carry their shame before their families on the Day of Resurrection."

It is related that Aqeel ibn Abi Talib came home on the day of Hunayn to his wife Fatimah bint Shaybah ibn Rabi'ah, his sword still stained with blood. She said: "I know you fought bravely — so what did you bring from the spoils of the polytheists?" He said: "I have nothing but this needle." Then he heard the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) proclaim that whoever had taken anything must return it, and that even a needle and thread must be given back. Aqeel said to his wife:

(1) See: Sirat al-Nabawiyyah 4/491–492; al-Maghazi 3/955–956.
(2) See: Sirat al-Nabawiyyah 4/492.

<!-- page 424 -->
"I think you have lost your needle." He then took it from her and went and threw it back into the spoils.

**[Giving to Those Whose Hearts Were to Be Won]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) gave generously to those whose hearts were to be won over — people who had submitted to Islam out of worldly desire and ambition. By winning them over with gifts, he hoped to draw them closer to Islam. He gave Abu Sufyan [ibn Harb] (1) one hundred camels, and he gave his son Mu'awiyah one hundred camels as well. Hakim ibn Hizam received one hundred camels, (2) and al-Harith [ibn al-Harith] (3) ibn Kaldah's son received one hundred camels. A man from Banu Abd al-Dar also received one hundred camels; it was said he was Nusayr ibn al-Harith ibn Kaldah.

Al-Harith ibn Hisham received one hundred camels, Suhayl ibn Amr received one hundred camels, Huwaytib ibn Abd al-Uzza received one hundred camels, and al-Ala ibn Jariyah al-Thaqafi — [265] an ally of Banu Zahra — received one hundred camels. Uyaynah ibn Husn ibn Hudhayfah ibn Badr received one hundred camels, al-Aqra ibn Habis al-Tamimi received one hundred camels, Malik ibn Awf al-Nasri received one hundred camels, and Safwan ibn Umayyah received one hundred camels. Men from the Quraysh below that rank received less than one hundred.

**[Abbas ibn Mardas's Complaint at Receiving Less Than a Hundred]**

Abbas ibn Mardas received less than a hundred camels and was aggrieved by this. He reproached
