(2) Additional detail from the biography of the Prophet.
(3) Additional detail from the biography of the Prophet.

<!-- page 425 -->
the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): (1)

[0/197]

Early in the morning at al-Jarra,
she was one who plundered three.
If the people settle down, I shall not settle —
my salvation is that the people sleep.
Between Uyaynah and al-Aqra
I have grown weary while my game was plundered.
I was given nothing, nor was I withheld —
yet I was present in the war that was turned aside,
among its many raids.
Except that she was given —
they have surpassed my sheikh in the assembly,
though he was neither a stronghold nor a prisoner.
Whoever is diminished today will not rise again,
and I was not without my place among them.

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Go to him and silence his tongue with gifts." So they gave him until he was satisfied.

**[The Prophet's Reply About Why Ja'il Was Not Given]**

It was said (3) to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "Al-Aqra ibn Habis was given one hundred camels, and Uyaynah ibn Husn was given one hundred camels, while Ja'il ibn Suraqah al-Damri was passed over." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "By Him in whose hand is Muhammad's soul, Ja'il ibn Suraqah is better than the whole earth filled with the likes of Uyaynah ibn Husn and al-Aqra ibn Habis. But I gave to those two in order to draw them toward surrender, while I entrusted Ja'il ibn Suraqah to his Islam."

(1) See: op. cit., 111–112.
(2) Additional detail from the biography of the Prophet.
(3) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 1/317; Ansab al-Ashraf 4/18; Sirat al-Nabawiyyah 4/496.

<!-- page 426 -->
[266] **[The Objection of Dhu al-Khuwaysirah al-Tamimi]**

(1) Dhu al-Khuwaysirah — who was from Banu Tamim — came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) while he was distributing the spoils among the people, and said: "O Muhammad, you have not been just in what you have divided." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) grew angry and said: [197/z] "Woe to you — if I am not just, then who is?" (2)

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then said: "From his followers will come a group who immerse themselves so deeply in religion that they pass right through it, just as an arrow passes clean through its target — nothing remains on the arrowhead, nothing on the shaft, nothing on the fletching. They will have gone beyond even the blood."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: (3) "They are the worst of creation, and the best of creation will be those who kill them." And those are the Khawarij who rose up against the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), and whom he killed at Nahrawan.

**[Hassan ibn Thabit's Poem on the Ansar Being Passed Over]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) gave generously to those he gave —

(1) See: Sirat al-Nabawiyyah 4/496.

(3) This addition is required by the context.

<!-- page 427 -->
the Quraysh and the Arab tribes — while the Ansar received nothing, Hassan ibn Thabit composed verses reproaching this: (1)

[Pg 198]
[Pg 267]

For the graceful women who neither bowed nor sacrificed,
nor hastened for the sake of Allah and made their confession —
if she is a lesson fashioned from pearls,
full of grace, neither profane nor violated,
and you find a smile within her smile,
a glance and the worst of connections the visitor brings —
Leave Shama for you as her affection was
for the faithful, if there are no others,
when the prophet came and said: O finest of the trusted —
they gave up their homes and gave victory.
The banner is called Salim and May is uprooted —
those who bear the guidance through the raging storm of war.
Allah named them Helpers for their aid;
the worries of the eye's corner slope inward —
except for swords and the tips of the lances and a single button.
And the people crowd around you for what is not ours,
and do not let what the wall implies go to waste.
We deliver people and keep no one back,
and when its fire blazes, it is bought dearly —
and do not let the makers of war call us
the hope of the wakeful — among us the peg is driven home.
As we turned back Badr without their entering it,
if they fought their parties with a hero, it is of consequence.
And we were your soldiers on the day of the throttle at Uhud —
we stumbled, and all the people stumbled.
So what did we say, and what did we imagine, and what were we told?

**[The Ansar's Grief at Being Passed Over, and the Prophet's Comfort]**

The Ansar felt it keenly when the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) distributed the spoils among others and left them with nothing, until many of them were saying: "The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) has found his own people."

(1) See: Diwan Hassan ibn Thabit, 265.

<!-- page 428 -->
So (1) Sa'd ibn Ubadah al-Ansari went in to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "O Messenger of Allah, a number of the Ansar have taken it hard that you did not include them in what you divided among your people — the great gifts you bestowed on the tribes of the Arabs — while the Ansar received nothing of it."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to him: "And where do you stand in all of this, Sa'd?" He said: "O Messenger of Allah, I am only a man of my people." The Prophet said: "Gather your people for me in this enclosure."

When they had gathered, Sa'd came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "The Ansar have assembled." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) came to them, praised Allah and gave Him His due, then said:

"O community of the Ansar, what is this talk that has reached me from you — this grievance you feel toward me? Did I not find you astray and Allah guided you? Were you not poor and Allah enriched you? Were you not enemies to one another and Allah made your hearts one?" They said: "Yes — to Allah and His Messenger belong grace and bounty." Then he said: "Will you not answer me, O [268] community of the Ansar?" They said: "And what shall we say in answer to you, O Messenger of Allah? To Allah and His Messenger belong grace and credit." He said: "By Allah, if you wished, you could say — and you would be telling the truth, and you would be believed — 'You came to us rejected,

Battle of Hunayn and Ta'if

"We believed in you, we supported you, we struggled alongside you, and we brought you joy. You have appointed, O community of the Ansar, a portion of your kinship through which you have bound people to submit to you. I entrust you to your Islam. Are you not content that the people depart with worldly goods while you return with the Messenger of Allah? By Him in whose hand Muhammad's soul rests, had it not been for the Hijrah I would have been a man of the Ansar. If the people took one path and the Ansar took another, I would follow the path of the Ansar. O Allah, have mercy on the Ansar, the sons of the Ansar, and the sons of the sons of the Ansar."

[He said:](1) The Ansar wept until their beards were wet, and they said: "We are content with the Messenger of Allah as our lot and our fortune."(2) And they parted from him.

[The Prophet's 'Umrah from al-Ji'ranah]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) departed from al-Ji'ranah to perform 'Umrah. He ordered the remainder of the fay' to be held at Majannah, in the region of Marr al-Zahran. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had completed his 'Umrah, he set out for Madinah and appointed 'Attab ibn Usayd as his deputy over Mecca. Mu'adh ibn Jabal was left with him to teach the people the Quran and instruct them in religion. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) provided them with the remainder of the fay'. His 'Umrah was in Dhu al-Qa'dah, and he arrived in Madinah during the nights of Dhu al-Qa'dah. That year he led the people on the pilgrimage—

402 — *'Uyun al-Akhbar wa Funun al-Athar* — by 'Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hasan (The First Part)

[The Story of Ka'b ibn Zuhayr after the Departure from Ta'if]

Ka'b ibn Zuhayr ibn Abi Sulma was brought before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) after he left Ta'if. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had previously been subjected to his mockery and abuse. After the conquest of Mecca, Ka'b wrote to his brother Bujayr ibn Zuhayr, saying:(1)

*Did you receive what I wrote to you — did you receive*
*The one who reported me in a letter?*
*So the safe ones perished from it, and the resin,*
[269] *I drank with al-Ma'mun a cup of resolution.*
*And I went against the means of guidance and sent it*
*On anything other than you — that*
*And she did not realize that he was a brother to you*
*On a nature that has not been twisted by mother or father.*
*If you do not, then I grieve,*
*And there is no one who says: Either I have stumbled.*

By "al-Ma'mun" he means the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). His brother Bujayr replied to him, and Bujayr ibn Zuhayr wrote urging him to fear Allah and directing him toward Islam, telling him: "The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) does not kill those who come to him as Muslims." So Ka'b took heart and presented himself to the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him).

[Bujayr said to Ka'b:](2)

[200/F] *From the measure of a fault, do you bear any reproach for one you blame in vain — one more resolute?*

(1) See: Sharh Diwan Ka'b ibn Zuhayr, 3–4.
(2) See: Biography of the Prophet, 502/4.

*You will survive if you are saved and be delivered*

*Zuhayr's way — and none is like him —*

He then recited his celebrated poem, in which he says:(1)

*Forgiveness is hoped for from the Messenger of Allah.*
*I believe the Messenger of Allah has promised me.*
*The excuse is accepted by the Messenger of Allah.*
*The Messenger of Allah came, and I came apologizing.*

*He kept thundering — whoever found his signs*
*In the hollow of the curses of the gossip.*
*Until I placed my right hand on what I disputed,*
*And he is attributed and responsible.*
*I will be with him when I address him.*
*Whoever is distressed by the hardship of the earth is stupefied,*

*Leave the horn untouched:* [200/z] [270]

*And do not walk in the valley of slanderers —*

*The Messenger is a light to be illuminated by,*
*In the belly of Mecca when they became Muslims and departed.*
*There is a company of Quraysh who said they are*

404 — *'Uyun al-Akhbar wa Funun al-Athar* — by 'Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hasan (The First Part)

*When you meet them there is no messenger of intermediaries —*

*Strike, even if the dark man returns —*
*They walk as the bearer walks; insults are cast at them.*
*From the weaving of David amid the tumult of Israel,*
*The mail-clad ones are heroes of their dread.*
*As if it were the throat of the one in disbelief —*
*◌ I have complained to her from a throat.*
*They are a people: not to be ignored when they intend,*
*Nor are they gladdened when their spears find their mark.*
*They have no withdrawal from the courses of death —*
*Striking only in their throats.*

Abu Bakr al-Anbari said: My father (may Allah have mercy on him) related to me from his elders, who said: When Ka'b ibn Zuhayr recited this poem — part of which we have mentioned in this book — he opened it with the line:

*Su'ad appeared, and my heart today is a captive, bound and helpless.*

The Ansar were displeased, saying: He praised his Muhajir brothers but did not praise us. Abu Khalid said: Abu al-Walid Ahmad ibn 'Abd Allah ibn 'Abd Allah ibn Tarif told me, from Abu al-Qasim Hatim ibn Muhammad ibn 'Abd al-Rahman, with his chain of transmission, from Abu Sa'id ibn 'Abd al-Rahim al-Barqi, from 'Abd al-Malik ibn Hisham, that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said, when Ka'b recited "Su'ad appeared, and my heart today is a captive": "Had he not mentioned the Ansar well, they would have(1) been worthy of it."

Ka'b ibn Zuhayr then said — and the poem was recited by al-Anbari:(2)

406 — *'Uyun al-Akhbar wa Funun al-Athar* — by 'Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hasan (The First Part)

*Truly believe me — I am*
*One who came away from Nazar*
*With the blood of those hanged from among the unbelievers.*
*Shahba' is blazing like fire,*
*And to her I have commended every companion.*
*She took the initiative to sleep like*
*Ghubra', whose habit is well known.*
*At dawn she is fierce and there is no path,*
*And she covered the shoulders of the free and the weary*
*From her tenderness to a nail.*
*Iraqi: a series of every kiss,*
*From a fault that reaped from the passage,*
*And sealed the neglect of the built-in illusion.*
*Even when the jars are veiled,*
*Like the shimmer of a running mirage,*
*After the hardship of her breast*(1)
[272] *I was pleased with her and forgave her.*
*If the enemies come to know of them,*
*They shook me on the day of the full moon with a shock.*
*They purify themselves as if they were ascetics,*
*And a sleepy patient was startled by her.*
*And I knew that I was in a wasteland —*
*The neck of treasure: its flesh survives; it is excavated, poor, later by poverty.*

[202/f]

*Swollen blossoms*
*In his shoulders — and I set out toward his broad plates,*
*After weariness and after sleep seized the night-traveler.*
*And it guides toward the distant burning*
*With the fingers of ambush at every orbit.*
*Our eyes are like the mirror of the makers that you guide —*
*She looks at her husband's glance and conceals,*
*With the beauty of her lair — and you know what.*

This poem is cited in this version from what was narrated by al-Anbari, the writer. Since it was briefly treated in the biography, it was shortened to its opening because that is what became well known and circulated.
