<!-- page 435 -->
407.
Battle of Tabuk

Then came the Battle of Tabuk.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had remained in Madinah from the month of Dhu al-Hijjah until Rajab. Then the Battle of Tabuk came, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered preparations for an expedition into the land of Rum. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) made his determination known, seven men from the Ansar and others came to him from Banu 'Amr ibn 'Awf: Salim ibn 'Umayr; 'Ulbah ibn Zayd, the brother of Banu Harithah; Abu Layla 'Abd al-Rahman ibn Ka'b, the brother of Banu Mazin ibn al-Najjar; 'Amr ibn al-Hammam ibn al-Jamuh, the brother of Banu Salamah; 'Abd Allah ibn — it was said: he is 'Abd Allah ibn 'Amr al-Muzani — and Harami ibn 'Abd Allah, the brother of [202/z] Banu Waqif; and 'Irbad ibn Sariyah al-Fazari. They presented themselves to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) in a state of need, and he said: "I cannot find anything to carry you on." They turned away, their eyes overflowing with tears of grief that they had nothing to spend. They are those about whom the verse was revealed.

[The Commander of the Faithful — peace be upon him]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) appointed 'Ali ibn Abi Talib as his deputy in Madinah and placed him in charge of affairs there. This sat heavily with the hypocrites, and they said: "He was left behind only because he is a burden to him." So 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) took his weapons and set out until he reached the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) at his encampment. He said: "O Messenger of Allah, the hypocrites claim that you appointed me as your deputy only because I am a burden to you." [Pg 273]

<!-- page 436 -->
408 — *'Uyun al-Akhbar wa Funun al-Athar* — by 'Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hasan (The First Part)

He replied: "They have lied. I left you behind as my deputy. Return, for you are my successor among my family. Are you not content to stand to me as Aaron stood to Moses — except that there is no prophet after me?"

So 'Ali ibn Abi Talib returned to Madinah, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) continued on his journey.

His saying "Are you not content to stand to me as Aaron stood to Moses?" — Ibn Hisham cited it in these terms in his biography.(1)

'Ali (peace be upon him) was not a brother to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) by lineage. This indicates rather that he is the guardian of Muhammad and his successor after him, just as Allah (Glorified and Exalted be He) said in the story of Moses: *"Succeed me among my people"* [al-A'raf: 142]. If the caliph of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) stood in that relation to his people, then is not one who defies him like the Samaritan who defied Moses and took to the calf?

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "You will assuredly follow the ways of those who came before you, span by span and cubit by cubit — even if they entered the burrow of a sand lizard, you would enter it."

His words (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) are true, and his promise is certain.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 520/4.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/1; Kitab al-'Ayn, 394/4; al-Majalis al-Mu'ayyadiyyah, 1/102, 127.


<!-- page 437 -->
Battle of Tabuk

[The Prophet's necklace went astray and the hadith of Ibn al-Lasit]

While the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) was traveling on a certain road, his camel went astray. His companions set out to search for it. Zayd ibn al-Lasit al-Qaitha'i — who was a hypocrite — said: "Muhammad claims that revelation comes to him from the heavens, yet he does not know where his own camel is." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) then announced: "A man from the hypocrites has said such-and-such. I know nothing except what Allah has taught me, and Allah has now shown me where the camel is. It is in this valley, in a place belonging to such-and-such people, held fast by a tree that has caught its rein." They set out and found the camel exactly as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had described. The Muslims asked who among the hypocrites had made that remark, and they were directed to him. He happened to be in the company of 'Ammara ibn Hazm, who was one of the Ansar of 'Aqbah and a veteran of Badr. 'Ammara then brought Ibn al-Lasit forward with his hands bound, saying: "O company of Muslims, I present him to you — I sensed his duplicity and could not ignore it."

[Pg 274]

[The Affair of Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) continued on his march, and one man after another was left behind. Each time, someone would say: "O Messenger of Allah, so-and-so has fallen behind." He would reply: "Call him back. If there is good in him, Allah will reunite him with you; and if there is not, Allah has spared you from him."

Then it was said: "O Messenger of Allah, Abu Dharr has fallen behind — his camel has slowed him down." He said:

<!-- page 438 -->
"Leave him. If there is good in him, he will catch up with you." Abu Dharr's camel had indeed slowed, so he unloaded his belongings and carried them on his back as he walked on foot.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had stopped at one of his halting places when someone said: "O Messenger of Allah, there is a man walking alone on the road." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "Let it be Abu Dharr." When the people looked, they said: "It is Abu Dharr, O Messenger of Allah." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "May Allah have mercy on Abu Dharr — he walks alone, dies alone, and will be resurrected alone."

And it came to pass exactly as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) foretold. Abu Dharr died alone in al-Rabadha, after 'Uthman ibn 'Affan had exiled him there. This is attributed to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), who also said: "The sky has not shaded, nor has the earth supported, anyone more truthful than Abu Dharr."

[The reconciliation between the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and the people of Ayla]

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) reached Tabuk, Yuhanna ibn Ru'bah, the ruler of Ayla, came to him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) made peace with him and granted him

<!-- page 439 -->
a covenant of protection. The people of Jarba' and Adhruh also came to him, paid the jizya, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) wrote them a document of dhimma.

[The capture of Ukaydir and his reconciliation]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) sent Khalid ibn al-Walid to take Ukaydir Duma' captive,

[275]

telling him: "You will find him out hunting wild cows." And so it was. Ukaydir was Ukaydir ibn 'Abd al-Malik, a man of Kindah who ruled as a king and was a Christian. When Ukaydir was brought before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), he spared his life and settled terms with him on the basis of the jizya.

On this occasion, Bajir ibn Bajrah al-Ta'i composed verses about the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and his words — "you will find him hunting wild cows":

*I saw that Allah guided all of this —*
*blessed be the one who drives the cow before me.*
*We have been commanded to fight as martyrs.*
*Who goes astray after Tabuk?*

[Return to the City]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) remained in Tabuk for several nights, then set out with his caravan toward Medina.

<!-- page 440 -->
[The account of Wadi al-Mashqaq and its water]

Along the way, there was a small trickle of water seeping from a rock — barely enough for two or three travelers — in a valley called Wadi al-Mashqaq. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "Whoever reaches that valley before us must not draw any water from it." A group of hypocrites rushed ahead of him and drew from it. When he arrived, he found no water and asked: "Who preceded us to this water?" They named them one by one. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) cursed them. He then went down, placed his hand beneath the rock, and poured into his hand whatever Allah caused to flow from that trickle; he wiped it across the rock and called upon Allah. A witness present described the rock as sounding like thunder from what poured out of it. The people drank and took all the water they needed. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) then said: "If you — or those of you who remain — live long enough, you will hear of this valley again: it will be one of the most fertile valleys in the land, and all that lies before it and behind it."

[276]

[The destruction of the Mosque of al-Dirar at the time of the Battle of Tabuk]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) continued until he halted at a place a short distance from Medina. There he found a mosque that the hypocrites had built as a place to gather.

<!-- page 441 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) ordered it to be demolished, and it was torn down. Regarding it, Allah revealed: "And those who took a mosque in opposition and disbelief,

[205/f]

and to cause division among the believers, and as an outpost for those who had previously made war on Allah and His Messenger" [al-Tawbah: 107].

[The account of those who lagged behind and those with valid excuses in the Battle of Tabuk]

Three men stayed behind from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) who were not hypocrites: Ka'b ibn Malik, Mararah ibn al-Rabi', and Hilal ibn Umayyah. Concerning them, Allah Most High revealed a verse. When they came before the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), they said: "We have no excuse for our staying behind except to ask Allah to forgive us, O Messenger of Allah." Allah then revealed: "And upon the three who were left behind — until the earth, vast as it is, closed in upon them, and their own souls became too narrow for them, and they were certain that there was no refuge from Allah except in Him. Then He relented toward them so that they might repent. Indeed, Allah is the Relenting, the Merciful" [al-Tawbah: 118].

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) returned to Medina. It was said: why did he not witness any direct combat during this campaign? Some narrators of the scholars mentioned that a large force of the people of Rum had gathered, and a fierce battle broke out between them and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family). He called out to his guardian 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), who was in Medina. 'Ali heard his voice in his heart and hastened to him, witnessed that fighting, and drove the polytheists away from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) with his own hand, by Allah's leave. Then he returned to Medina.

<!-- page 442 -->
[205/z]

This episode is not in conflict with the established virtue of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and his family, or with the merit of 'Ali (peace be upon him) — on the contrary, the honor belongs to both of them.

Those who cite the account of 'Umar calling out to Sariya — who was a commander in one of the expeditions — saying "O mountain, O mountain!" argue that the Muslims were routed, that Allah cast his words into Sariya's ears, and that Sariya took shelter at the mountain while the Muslims fortified themselves there.

Yet the call of 'Umar is not comparable to the call of the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), nor is it as credible as what is transmitted from 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him). Allah knows best about these matters. The miracles Allah grants His prophets in the service of their guardians and successors are immense, and His bounty to the servants of their Lord is generous.

[Pg 277]

<!-- page 443 -->
Arrival of Delegations in Medina (Ninth Year)

[The delegation of Thaqif and their acceptance of Islam in the month of Ramadan in the ninth year]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) arrived in Medina in the month of Ramadan. That same month, a delegation of the people of Ta'if came to him and accepted Islam. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "O people of Ta'if, establish the prayer and pay the zakat — otherwise I will send upon you a man who is as I am, and he will put you to the sword." He then took 'Ali's hand (peace be upon him) and raised it, at which 'Umar said: "Deflect, deflect" — as if trying to divert this virtue from him.

The delegation of Thaqif remained with the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) until the end of the month of Ramadan. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) commanded al-Mughira ibn Shu'ba and Abu Sufyan ibn Harb to demolish al-Lat, the idol of Thaqif, which was also known as al-Taghiya. Al-Mughira demolished it, while Abu Sufyan

[206/f]

grieved over it and cried out: "Alas — your father's ruin!"

[The hadith: "None shall lead you except a man from among you"]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) sent the declaration of innocence with Abu Bakr. When Abu Bakr had set out on the road, Gabriel descended upon the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and said: "None shall deliver this but a man of your own people." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) then dispatched 'Ali, who caught up with Abu Bakr, took the surah from him, and proceeded with it. Abu Bakr returned in distress and said: "O Messenger of Allah, has something been revealed against me?" He replied: "No — only that Gabriel came down to say that none should deliver it but 'Ali."

<!-- page 444 -->
Those of the Shi'a who argue from this point have said: "How can someone be fit to succeed the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) when he was not even trusted to deliver a surah that the Messenger of Allah had assigned to him?"

Ibn Hisham stated that this event took place in the ninth year — namely, the incident of Surat Bara'ah. It has also been said that it occurred before the conquest of Mecca.

It is narrated by Ibn Salam, with a chain of transmission going back to 'Ali (peace be upon him),

[Pg 278]

who said:

"When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) set out to conquer Mecca, he wished to reason with them and call them to Allah Most High just as he had called them at the outset. He wrote them a letter warning them and alerting them to Allah's punishment, while promising them forgiveness. He included within it the opening of Surat Bara'ah, to be recited before them. He then delegated Abu Bakr to take it to them and direct them to it. Gabriel (peace be upon him) descended and said: 'O Muhammad, none shall lead you in this matter

<!-- page 445 -->
except a man from among you.' The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) informed me of this and directed me to go after Abu Bakr. I set out after him, caught him on the road, took the surah from him, and came to the people of Mecca. At that time there was not a man among them who had not sworn an oath — binding upon every mountain of water — to exert himself in it with his own person, his family, his wealth, and his children. I conveyed to them the letter of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and recited to them: 'A declaration of immunity from Allah and His Messenger to those polytheists with whom you had made a treaty — travel freely through the land for four months' [al-Tawbah: 1–2] — from the twentieth of Dhu al-Hijjah, and the sacred months being Muharram, Safar, Rabi' al-Awwal, and the tenth of Rabi' al-Akhir. He also declared that no one should circumambulate the House naked or unclothed, and that no polytheist, man or woman, should do so. Every man I met, whether threatening or peaceable, whether showing me hostility or bringing accusations — not one of them gave me any trouble. I carried out what the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had commanded me."

[The ninth year and its designation as the Year of Delegations]

In the year 9, delegations came

[207/f]

to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family)

[279]

from across the Arabian Peninsula, and the Arabs called it the Year of Delegations.

[The arrival of the Banu Tamim delegation]

The delegation of Banu Tamim was led by 'Utarid ibn Hajib ibn Zurara al-Tamimi, and they accepted Islam. When they arrived, they called out to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) from behind his chambers:


<!-- page 446 -->
The delegation called out: "Come out to us, Muhammad!" In response, the following verses were revealed: *"Those who call to you from behind the rooms — most of them do not understand"* [al-Hujurat: 4].

**[The Story of 'Amir ibn al-Tufayl]**

'Amir ibn al-Tufayl came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) as part of the Banu 'Amir delegation. He said to the Prophet: "By Allah, I will fill this land with horses and men." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) replied: "Allah is sufficient for you." As 'Amir set out on his way, Allah afflicted him with plague in his neck, which killed him in the house of a woman from Banu Salul. He cried out: "O Banu 'Amir — a gland like the gland of a camel, and death in the house of a Salulite woman!"

**[The Arrival of Dammam ibn Tha'labah]**

Dammam ibn Tha'labah came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) as a representative of Banu Sa'd ibn Bakr. He accepted Islam, returned to his people, called them to Islam, and they embraced it.

**[Al-Jarud's Arrival in the Delegation of 'Abd al-Qays]**

Al-Jarud ibn 'Amr ibn Hanash came before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) with his family and his people from Banu 'Abd al-Qays.

**[The Arrival of the Banu Hanifah Delegation with Musaylamah the Liar]**

The Banu Hanifah brought Musaylamah the liar among them. They accepted Islam, but Musaylamah

<!-- page 447 -->
went on to claim prophethood alongside Muhammad (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). He employed rhymed prose and declared: "I am associated with Muhammad."

The accursed Musaylamah said: "God has blessed the pregnant woman, and brought forth from her what He wills, from among the enclosed membrane."

**[Zayd al-Khayl Arrives in a Small Delegation]**

Zayd al-Khayl came in a small delegation; they accepted Islam and their Islam was good. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "No man was ever described to me with great virtue that, when I finally met him, did not fall short of what had been said — except for Zayd al-Khayl." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) renamed him Zayd al-Khayr. On his return journey, he was struck by fever at one of the watering places of Najd, called Farda, and he died there. When death was certain, he said:

*And I leave behind a house alone, furnished,*
*While tomorrow my companions face the east.*
*Those who did not recover from it will strain themselves —*
*O God, if I fall ill one day, grant me return.*

**[The Arrival of 'Adi ibn Hatim]**

Concerning 'Adi ibn Hatim, it is narrated from him that he said: I held a religion in my heart, and I was a ruler among my people. When news of the Messenger of Allah reached me, I despised him. I said to a servant boy of mine who tended my camels: "Prepare for me all my camels." I told him to bring them close to me, so that if I heard of Muhammad's army advancing, I could flee quickly. He did so.

<!-- page 448 -->
Then one day he came to me and said: "O 'Adi, if you intend to act, then act — I have seen banners." I asked about them and was told: "These are the armies of Muhammad." I said: "Bring them closer to me," and he did.

I gathered my family and children and resolved to join the people of my religion — the Christians — in the Levant. I went to al-Jushiyyah and left behind a daughter of Hatim among those who remained there.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) launched a raid against my territory, and among the captives taken was Hatim's daughter, who was brought before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)

<!-- page 449 -->
[281] among the prisoners. She was held in an enclosure at the door of the mosque where the captives were kept. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) passed by her, and she rose and addressed him: "O Messenger of Allah, my father has perished and my guardian is absent — show me your generosity, and may Allah show you His."

He said: "Who is your guardian?" She replied: "'Adi ibn Hatim." He said: "The one who fled from Allah and His Messenger?" Then the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) moved on and left her.

The next day, when he passed by her again, she repeated what she had said. He moved on once more, and she despaired. Then 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) passed behind the Prophet and pointed to her, indicating that she should rise and speak to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). She approached him and said as she had said before: "Who from my people can vouch for me before you?"

He replied: "I have heard your words — do not be in haste to leave until you find someone from your people whom you trust to escort you to your homeland, then come and let me know." So she waited until a delegation from her tribe arrived. She then came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "O Messenger of Allah, a group of my people has come — trustworthy people who can accompany me." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) provided for her and gave her provisions, and she set out with them

<!-- page 450 -->
until she reached the Levant.

'Adi said: "By Allah, I was in my household when I saw her approach — and when she recognized me, she was ready to say: 'What a wretched man!' She called out: 'You abandoned your family and your son, and left the rest of your father's honour exposed!' I said: 'Say only good, for by Allah I have no excuse to offer — I did what I have mentioned.'" He said: Then she came down and stayed with me. She was a firm and resolute woman. I consulted her:

<!-- page 451 -->
"What do you make of this man?"

She said: "By Allah, I think you should join him without delay. [209/f] If this man is a prophet, then whoever preceded him to his side will have a distinction; and if his authority is that of a king, you will not be diminished in the glory of Yemen."

He said: I said, "By Allah, this is sound counsel."

So I set out until I came before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) in Medina. I entered upon him while he was in the mosque, greeted him, and he said: "Who is this man?" I replied: "'Adi ibn Hatim."

[282] The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) rose and took me toward his house. On the way, a weak elderly woman stopped him and detained him for a long while, speaking to him about what she needed. I said to myself: "By Allah, this is no king."

Then the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) walked with me until we entered his house. He took a leather cushion stuffed with palm-fibre and threw it to me, saying: "Sit on this." I said: "No, you sit on it." He said: "No, you." So I sat on it, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sat on the ground. I said to myself: "By Allah, this is the humility of prophethood, and not the manner of kings."

He then said: "Perhaps, O 'Adi, what keeps you from entering this religion is the poverty you see among its followers — but by Allah, wealth is soon to pour in upon them so abundantly that there will be no one

<!-- page 452 -->
left to receive it. And perhaps what holds you back is the weakness and small numbers you see among them — but by Allah, you will soon hear that a woman travels alone from the village without fear. And perhaps what holds you back is that you see authority and power in the hands of others — but by Allah, you will soon hear of the white palaces of Babylon opening to them."

He said: So I accepted Islam and said: "O Messenger of Allah, accept my pledge." He accepted my pledge of allegiance, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) recited to me a verse about the Christians: *"They took their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah"* [al-Tawbah: 31].

I said: "O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, we never worshipped them or prayed to them." He said: "Did they not make lawful things forbidden for you, so you forbade them; and make forbidden things lawful for you, so you permitted them?" I said: "Indeed, that was so." He said: "That is your worship of them."

'Adi ibn Hatim was among the finest of those who followed the Commander of the Faithful 'Ali, peace be upon him.

**[The Arrival of Farwah ibn Masik al-Muradi]**

The delegation of Farwah ibn Masik al-Muradi came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), departing from the kings of Kindah and distancing himself from them. He said:

*When I saw the kings of Kindah, I was exposed — like a man whose women have come to surpass him in the race.*

<!-- page 453 -->
[283] *I am turning toward Muhammad's rising community, hoping it will be blessed and flourish.*

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) appointed him over Murad and Zabid, and sent with him a collector of alms.

**[The Arrival of 'Amr ibn Ma'ad Yakrab]**

'Amr ibn Ma'ad Yakrab came before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). It is said that he was resistant at first and that war broke out between them, and that the Commander of the Faithful 'Ali (peace be upon him) eventually captured him after a struggle and overpowered him.

**[The Arrival of al-Ash'ath ibn Qays in the Delegation of Kindah]**

Al-Ash'ath ibn Qays came with eighty riders from Kindah.

**[The Arrival of Surad ibn 'Abd Allah al-Azdi]**

Surad ibn 'Abd Allah al-Azdi came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) at the head of a delegation from Azad and accepted Islam. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) commanded him to wage war against those of his neighbouring people who still practiced shirk, from the tribes of Yemen.

Surad ibn 'Abd Allah set out by the order of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) until he came down upon Jurash — a fortified city at that time — where tribes from the Yemen had gathered. They sealed themselves within it when they learned of the advance

<!-- page 454 -->
of the Muslims. The Muslims besieged them for about a month, but the defenders held out. Then Surad withdrew from them in the direction of a caravan, and when he reached a mountain pass, he was told: give thanks — the people of Jurash, thinking he had retreated in defeat, pursued him until he turned upon them and cut them down with great slaughter.

The people of Jurash had already sent two men to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). The Prophet said to them: "In what land is 'Thankful Mountain'?" They said: "O Messenger of Allah, there is a mountain in our land that is called that." He said: "It is not 'Sham' — it is 'Thanks.'" They asked: "What is the matter, O Messenger of Allah?" He said: "There, the army of Allah is engaged with your companions at this very hour."

They returned to their people and found that the day on which the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) told them this had been the very day Surad ibn 'Abd Allah killed their companions at that mountain. The people of Jurash eventually presented themselves, and they embraced Islam.

**[The Arrival of the Messenger of the Kings of Himyar with Their Letter]**

A letter from the kings of Himyar was brought to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), along with their messenger, conveying their acceptance of Islam. The letter was from al-Harith ibn 'Abd Kalal, Na'im ibn 'Abd Kalal, al-Nu'man — known as Dhu Ra'in — Ma'afir, Hamdan, and Zur'ah Dhu Yazan, king ibn


<!-- page 454 -->
When the Rahawi converted to Islam and turned away from shirk and its people, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had his cousin Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) write the following letter:

"In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful

[211/z] From Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), to al-Harith ibn Abd al-Kalal, to Na'im ibn Abd Kalal, and to al-Nu'man — known as Dhu Ra'in — of Ma'afir and Hamadan.

To proceed: I praise Allah to you, He beside Whom there is no god. Your messenger came to us from the land of Rum and reached us in Medina, conveying what you had sent him with — the news of your affairs, and your embrace of Islam and the killing of the polytheists. We have been informed that Allah has guided you. If you reform, obey Allah and His Messenger, establish prayer, and pay zakat, you owe from your harvest one-fifth for Allah, along with the share of the Messenger and what has been prescribed. What the believers owe in charity from land watered by rain or running water is one-tenth, and from land irrigated by effort, one-half of a tenth. For every forty camels, one bint labun is due; for every sixty camels, one ibn labun male; for every five camels, one sheep; for every ten camels, two sheep. For every forty cows, one cow; for every thirty cows, one tabī' — a yearling calf — [285] male or female. For every forty sheep, one sheep — a ewe — offered alone. [211/z] This is Allah's obligation imposed upon the believers in charity, and whoever does more good, it is better for him."

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/589–590.
(2) An addition from "B".

<!-- page 455 -->
"Whoever fulfils this and bears witness to his Islam, and stands with the believers against the polytheists, is counted among the believers — sharing their rights and bearing their obligations — and enjoys the protection of Allah and His Messenger. Whoever is a Jew or a Christian and becomes a believer likewise shares their rights and obligations. Whoever holds fast to his Judaism or Christianity will not be turned away from it, but he must pay what is due. Every adult, male or female, free or slave, owes one dinar of the value of al-Ma'afir cloth, or its equivalent in garments. Whoever fulfils this has the protection of Allah and His Messenger, and whoever withholds it is an enemy of Allah and His Messenger."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) also wrote to Zar'ah Dhi Yazan:

"I am sending my messengers and commend them to you in the best terms: Mu'adh ibn Jabal, Abdullah ibn Zayd, Malik ibn Ubadah, Uqbah ibn Nimr, Malik ibn Murrah, and their companions. Collect from your people what is owed in charity and jizyah from your various regions, and deliver it to my messenger. Their leader is Mu'adh ibn Jabal. Do not turn away anyone who comes satisfied to Allah.

To proceed: Muhammad testifies that there is no god but Allah and that he is His servant and messenger. Malik ibn Murrah al-Rahawi has informed me that you accepted Islam before the first delegation, and that you renounced the polytheists. I give you glad tidings, and I urge you to do good. Do not betray or deceive. The Messenger of Allah is the guardian of your rich and poor alike. Charity is not lawful for Muhammad or his household; it is zakat for the poor Muslims and the wayfarer. Even so, the good news has reached you and the unseen has been preserved. I have commanded you to do good, and I have sent you from the righteous of my family — guardians of their religion and repositories of their knowledge — and I urge you to treat them well, for they stand as witnesses."

<!-- page 456 -->
"May Allah's peace, mercy and blessings be upon you."

**[The Prophet's counsel when he sent Mu'adh to Yemen]**

When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) dispatched Mu'adh ibn Jabal to Yemen, he entrusted him with his mission and said to him: "Be easy and do not be harsh; give glad tidings and do not drive people away. Some of the People of the Book will ask you: What is the key to Paradise? Say: The testimony that there is no god but [212/z] Allah alone, with no partner."

Mu'adh ibn Jabal then set out with his companions until he arrived in Yemen.

**[The narration of the Ahl al-Bayt concerning zakat on livestock]**

[Pg 286]

This narration is found in the letter of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) carried by Mu'adh ibn Jabal, as Ibn Hisham transmitted it, in which it states: (1) "For forty camels, one bint labun is due; for thirty camels, one ibn labun."

Al-Qadi al-Ta'man ibn Muhammad (may Allah be pleased with him) narrated: (2)

"[We narrate] (3) from al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad ibn Ali ibn al-Husayn, from his father, from his forefathers, from Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon them all), that they said: When camels reach one hundred and twenty, and [if] (4) they exceed that number, then for every forty

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/589.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/259–260.
(3) An addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.
(4) An addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.

<!-- page 457 -->
one bint labun is due, and for every fifty, one hiqqah — a three-year-old she-camel — and one bint labun."

They did not mention the thirty. They said:

"If the camels reach twenty-five, one bint makhad is due; if no bint makhad is available, then one ibn labun male is given up to thirty-five; if the number increases by one, then one bint labun is due."

Should it be said: This ruling derives from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his household, and is therefore binding — we reply: they narrated nothing except from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his household. Their narration is more firmly established and more correct than the narration of Ibn Hisham, for they are the [213/f] custodians of the Quran.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "I am leaving behind two weighty things. If you hold fast to them, you will not go astray: the Book of Allah and the two prayers of my household. They will not part from each other until they return to me at the Pond."

Their word is binding and their doctrine is the soundest.

**[The narration of Ali (peace be upon him) concerning jizyah]**

Ibn Hisham also mentioned in this letter (1) from the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family):

"Every adult, whether Jewish or Christian, male or female, free or slave, owes one dinar."

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/589.

<!-- page 458 -->
It was narrated from Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) that the Prophet (peace be upon him) said:

[Pg 287]

"The jizyah is levied on free dhimmis who are adult men — not on their slaves, not on their children, and not on their women."

He (peace be upon him) further said: "It is taken from the wealthy among the dhimmis and those of comparable means: forty-eight dirhams per year from each of them; from those of middle means, twenty-four dirhams; and from those of lesser means, twelve dirhams."

The narration of the Ahl al-Bayt is well-established and is relied upon. Their pronouncements are beyond reproach, for they conveyed nothing in matters of religion except from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family. Jibril is the "Faithful Spirit" who brought the revelation.

We did not intend in this chapter to extend the discussion at length [213/z], but we have completed it and examined it closely. Rather, we quoted what the Ahl al-Bayt narrated from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family so that no one might suppose we are unmindful of what has been transmitted from the Ahl al-Bayt (peace be upon them) concerning the matters upon which reliance is placed — even if the people of deviation and obstinacy deny it.

**[The Islam of Farwah ibn Amr al-Judhami]**

Farwah ibn Amr al-Nafarah al-Judhami accepted Islam while serving as an agent of the Romans over Ma'an and the surrounding territories, and the regions of the Levant about it. He sent a gift to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family — a white mule, which the Prophet accepted. When news of his Islam reached the Romans,

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/389.

<!-- page 459 -->
they sought him out, seized him, and imprisoned him. While in their custody he composed these verses: (1)

And the visions between the door and the Quran
And I was determined to fall asleep and he made me cry
Peaceful and do not owe to the selfies
Don't close your eyes after me

And I taught Abu Kubaisha that I
Among the dear, my tongue is innumerable
And if you stay you will know where I am
If you are destroyed, you will lose them, I will be judged.

And I have collected for what the boy has gathered.
Of quality, courage and statement

When the Romans gathered to crucify him at a body of water called 'Afra (2) in Palestine, [288] he said: (3)

I knocked Salima, weakening my friends
Repel the imagination and its evil what it has seen

[214/f]

Did Salma come to the water of Afri on one of the rivers?
Trimmed with apiaries

On a camel the stallion did not hit its mother

When they came to execute him, he said:

Peace be upon my Lord, my greatest, and my maqam
Inform the Muslims of any

They then struck off his head and crucified him at that body of water. May Allah have mercy on him.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/591.
(2) See: Mu'jam al-Buldan, 3/688–689.
(3) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/592.
(4) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/592.

<!-- page 460 -->
**[The Islam of Banu al-Harith ibn Ka'b and the covenant of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) with them]**

The Banu al-Harith ibn Ka'b, the people of Najran, accepted Islam, and their delegation came before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family. He sent Amr ibn Hazm back with them to guide them in their religion, teach them its pillars, and write them a letter, which reads: (1)

"In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful

This is a declaration from Allah and His Messenger: 'O you who believe, fulfil your contracts.' [Al-Ma'idah: 1]

A covenant from Muhammad, the Prophet of Allah, to Amr ibn Hazm, upon sending him to Yemen. He commanded him to fear Allah in all his affairs, for Allah is with those who fear Him and strive to do good. He commanded him to uphold the truth as Allah has commanded, to give people glad tidings and encourage them toward good, to teach people the Quran and help them understand it, and to prevent people from touching the Quran unless they are in a state of purity. He is to inform people of their rights and their obligations, to be gentle with people in matters of truth, and to be firm with them against injustice — for [214/z] Allah hates injustice and has said: 'Is not the curse of Allah upon the wrongdoers?' [Hud: 18]. He is to preach to people about Paradise and its deeds, and to warn them about the Fire and its deeds, and to keep people composed so that they may understand the religion, and to acquaint people with the rites of Hajj — its obligations and what Allah has commanded regarding it. The major Hajj is the greater pilgrimage, and the lesser Hajj is Umrah. He is to forbid people from praying [289] in a single small garment unless it is one that covers the 'awrah; people must wear garments that reach down over their private parts. He is to forbid that

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/594–596.
(2) In "B": composed.

<!-- page 461 -->
a man gather the hair of his head at the back of his neck. If conflict arises among the people, he is to stop them from calling upon tribes and clans, and their call must be to Allah alone, with no partner. Whoever does not call upon Allah but instead calls upon tribes and clans is to be put to the sword, so that their call is directed to Allah alone. He is to command people to perform ablution — washing their faces and hands to the elbows, their feet to the heels, and wiping their heads as Allah has commanded them. He is to command them to pray at the appointed times of the day, to bow, to prostrate, and to observe full reverence. He is to lead the dawn prayer in the morning, and the midday prayer when the sun has passed its zenith, and the afternoon prayer while the sun still has height in the sky, and the sunset prayer when night descends — not delaying it until the twilight appears in the sky — and the night prayer at the beginning of the night. He is to call people to prayer when it is called, and to wash when the time comes, and he is commanded to take one-fifth of the spoils for Allah. What the believers owe in charity from land watered by rain or flowing water is one-tenth; from land irrigated by effort, one-half of a tenth. For every ten camels, two sheep; for every twenty, four sheep; for every forty cows, one cow; for every thirty cows, one tabī' — a yearling calf — male or female. For every forty sheep, one ewe is given alone. This is Allah's obligation imposed upon the believers in charity, and whoever does more good, it is better for him. Whoever converts from Judaism or Christianity to Islam sincerely and of his own accord is a believer who shares the believers' rights and obligations. Whoever holds fast to his Christianity or Judaism is not to be turned away from it; whether free or slave, he owes a full dinar or its equivalent in garments."

(1) (2) and "B": + "And the jizyah is upon him" — and what is established in the Biography of the Prophet, 4/596.

<!-- page 462 -->
"Whoever fulfils this has the protection of Allah and His Messenger, and whoever withholds it is an enemy of Allah, His Messenger, and all the believers. Allah's prayers upon Muhammad, and may Allah's peace, mercy and blessings be upon him."

**[The arrival of Rifa'ah ibn Zayd al-Judhami]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) received Rifa'ah ibn Zayd al-Judhami, then al-Dhabi, during the truce of al-Hudaybiyah before the conquest of Khaybar, and he gave the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) a slave as a gift.

**[The arrival of the Hamadan delegation]**

The delegation of Hamadan came before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family. Among them were: [Pg 290] Malik ibn Namat — Abu Thawr, known as Dhu al-Mash'ar — Malik ibn Ayfa', Dhammam ibn Malik al-Salmani, and Amirah ibn Malik al-Kharafi. They met the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) upon his return from Tabuk, wearing garments of striped cloth and turbans. They had ridden swift horses along the route of al-Mays through al-Dawhan and al-Rahbah, and Malik ibn Nawab and another man from among them recited verses. One of them said: (1)

Hamadan is honoured by its markets and its noble lords —
None like them exist in all the worlds.
Its place is the high plateau, and from it come the heroes;
It has tables laid out, with food generously spread.

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet, 4/597.


<!-- page 463 -->
435
Arrival of delegations in Medina (ninth year)
The other one says:

Here you are, setting out through the dark stretches of open country,
through the rains of summer and autumn,
straining at fiber ropes.

So Malik ibn Nawab rose before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: O Messenger of Allah, here is a delegation from Hamadan — from every one present and from those who could not make the journey. I come to you along the bottom of the nawaj, bound by the ropes of Islam; do not hold against them, for the sake of Allah, the disagreement among the flocks. On the day of thanksgiving, the people of the black cattle and the cows answered the Prophet's call. They abandoned the gods of the nisab, and their covenant does not break what they established for Al-'Ala and what befell al-Yafoor — Bald.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) wrote out their covenant after they embraced Islam and proved their commitment to it.

Malik ibn Tanib then said, after they had departed from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him):

We are at the summit of Rahrahan and Saldad —
I ride with a heart full of ardent longing,
passing through the narrow, faint passage.

<!-- page 464 -->
436 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities — by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

A song for the riders descending from the monkey plateau:

I swore by the Lord of the dancers toward Mona —
a messenger who came from the Lord of the Throne,
that the Messenger of Allah truly believes in us.
No she-camel on her journey has ever carried a burden like his.
More formidable to his enemies than Muhammad there is none.
He gives when asked, and generosity comes naturally to him;
he spent the day in the company of the overseer, al-Muhannad.

<!-- page 465 -->
437
Mentioning the sentence of conquests

[Mention of the Conquests]

[216/z] As for the military expeditions dispatched by the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family — those are distinct from what we have already mentioned regarding the campaigns led by the princes. They include the following:

**The raid of Ghalib ibn Abdullah al-Laithi against Bani al-Malluh:**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered him to launch a raid against Bani al-Malluh. When he reached al-Kadeed at sunset and was resting in the valley, he sent Jundub ibn Mukaith al-Juhani ahead as a scout. Jundub said: I went out until I came to a vantage point overlooking the encampment, and I leaned against it and observed them. He said: I was keeping watch from the hill when one of them emerged from his tent and said to his wife, "I can see on the hill a dark shape that I did not see before. Check your vessels — have you lost anything? The dogs have not been pulling at each other." He said: She looked and said, "No, by Allah, I have lost nothing." He said: "Then hand me my bow and arrows." She gave them to him, and he shot an arrow. Allah did not let it miss its mark — it struck me and I pinned it down, but held firm. Then he shot another arrow that lodged in my arm. He said to his wife: If he were not a people, he would have moved — my arrows have found him, but he will not be your father's problem if you leave and want them; the dogs shall not gnaw their thighs. Then he went back inside.

<!-- page 466 -->
438 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Archaeology — by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

We waited until they were reassured and fell asleep, then when the night was at its darkest we launched our raid. We cut them down, received the blessings of the engagement, and the cries of the people rang out. They came at us, but the valley of Qadeed lay between us and them, and Allah sent a torrent through that valley from wherever He willed — Blessed and Exalted — without a cloud or rain in sight. It came with a force that no one had the power to cross or withstand. [292] It stood between us and them, protecting us. We drove our gains ahead of us, then swiftly pressed on until we drew them back, and came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family.

**The raid of Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him — against Bani Abdullah ibn Sa'd from the people of Fadak:**

When Gabriel came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) with the command concerning Fadak, he gathered his community and set out by night with Ali ibn Abi Talib and a company of men. When they reached Fadak, they found its people on guard and keeping their distance. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ordered Ali and his family — peace be upon him — to take the road into Fadak. When he advanced, the people of Fadak came out to fight him, and he engaged them. It is said that he killed eighteen of them. They fled, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family received them along with the Muslims. They submitted themselves to the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), and he accepted their wealth as ransom, giving his daughter Fatimah — peace be upon her — the land as her redemption. Witnesses to this were — peace be upon him — and Umm Ayman — may Allah have mercy on her.

<!-- page 467 -->
[Pg 293]
439
Mentioning the sentence of conquests

May Allah bless her. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) took a fifth of the spoils from the descendants of the people of Fadak, and whatever he saw fit, and divided the remainder among the Muslims. This was narrated by Abu 'Ubaydah with his chain of transmission.

**The conquest of Zayd ibn Haritha at Jadhab:**

This took place before Mu'tah. Zayd ibn Haritha struck some of those who had embraced Islam and was wounded. He brought back captives among them, and delivered a group of them to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family. Rifa'ah ibn Zayd al-Hudhami was presented to him with a letter written by the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent to his family, and the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him — gave him his sword and ordered him to ride out on this mission. Whenever Ali — peace be upon him — encountered anyone from Zayd's army, he took what they had and returned it to its people, so that everything they held was returned. We have already mentioned the martyrdom of Zayd ibn Harithah — may Allah have mercy on him — but we mention this expedition here, as we had not mentioned it before.

Abu Ja'al said:

If we had not ransomed her price with it,
and she was tormented and could not be set right by medicine,
and she did not want a neck that walks,
she defends the captives with her two daughters
to bring them out of the collar —
and if you entrust to Aws, then Aws,
beware lest the path be overwhelmed by it.
And if you witness our passengers in Egypt,

<!-- page 468 -->
440 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities — by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

nearly blind at a quarter-sight,
on the strength of a patient survivor —
in Yathrib when the tribulation comes,
the division among the people is its weight around them.

**The conquest of Zayd ibn Haritha — also — against Bani Fazara:**

A number of his companions were wounded, and Zayd swore an oath over his fallen dead, vowing not to touch his head with a ghusl from janabah until he had raided them. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent him, and he killed them in the valley of the villages.

**The conquest of Abdullah ibn Rawaha at Khaybar:**

This was before Khaybar was opened. He was accompanied by a group of companions, among them Abdullah ibn Anis, and al-Yusir ibn killed him. They reached water from Hafiz —

With every warrior like the master of Nahd,
a ransom for my mother Salimi in every neighborhood,
the next day you will see the commander in our dwelling,
Razam, and a group of Jews with him.

**The raid of Abdullah ibn Anis:**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) summoned him and said to him: "It has been reported to me that Khalid [218/D] ibn Sufyan ibn Yabih al-Hadhli is mustering men to attack me. He is at a certain palm grove. Go to him and kill him."

'Abdullah ibn Anis said: When I had done what was needed, the man said: Who are you? I said: A man from the Arabs. He heard of you and has mustered men against this man, and I have come to you for that purpose. He said: Yes, I am

<!-- page 469 -->
441
Mentioning the sentence of conquests

doing exactly that. Ibn Anis said: I walked with him until I found an opportunity, then I killed him with the sword and left his wounds upon him.

Then I went back to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and told him [294], and he handed me a staff and said: "This will be a sign between me and you on the Day of Resurrection." Abdullah ibn Anis tied it to his sword, and it remained with him until he died; he left instructions that it be buried with him, and so it was buried alongside him.

Abdullah ibn Anis said regarding this:

Remedies that free every crumbling pocket,
bright from the water of iron, al-Muhannad —
I left Ibn Thor like a fallen thing, with those around him.
I took what I came for, and the shadows were behind me and behind it.
A shooting star that burst from a burning flame,
a mother for the daring, as though it were —
I am the son of Anis, a knight beyond reckoning.
I told him while the sword was at his head:
the wide courtyard of the house is not withheld. [219/0]
I am the son of one whose destiny has not been exposed by time —
a man of pure faith, devoted to the way of Muhammad.
I told him: take this blow, one that none
preceded with both tongue and hand before me.
And if worry weighs upon my heart, that is enough.

**The conquest of 'Uyaynah ibn Husn against Bani al-'Anbar:**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) dispatched him against them, and he raided them.

<!-- page 470 -->
442 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities — by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

Among those killed at that time from Banu al-'Anbar were: Abdullah and his brothers, the sons of Wahb, Stress ibn Firas, and Hanzalah ibn Darim. Among those whose wives were taken captive at that time were: Asma' bint Malik, Kass bint 'Ari, Nahra bint Nahd, Jumi'ah bint Qays, and Umrah bint Matar. The Messenger of Allah redeemed some of them.

Salma bint 'Ataab said:

From such evil, so much sorrow ventilates as grief —
for my life, I encountered 'Uday ibn Jundub.
She lost her pride and her grandfather,
surrounded by enemies on every side.

Ibn Hisham said: Al-Farazdaq said about that:

And with the Messenger of Allah, Ibn Habis stood up
with a plan of broad resolve for al-Muhad, firm and resolute —
breaking their necks in the ropes,
for him he released the prisoners held in his bonds —
at the price of redemption, or by the arrows of the dividers.
Enough — the mothers who disagree with them.

[Pg 295]

**The raid of Ghalib ibn Abdullah in the land of Bani Murrah:**

[219/z] Mardas ibn Nuhayk, an ally of theirs from the Harqa tribe of Juhaynah, was killed by Usama ibn Zayd, even though he had declared: [I bear witness] that there is no god but Allah. When

<!-- page 471 -->
443
Mentioning the sentence of conquests

the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) learned of this, he said he was not to fight those who say: There is no god but Allah.

**The conquest of 'Amr ibn al-'As with the expedition of Chains, in the land of Bani 'Adhra:**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) dispatched him, and he arrived at a watering place known as al-Silsil. Abu Bakr, 'Umar, and Abu 'Ubaydah ibn al-Jarrah were among those with him. 'Amr ibn al-'As led them all in prayer throughout, and they served under his command.

**[Shi'a scholars protest against those who elevated Abu Bakr]**

On this basis, the Shi'a scholars challenged those who had elevated Abu Bakr, arguing against what they claimed: that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family had commanded the people to pray behind him. The Shi'a scholars said: 'Amr ibn al-'As has a stronger claim to the Imamate than Abu Bakr by that reasoning, for the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) appointed 'Amr to lead them in prayer. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) never appointed anyone over Ali ibn Abi Talib, and no one led prayer over him save the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) himself.

Abu Bakr had a Fadaki cloak that he used to wear on those expeditions. When the commanders were appointed, some said: By Allah, do not pledge allegiance to the man of the cloak.

Rafi' ibn Abi Rafi' al-Ta'i — who is Rafi' [220/f] ibn 'Umairah — said: I accompanied Abu Bakr on those expeditions, and he would regularly counsel me. He said: I advise you, and he told me [296]: never to plot against two Muslim men. I said to him: O Abu Bakr,


<!-- page 472 -->
As for the Emirate — if I saw that people did not honor the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and there was no one among the people except her, why did you forbid me from it? When he became my guardian and the people chose him, I came to him and said, "O Abu Bakr, have you not released me from commanding two Muslim men?" He said: "Yes, and now I am weary of that." I said to him: "What made you comply with the people's command and conspire against them?" He said: "I find no justification for it, but I feared division for the ummah of Muhammad (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)."

This is the narration of Ibn Abi Rafi': that Abu Bakr commanded him but he did not obey, and advised him but he did not take the advice himself.

As for Abu Bakr's claim that he feared division for the ummah of Muhammad (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) — by my life, he brought about the very division he feared. Had he left the guardianship and the emirate to the one whom the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had appointed over the believers, saying, "You are to me as Aaron was to Moses," no two people would have disagreed.

But the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "You will follow the paths of the Children of Israel." And in another place: "You will follow the ways of the nations before you, span by span and cubit by cubit."

(1) See: Pillars of Islam 1/1, Difference of Principles of Schools 30, Kitab al-'Ayn 394/4, Majalis al-Mu'ayyadiyyah 102/1, 127.

<!-- page 473 -->
And rung by rung — even if they had entered the burrow of a lizard, you would have entered it." — meaning the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) referred to their disputing with the guardians and their claiming authority other than what the prophets had commanded.

**The Expedition of Ibn Abi Hadrad into the Valley of Idham**

This expedition took place before the conquest. When a group of Muslims reached the valley of Idham, 'Amir ibn al-Adbat al-Ashja'i came out toward them. As he passed by the Muslims and greeted them with the greeting of Islam, Muhallim ibn Jathamah set upon him and killed him — on account of something between them from the days of pre-Islamic ignorance.

Concerning this, Allah (exalted be He) revealed to His Prophet: *"And do not say to one who greets you with peace, 'You are not a believer,' seeking the goods of this worldly life"* [al-Nisa': 94].

After the Battle of Hunayn, al-Aqra' ibn Habis and 'Uyaynah ibn Husn arose and disputed with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) over the blood of 'Amir ibn al-Adbat al-Ashja'i. 'Uyaynah demanded blood for 'Amir's death, since he was at that time the chief of Ghatafan, while al-Aqra' was ranged against Muhallim ibn Jathamah by virtue of his standing among Khandaf. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) ruled that blood money be paid for 'Amir ibn al-Adbat, and it was accepted.

As for Muhallim ibn Jathamah, he came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) seeking forgiveness. The Messenger of Allah raised his hands and said: "O Allah, do not forgive Muhallim ibn Jathamah." He repeated this three times. Muhallim ibn Jathamah did not survive beyond seven days after that before he died.

Al-Hasan al-Basri said: The earth rejected him, and they returned. 

<!-- page 474 -->
Then the earth rejected him again, and they returned to him once more, and the earth expelled him. His people then placed him between two shields and pressed him between them, then heaped stones upon him until they covered him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "By Allah, the earth accepts those far more wicked than him, but Allah wished to make an example for you in the sanctity of what I have forbidden you from."

**The Expedition of Ibn Abi Hadrad — Also — to Kill Rifa'ah ibn Qays al-Jushami in the Forest**

Ibn Abi Hadrad said: I had married a woman and pledged two hundred dirhams as her dowry. I then came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and asked him to help me with the marriage. He said: "How much?" I told him, and he said: "Glory be to Allah — if you were to take dirhams from the belly of a valley, you could not have increased more. By Allah, I have nothing with which to assist you." So I went to the mosque, and I became aware of a man from Banu Jashim ibn Mu'awiyah known as Rifa'ah ibn Qays, or Qays ibn Rifa'ah. He had descended with his people and those with him into a forest, intending to gather Qays against the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). So the Messenger of Allah called me and two men from among the Muslims, and said: "Go out to this man so that you may bring us intelligence and news of him."

He said: So we set out with our swords, spears, and bows as the sun set. We approached some of their people after dark had fallen. I took cover in one direction and ordered my two companions to conceal themselves in another direction. I said to them: "When I make my move, hold firm and close in with me." We waited in ambush as the night grew dark around the people. They had a shepherd who was delayed in returning to them, and they grew anxious for him.

Then their leader, Rifa'ah ibn Qays, stood up, strapped his sword around his neck, and said: "By Allah, I will go after our shepherd's tracks." Some of his people said to him: "We are enough for you." But he turned away from them and said: "By Allah,

<!-- page 475 -->
none of you shall follow me." He went out alone, and as he passed by me I shot him with my arrow and struck him in the heart. He said nothing; he fell fixed to the ground. I took his head and secured it, then rallied my companions. We closed in, and I swear that not one of them had any chance of escape save what they could manage — their women and children, and whatever of their property they could carry off. They left behind great camels and many sheep. We brought these before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and I came to him bearing the head of Rifa'ah. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) helped me — from those camels — with thirteen for my marriage, and I was thus united with my wife.

**The Expedition of 'Abd al-Rahman ibn 'Awf to Dumat al-Jandal**

Some of the narrators of hadith said:

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) — and 'Ali (peace be upon him) was among his companions — received a young man from the Ansar who greeted the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), then sat down and said: "O Messenger of Allah, which of the believers is best?" He said (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "The best of them in character." He said: "Which of the believers is most discerning?" He said (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "Those who most remember death and are best prepared for it before it comes to them." Then the young man fell silent.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) turned to his companions and said: "O company of the Muhajirun: five things — if they befall you, I seek refuge in Allah that you should ever see them. Immorality never spreads openly among a people until they flaunt it, but plague appears among them —

(1) See: Biography of the Prophet (peace be upon him) 631/4.

<!-- page 476 -->
— and diseases that were unknown to their ancestors who had gone before. Nor do a people withhold the measure and the balance without being seized by years of drought, grinding hardship, and the injustice of their ruler. Nor do they withhold the zakat due on their wealth without the rain of the sky being withheld from them — and were it not for the animals, no rain would fall at all. Nor do they violate the covenant of Allah and the covenant of His Messenger, without an enemy from beyond being given power over them, taking from them what was in their hands. And whenever their rulers do not govern by the Book of Allah and choose from what Allah has revealed, Allah sets them against one another."

**[The Mission of 'Abd al-Rahman ibn 'Awf]**

The Prophet then commanded 'Abd al-Rahman ibn 'Awf to prepare for the secret mission to Dumat al-Jandal. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) bound a turban on his head and said: "Go forth, O Ibn 'Awf. Lead them all in the ways of Allah — in the name of Allah. Fight those who disbelieve in Allah. Do not be treacherous. Do not mutilate. Do not kill children. This is the covenant of Allah and the way of His Prophet with you."

So 'Abd al-Rahman ibn 'Awf took command of the detachment and set out for Dumat al-Jandal.

**[The Expedition of Abu 'Ubaydah ibn al-Jarrah to the Shore of the Sea]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent him in command of a company and provisioned them with a sack of dates. When their supplies ran out and they were exhausted, Allah brought forth for them a creature from the sea. They struck it and fed on its flesh and fat, remaining there for twenty nights until they grew stout.

'Ubadah ibn al-Samit said: We took one of its ribs and stood it upright along the road, then we rode beneath it on one of the largest-bodied mounts we had, and the rider passed clean under that rib. When we returned to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family,

<!-- page 477 -->
we told him the news, and he said: "That is provision which Allah the Almighty has sent forth for you."

**The Expedition of 'Amr ibn Umayyah al-Damri**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent him to kill Abu Sufyan ibn Harb. This was before the conquest. He also dispatched Jabbar ibn Sakhr al-Ansari with him. They came to Mecca intending to kill Abu Sufyan ibn Harb. When they arrived in Mecca, they tethered their camels among a group of people, then entered the city by night. Jabbar said to 'Amr: "What if we entered the mosque and prayed two rak'ahs?" 'Amr said: "The people will be alert when they are awake with their troubles." Jabbar said: "No, by Allah willing." 'Amr said: So we went to the mosque, prayed, and then set out in search of Abu Sufyan. But as we were walking through Mecca, a man from the people of Mecca caught sight of me and recognized me. He said: "'Amr ibn Umayyah! By Allah, this can only mean evil." I said to my companion: "Run for safety."

So we went out and climbed a mountain, and they came out in pursuit of us. When they had almost reached us on the mountain, they lost our trail. We turned back and entered a cave in the mountain and stayed in it. We blocked its entrance with stones we gathered around us. In the morning, a man from Quraysh came leading his horse. 

We held still inside the cave, and I said: "If he sees us, he will raise the alarm."

[He came close,] then I would have been killed.

He said: I had a dagger I had prepared for Abu Sufyan. I went out to him and struck him a blow, and he cried out so loudly I heard the people of Mecca stir. I went back and returned to my place. He came —

(1) An addition from the Biography of the Prophet 633/4.
(2) An addition from the Biography of the Prophet 634/4.
(3) An addition from the Biography of the Prophet 634/4.

<!-- page 478 -->
— with people gathering around him. He was drawing his last breath. They said: "Who struck you?" He said: "'Amr ibn Umayyah."

Death overcame him and he died in his place, without revealing where we were. They carried him off. I said to my companion: "We are saved." So we walked to Dahnan, then took refuge in a mountain and entered a cave. An elderly one-eyed man from Banu al-Dil came in upon us with his provisions. He said: "Who are you?" I said: "From Banu Bakr." He said: "And who are you?" He said: "From Banu Bakr." I said: "Welcome."

He then raised his head and said:

*I am not a Muslim, and I will never embrace nor praise the religion of the Muslims.*

So I waited until he fell asleep. Then I took my bow, fitted an arrow, and drove it into his eye until it reached the bone. Then we left, and I said: "We are safe."

On our descent we encountered two men from among the polytheists whom Quraysh had sent to watch the city and gather intelligence. I said: "Surrender as prisoners." I shot one of them with an arrow [and killed him]. The other I took captive, bound him, and brought him to Medina.

**The Secret Mission of Zayd ibn Harithah to Madyan**

This was before Mu'tah. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent with him the freed slave of 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) and his brother. They were taken captive by the people of Mina — a coastal region with a large population — and the captives were sold and separated.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Who will deal with this wicked man for me?" So Salim ibn 'Umayr, of the clan of Banu 'Amr ibn 'Awf, went out and killed him. And Umamah al-Muzarriyyah said in response to this:

[302] [The capture of Thumamah ibn Athal al-Hanafi and his conversion to Islam]

A cavalry force went out on behalf of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, and before

they knew it, they had captured a man from Banu Hanifah. When the Messenger of Allah (may peace be upon him and his family) was informed, he said: "Do you know whom you have captured? (1) This is Thumamah ibn Athal al-Hanafi — release him from his bonds."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then returned to his family and said: "Bring together whatever food you have and send it to him." He also ordered that his she-camel be milked for him. Yet Thumamah rested unmoved — nothing that was offered prompted him to eat or benefit from it. Eventually he embraced Islam, and from that point he gave the Muslims no further cause for wonder. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then said: "What do you marvel at? A sound man (3) eats at the start of his day with the appetite of a disbeliever, then eats again — for the intestines of a disbeliever eat in seven portions, while the Muslim eats in one."

After his conversion, Thumamah traveled to Mecca to perform 'Umrah. He entered the city to see to his needs, and the Quraysh seized him. They were troubled over what to do with him, fearing that if they harmed him it would cut off their food supply and the goodwill of their neighbors. Abu Sa'id al-Khudri said:

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) dispatched 'Alqamah ibn Mahziz at the head of an army, and I was among them. At some point along the way, 'Abdullah grew angry with those under his command and ordered a fire to be lit. He then said to the men: "Am I not owed your hearing and your obedience?" They said: "Yes." He said: "Is there anything I might command you that you would not carry out?" They said: "Yes." He said: [226/f] "Then I charge you, by your obedience to me, to throw yourselves into this fire." At that, some of them rose as though to leap into it, but he stopped them.

I tracked them to the edge of Yemen, where they had entered one of the valleys and found themselves unable to get out of it. I took them and brought them before the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family. He recited to them this verse: 'Indeed, the penalty for those who wage war against Allah and His Messenger and strive to cause corruption in the land is none but that they be killed or crucified' [al-Ma'idah: 33], to the end of the verse. [Ali (peace be upon him) sent to the people of Najran]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) to Najran. He collected zakat from the Muslims there, while the Christians and polytheists became flustered; he slew their warriors, and they paid him the tribute submissively and under compulsion. He returned to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) bearing their charitable dues, gifts, and the tribute from the protected subjects among them.

[The Campaign of 'Ali (peace be upon him) to Yemen]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) dispatched 'Ali (peace be upon him) to Yemen on two occasions. Ibn Hisham narrates that on one of them [227/f] Khalid ibn al-Walid was present [in a separate contingent], (1) yet 'Ali ibn Abi Talib was appointed commander over all. Indeed, whenever the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent 'Ali (peace be upon him) on any campaign, he invariably placed him in command — 'Ali was the prince over all the Companions.

'Abdullah ibn Buraydah narrated from his father Buraydah al-Aslami, who said:

[Section 305]

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) dispatched us to Yemen under 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), and sent with him a detachment that included Khalid ibn al-Walid. He said: 'If you come together under one command, 'Ali shall be over all; if you divide, each shall command his own men.' We met the enemy, fought them, killed their fighters, and took captives. 'Ali (peace be upon him) chose for himself a slave girl from among the captives.

Khalid ibn al-Walid then wrote to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) reporting what 'Ali (peace be upon him) had done, and ordered me to carry the letter. I was among those assigned to him. I brought the letter to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and delivered it. I said: O Messenger of Allah, you sent me with a man whom you commanded me to obey, and he has now sent me to you to complain against him —

(1) An addition from the Prophet's biography 641/4.
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 93/1–94, Manaqib Amir al-Mu'minin, 388/2.

<!-- page 486 -->
and this matter is one you are best placed to judge." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said to me: "O messenger, do not speak against 'Ali — for 'Ali is from me and I am from him, and he is your guardian after me."

It was also narrated from Abu Rafi' that he said:

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent 'Ali as commander, and when 'Amr ibn Shas returned from the campaign he began to speak ill of 'Ali (peace be upon him) and to complain about him. Word of this reached the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), who summoned him. When 'Amr came before him, the Prophet said: 'Tell me about 'Ali — did you find any injustice in his judgments, or any unfairness in his conduct?' He said: 'No.' The Prophet said: 'Then why do you speak against him, saying what has reached me?' The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) grew angry until his color changed, and we could see the anger plainly in his face. Then he said: 'Whoever claims to love me yet hates 'Ali has hated me, and whoever hates me has hated God. And whoever loves 'Ali has loved me, and whoever loves me has loved God.'"

And in another narration from 'Amr ibn Shas: (2)

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Whoever harms 'Ali has wounded my very ear." He said: That was when I went out with 'Ali (peace be upon him) to Yemen. I felt some resentment toward him, and when I returned to the city [228/f] I began to voice complaints about him to those who would sit with me.

(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 154/1.
