One day I entered the mosque (1) and saw the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) looking at me steadily until I took my seat. When I had settled, he said: "By Allah, O 'Amr ibn Shas, you have hurt me." I said: "I seek refuge in Allah and in Islam [306] from ever hurting the Messenger of Allah." He (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Indeed — (2) whoever harms 'Ali has hurt me." I said: "By God, I will never harm him."

[The account of the Commander of the Faithful (peace be upon him) coming to the aid of Khalid ibn al-Walid and freeing him from captivity]

On one of the campaigns, Khalid ibn al-Walid found himself isolated in a valley of Tihama in which there lived a formidable warrior known as Muhlhal ibn al-Fayyad. Khalid called him to Islam and to bearing witness that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah. But Ibn al-Fayyad refused outright — the pull of the pre-Islamic order overtook him — and he called out to his people. They came to him in successive waves, gathering around Khalid ibn al-Walid until they surrounded him, took him captive, bound him in restraints, and strung him up with palm fronds. He then wrote to the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali (peace be upon him), saying: (3)

And what I am in from the humiliation of humiliation
Shame on me to see me
And it was tied to my neck with my sons.
It was suspended (228/i) in a leather jacket.
And in the palm of his hand a whip has been revealed to me
He says playful with the stupidity from me
I swear that accidents are enough for me
Your God means nothing about you.

The Commander of the Faithful (peace be upon him) set out at once, struck down the warriors opposing him, and drove the men from their positions. He reached Khalid, unbound him, and set him free.

**Farewell Argument**

[Mention of the Farewell Argument]

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) performed the Farewell Hajj in the tenth year of the Sunnah, after he had informed the people of Islam in al-Tawahi that he intended to perform Hajj, so that the community would learn the rituals of Hajj and what Allah (may He be exalted) had prescribed for them. He departed — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — from Madinah in the sacred month of Dhu al-Qi'dah, five nights into the month.

This pilgrimage is called "the Farewell Argument" because it was the last pilgrimage of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and it is called "the Argument of Conveyance" (al-Balagh) on account of what was revealed to him during it: "O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord; if you do not, you will not have conveyed His message" [al-Ma'idah: 67]. We will mention the hadith when we conclude it.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) traveled with his family. When he had finished at Sarf,(1) he instructed the people to perform 'Umrah, except for those who had brought a sacrificial animal with them.

[Mention of the Ihram]

From Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him), from his fathers (peace be upon them), they said:(2)

"Hajj is superior to 'Umrah — that is, the Hajj with which the Quran was revealed. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) had brought a sacrificial animal. When he reached Makkah, he circumambulated the House and walked between al-Safa and al-Marwah.

(1) See: Mu'jam al-Buldan, 3/77–78.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/307.

<!-- page 490 -->
462

What was then revealed to him from revelation led him to say: 'Had I known in advance what has now been shown to me, I would not have brought a sacrificial animal and would instead have performed 'Umrah. So whoever has no sacrificial animal, let him dissolve his ihram.' The people then dissolved their ihram and converted it to 'Umrah, except for those who had a sacrificial animal with them. These were then re-consecrated for Hajj on the day of watering at the Grand Mosque.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: This is the basis for the tamattu' — combining 'Umrah with Hajj — for those who were not among the people of the Haram, as Allah (may He be exalted) said: 'For those who were not residents of the Sacred Mosque' [al-Baqarah: 196]. For the people of the Haram are able to perform 'Umrah whenever they wish. But Allah (may He be exalted) extended this blessing to those who came from afar, granting them both a Hajj and an 'Umrah in a single journey — a mercy from Allah to His creation and a kindness bestowed upon them."

[229/z]

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said:

"When someone who is entering ihram intends to do so, he should make his intention explicit and declare what is forbidden to him, whether it be Hajj combined with 'Umrah, Hajj alone, or 'Umrah alone. He says: 'O Allah, I intend tamattu' — 'Umrah followed by Hajj.' Or he says: 'O Allah, I intend qiran — combining Hajj with 'Umrah,' if he has a sacrificial animal. Or he says: 'O Allah, I intend Hajj,' if he is performing Hajj alone.

[Pg 308]

Or he says: 'O Allah, I intend 'Umrah,' if it is 'Umrah alone — upon Your Book and the example of Your Prophet. O Allah, keep me in that state, and if You have decreed something against me by Your power,

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/306–307.

<!-- page 491 -->

then let that be what You will for me, and accept it from me, and release me from it.'

Then he should pray as he wishes, even if his full intention is to perform what he intends — whether Hajj or 'Umrah — without his limbs uttering it aloud."

[The Meeting of Ali — peace be upon him — with the Messenger of Allah — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family — upon his return from Yemen]

Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) made his way back to the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) from Najran, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent word to him. They met in Makkah.

['Ali — peace be upon him — sending word ahead to the Messenger — peace be upon him — about releasing the soldiers from ihram]

As for the report traced by Ibn Hisham, it goes back by way of Yazid ibn Talha ibn Yazid ibn Rukanah, who said:

"When Ali (peace be upon him) came from Yemen to meet the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) in Makkah, he hurried ahead [230/f] to greet the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and left a man in charge of the soldiers with him. That man then dressed the soldiers, and each man among them was clothed in a garment. When the army came out to meet the people, Ali said to the man in charge: 'What is this?' He replied: 'I clothed the men so that they might appear presentable before the people.' Ali said: 'Woe to you! Take the clothes off them before you bring them to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him

[Mention of Entering the Haram and Its Observances]

From him (peace be upon him) from his fathers (peace be upon them):(2)

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) forbade hunting in Makkah, cutting down its trees, and carrying off its lost property — except to announce it publicly on behalf of its owner. He said: 'Whoever injures game, fells a tree, or takes any game — meaning within the Haram — it is incumbent upon you to make restitution to him and to make his back ache with what is permissible within the Haram.'"

From Ali (peace be upon him):(4)

"When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) entered the Sacred Mosque during the Farewell Hajj, he began at the corner — touching it — and then proceeded with the circumambulation."

[Pg 312] / [Mention of the Circumambulation]

Al-Baqir Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon him) said:

(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/318.

469

"When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) entered the Sacred Mosque, he began by moving to his right with the House to his left, and he completed seven circuits — jogging three of them and walking the remaining four."

From al-Sadiq (peace be upon him), who said:

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) used to touch both pillars: the pillar in which the Black Stone is set and the Yemeni corner [232/5], each time he passed them during the circumambulation."

Al-Baqir (peace be upon him) said, regarding the words of Allah the Almighty: "Indeed, al-Safa and al-Marwah are among the rites of Allah. So whoever performs Hajj to the House or 'Umrah, there is no blame on him for walking between them" [al-Baqarah: 158]. Abu Ja'far(3) — peace be upon him — said: "The walk between them is an obligatory duty, and the words of Allah the Almighty are a declaration of that. Had there been a dispensation to abandon the walk between them, He would have said: 'There is no blame on one who does not walk between them.' But when He said: 'There is no blame on him for walking between them,' it becomes clear that people used to regard it as blameworthy to walk between them — and so it was.

The Ansar used to celebrate Manat — a great idol — and they were therefore reluctant to walk between al-Safa and al-Marwah. When Islam came, they asked the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) about it, and Allah the Almighty revealed: 'Indeed, al-Safa and al-Marwah are among the rites of Allah. Whoever performs Hajj to the House or 'Umrah, there is no blame on him for walking between them.'"

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/320.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 1/323–324.

[Mention of Going Out to Mina and Standing at Arafah]

We have narrated (1) from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family:

"Tomorrow is the day of Arafah. From Mina, after the sun has risen, I prayed the noon prayer."

And from Ali (2) (peace be upon him):

[Pg 313]

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) descended from 'Arafah to Nimrah and stayed there. [232/z] When the sun went down, he commanded that al-Qaswa be brought out, and he rode her. May Allah bless him and his family. He continued until he came to the valley floor, where he stood to address the people. Then Bilal gave the call to prayer, and he prayed the noon prayer, then the afternoon prayer, with nothing between them. Then he rode until the station came."

[The Sermon of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) at the Farewell Pilgrimage]

The sermon of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), after he praised Allah and glorified Him, included the following (3):

"O people, listen to me, for I do not know — I may never meet you again after this year in this place.

O people, your blood and your wealth are forbidden to one another until you meet your Lord,

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 327/1.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 327/1.
(3) See: Biography of the Prophet 603/4–605.

<!-- page 499 -->
471

Farewell Pilgrimage

just as the sanctity of this day of yours and the sanctity of this month of yours are inviolable. You will meet your Lord, and He will ask you about your deeds. It has been conveyed to you, so whoever holds a trust, let him return it to the one who entrusted it to him.

I have left among you something by which, if you hold fast to it, you will never go astray after me: the Book of Allah and my family. They will not part from one another until they return to the Basin.

O people, listen to what I say and take it to heart, for every Muslim is a brother to every Muslim. The Muslims are brothers. It is not permissible for a man to take from his brother anything except what that brother willingly gives him. [5/233] Every form of usury is annulled, but your principal is yours — 'you will not wrong, and you will not be wronged' [al-Baqarah: 279]. Allah has decreed that there is no usury, and the usury of Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib is all annulled. Every blood debt from the pre-Islamic era is annulled. The first blood debt I begin with from the blood of the Jahiliyyah is the blood of Ibn Rabi'ah ibn al-Harith ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib — he was nursed among Banu Layth and was killed by Hudhayl — for he is the first claim I set aside from the blood of the Jahiliyyah.

Yet, O people, Satan despairs of ever being worshipped in this land of yours again, but if he is obeyed in anything less than that, he will be satisfied with what you regard as trifling among your deeds. So guard your religion against him.

'O people, the intercalation is an increase in disbelief, by which those who disbelieve are led astray. They make it lawful one year and forbid it another year, so as to agree with the number that Allah has made sacred, thereby making lawful what Allah has forbidden.' [al-Tawbah: 37]

Indeed, time has turned full circle as it was on the day Allah created the heavens and the earth. The number of months with Allah is twelve months [al-Tawbah: 36]. Of these, four are sacred — three

<!-- page 500 -->
472 The Eyes of News and the Arts of Antiquities - by Imad al-Din Idris ibn Hassan (The First Seven)

consecutive, [233/] and Rajab Mudar, which falls between Jumada and Sha'ban.

Now then, O people: you have rights over your wives, and they have rights over you. Your right over them is that they not allow onto your beds anyone you dislike, and that they not commit 'a manifest indecency' [al-Nisa': 19]. If they do so, Allah has given you permission to desert them in their beds and to strike them without severity or humiliation. Their right over you is 'their provision and their clothing in a fitting manner' [al-Baqarah: 233]. Treat women well, for they are helpers to you, not independent agents. You have taken them in the covenant of Allah and made their intimacy lawful through the word of Allah.

Take heed, O people: Allah has appointed to every rightful heir his right. It is not permissible to make a bequest in favor of an heir. A child belongs to the marriage bed, and the one who claims otherwise is subject to stoning. Whoever claims a father other than his own, or claims a guardian other than his rightful one, the curse of Allah, of the angels, and of all mankind is upon him, and Allah will accept from him neither an obligatory act nor a voluntary one."

By "obligatory" he meant the obligatory duties; by "voluntary" he meant the supererogatory prayers.

As for the particular: it is obedience to him. Whoever obeys him obeys me, and whoever disobeys him disobeys me." Then he said to Ali: "Rise." So Ali stood, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) took his hand, then said: "O people, I am the Messenger of Allah to you all, and obedience to me is obligatory.

Al-Sadiq (peace be upon him) said:

"It is recommended for one who departs from Mina to descend at al-Abtah, which is al-Batha', and to rest there for a while before traveling to Mecca, for the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) did so."

It was narrated from Ali ibn al-Husayn (peace be upon him) that he said (2):

Al-Sadiq (peace be upon him) said:

"Do not pray (4) an obligatory prayer inside the Ka'bah."

[The Birth of Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr during the Farewell Pilgrimage]

Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr was born during that pilgrimage in Mecca. He was the son of Asma' bint 'Umays. Abu Bakr had married her after Ja'far al-Tayyar was martyred, and she bore him Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr. After Abu Bakr's death, Ali (peace be upon him) married her, following the death of Fatima al-Zahra — peace be upon her.

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 341/1.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 341/1.
(3) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 341/1.
(4) "And do not perform" in Da'a'im al-Islam.


<!-- page 508 -->
[320] When Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr was born, his "sister" Aisha bint Abi Bakr brought him to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) and said: [5/236] O Messenger of Allah, pray for him. He said: "O Allah, grant him the guardianship of the people of my house." She said: O Messenger of Allah, pray for him again. He said: "I have done."

So the supplication of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) was answered, and Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr became one of the close companions of the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him). He died in obedience to him and in devotion to his guardianship; Ali drew him near and held him dear, and never turned him away or excluded him.

[On the textual designation of the guardianship of the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him]

Allah commanded Muhammad to formally designate Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) as his guardian and to proclaim that guardianship before all those of his community who were present. This took place after his return from Mecca, when the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) declared the guardianship on the eighteenth day of Dhu al-Hijjah, before the Muslims had dispersed from the pilgrimage gathering. Those present, according to the reports, numbered seventy thousand.

It is narrated from Abu Ja'far Muhammad [ibn Ali] (peace be upon him): (4)

A man said to him: O son of the Messenger of Allah, al-Hasan al-Basri told us that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Allah sent me with a message [4/237]
(1) An addition from "B".
(2) An addition from "B".
(3) An addition from "B".
(4) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 14/1–16.

<!-- page 509 -->
and I feared that people would deny me, so he warned me that if I did not deliver it, He would punish me."

Abu Ja'far said to him: Did he tell you what the message was? The man said: No. Abu Ja'far said: By Allah, al-Hasan knew what it was, but he deliberately suppressed it.

The man said: O son of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah make me your ransom — what was it?

He said: Allah (Glorified and Exalted be He) commanded the believers to pray in His Book, but they did not know what prayer was or how to perform it. So Allah Almighty commanded His Prophet Muhammad (peace be upon him) to show them how to pray and to explain to them everything that Allah had ordained for them regarding prayer. [321] Prayer is mentioned in the Quran in summary form, and so the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) interpreted it and informed his community of what Allah had commanded them to do in prayer.

Allah also commanded that zakat be given, but the people did not know what zakat was, so the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) explained it and informed them of what is to be taken from gold, silver, camels, cattle, and sheep. He left nothing of what Allah had ordained regarding zakat without explaining it fully to his community and laying it out for them.

Allah made fasting obligatory upon them, but they did not know what fasting was or how to fast. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) showed them what was required of them in fasting and how to fast. (2)

Allah also commanded Hajj, and He commanded His Prophet to explain to them how to perform it, so that he might make clear to them
(1) An addition in the Pillars of Islam.
(2) An addition in the Pillars of Islam.

<!-- page 510 -->
all of that in his Sunnah.

Then He commanded guardianship, and said: "Your guardians are only Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe — those who establish prayer and give zakat while bowing" [al-Ma'idah 5:55]. Thus Allah imposed the guardianship of the rulers upon them, and they did not know what it entailed, so Allah commanded His Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) to explain to them what the guardianship is, just as he had explained to them prayer, zakat, fasting, Hajj, and jihad.

When this command came to him from Allah, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was troubled by it. He feared, and was burdened — fear that the people would apostatize from his religion and call him a liar, and his heart was heavy with that concern. He turned to his Lord, and Allah revealed to him: "O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord; and if you do not, then you will not have conveyed His message. And Allah will protect you from the people" [al-Ma'idah 5:67].

So he carried out the command of Allah and proclaimed the guardianship of the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), on the Day of Ghadir Khumm. He called out: "Prayer is a gathering." He commanded that those absent be informed. The obligatory duties had been revealed one after another, each obligatory matter descending in its time; and guardianship was the last of the obligatory duties. Then Allah Almighty revealed: "Today I have perfected your religion for you and completed My favor upon you, and I have approved Islam as your religion" [al-Ma'idah 5:3].

Abu Ja'far said: Allah Almighty is saying: I will not send down upon you after this any further obligatory duty — I have completed them for you.

It is also narrated, raised in a chain to Zayd ibn Arqam, that he said: (1) [Pg 322]
(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 99/1–100.

<!-- page 511 -->
"We went out with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) during the Farewell Pilgrimage. When we departed and reached Ghadir Khumm, he dismounted — and it was a scorching day. He commanded that a shaded shelter be prepared, its thorns gathered beneath it; he took shelter there and called out to the people: Prayer is a gathering. They all came to him, for only a few of the Muslims who had not accompanied him on that pilgrimage were absent. When they had assembled, a preacher stood among them, and after praising and glorifying Allah, he said:

'O people, Allah — ever living and eternal — has never sent a prophet except that He granted him half the lifespan of the prophet who came before him. I am on the verge of being summoned and I shall answer. I am leaving among you two things: if you hold fast to them after me, you will never go astray — the Book of Allah and the people of my household.'"

Then he took Ali's hand (peace be upon him) and raised it up, lifting it in his own hand until the whiteness of their armpits was visible.

He said: "Who has greater claim over you than your own selves?" They said: Allah and His Messenger know best. He said: "Is it not so that the Prophet has a greater claim over the believers than they have over themselves?" [al-Ahzab 33:6]. They said: Indeed, O Allah, yes.

He said: "Whoever I am his master — Ali is his master. O Allah, be the guardian of whoever is his guardian, and be the adversary of whoever is his adversary.

[5/238] Have you heard and do you obey?"
(1) An addition to the explanation of the narration.
(2) An addition to the explanation of the narration.

<!-- page 512 -->
Zayd ibn Arqam said: I later heard Ali (peace be upon him) in al-Raḥbah calling upon those who had heard the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) say, "Whoever I am his master — Ali is his master," to stand and testify. He said: So there rose from those present sixteen men, and they testified to that. As for me, I was among those who suppressed it, and my sight was then gone. "This happened after my uncle had died."

And from al-Sadiq, from his father, from his forefathers (peace be upon them):

"The last obligation that Allah Almighty sent down was the guardianship of Ali. [Pg 323] The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) feared — when the news reached the people — that they would deny him and that most of them would turn away from Islam, due to the envy that the hearts of many of them harbored over what had been placed in their breasts. When he performed Hajj with the people, he delivered the Farewell Sermon and addressed them at Arafah. The witnesses of the Hajj had gathered from every horizon, and he introduced the key features of their religion in his sermon and counseled them. He said:

'I fear I may not see you again in this place of mine. I have left among you something that, if you hold fast to it after me, you will never go astray: the Book of Allah and my people — the people of my household.
(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 104/1–105.
(2) An addition to the explanation of the narration.
(3) An addition from "B".
(4) An addition to the explanation of the narration.
(5) An addition to the explanation of the narration.
(6) "Teach them" in the explanation of the narration.

<!-- page 513 -->
They will not be separated from one another until they return to the Basin — a rope stretched from the sky to you, one end in the hand of Allah and the other in your hands.'"

The most beautiful thing he did — may Allah bless him and his family — was his most careful mention of guardianship among his family, for he had come to know [239/1] that no one among them would dispute with Ali (peace be upon him), and that the people would hand it over to them — meaning they would hand it over to Ali (peace be upon him). He kept to it and they were obliged to uphold it.

When he completed his pilgrimage and reached Ghadir Khumm, Allah Almighty sent down: "O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord; and if you do not, then you will not have conveyed His message. And Allah will protect you from the people" [al-Ma'idah 5:67]. So he proclaimed the guardianship of Ali (peace be upon him) as Allah (Glorified and Exalted be He) had commanded, and Allah (Glorified and Exalted be He) revealed: "Today I have perfected your religion for you and completed My favor upon you, and I have approved Islam as your religion" [al-Ma'idah 5:3].

It is stated in the narrations: (1)

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) received the pledge of allegiance to Ali (peace be upon him) from all the Muslims who were present that day, and Umar ibn al-Khattab said to him: "Congratulations, O Abu al-Hasan — you have become my master and the guardian of every believer."

[Pg 324]

It is mentioned in this connection that the following was narrated from Hassan ibn Thabit: (2)
(1) See: Al-Amali by al-Saduq 50, Al-Iqtisab by al-Tusi 220, Al-Irshad by Sheikh al-Mufid 1/177. Letters of al-Sharif al-Murtada 131/4, Reference to the Beginning of Abu al-Majd al-Halabi 53.
(2) See: Manaqib Amir al-Mu'minin 363/1, [verses 1–4], The Path of Guidance to the Way of Guidance 221, Al-Amali by Sheikh al-Saduq 670, Characteristics of the Imams 42, Warning the Heedless 60, Masar al-Shi'a 39, Irshad 1/177.

<!-- page 514 -->
I hear the Messenger calling out —
He calls them on the day of Ghadir, their Prophet.
They responded, and none among them stood blind to it.
He said: "Who is your master, and who are you?"
Your God is our guide — you and we —
And none of us has been disobedient in this state.
"I have appointed you after me as Imam and one to rest with."
He said to him: "Rise, O Ali, for I
am your guardian, and be hostile to whoever is hostile to me."
There is a prayer: O Allah, for him —
so be his supporters, sincerely loyal.
Whoever you are his master — this one is a guardian;
show him the guardianship if he obeys.
And that was the forenoon — the forenoon of Ghadir Khumm —
and I have never seen its like, truly without equal.
I have never seen a day like it,
nor have I seen a danger like it exchanged away.
Yet men pledged allegiance to her
in injustice, and let what was saved go to waste.
So the one closest to justice among them rose,
and the first of them was wronged in that.
They did not convey it to us, save —

[Pg 239]

In this regard, al-Kumait (1) says: This is from one of his poems. There is a great deal of poetry and news on this subject — had we cited it all, no single book would have sufficed, and the chapters would have expanded endlessly in its explanation.

The hadith regarding the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) standing at Ghadir Khumm to proclaim the guardianship of Ali (peace be upon him) is well known to all the people of Islam, widely narrated and collected. In it is the clearest evidence and the most evident path to his Imamate, and to his being the caliph after the Prophet within his community, and the most deserving of those the Prophet left behind to succeed him — for the Prophet made him the foremost among them
(1) See: Diwan [3–4], the Hashimiyya, pp. 223–224.

<!-- page 515 -->
in relation to themselves. And whoever stands first among them is their master [240/f], just as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was the most deserving in standing before the community of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family). Whoever the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) appoints as his guardian — that guardianship [325] is established for him, and for every one of the Imams of truth after him, even if the people of obstinacy deny his virtue, just as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) denied the virtues of the people of shirk and atheism.

This is the well-known textual designation that the Prophet announced, stood up for, and proclaimed before the assembly of the Muslims, so that people would have no argument against Allah after the Messengers, for glad tidings and forewarnings had already come to them.

[On some of the virtues of the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him]

Beyond this, it has been narrated from the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that he described Ali's virtue and his being the first of the people to succeed him after the Prophet — things the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) would repeat throughout his life and among his companions. Among what he said (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family): "Ali is to me as Aaron was to Moses, except that there is no prophet after me." It is well known that Aaron was Moses's brother, his legatee, and his successor among his people, and that he was a prophet. Ali (peace be upon him) was not a prophet, because there is no prophet after Muhammad (peace be upon him) — with whom Allah sealed prophecy and fulfilled the divine messages, and after whom there is none.

As for Ali (peace be upon him) — he was not Moses's blood brother as Aaron was [240/z], even though the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) had bound him as a brother and related himself to him when he established brotherhood among his companions in the well-known manner that has been mentioned. We have noted this, and it indicates that Ali stands in the position of Aaron — the successor of Moses and the guardian of his people.


<!-- page 516 -->
It is also narrated from Jabir ibn Abdullah, Abu Ishaq, from Hubayrah ibn Yarim, who said:

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to 'Ali (peace be upon him): "O 'Ali, you are my brother, my guardian, and my successor after me, and the father of my progeny. You fight in defense of my Sunnah. You will fulfill my religion and repay my debts. Whoever loves you during your lifetime, Allah holds him as a treasure. Whoever loves you after your death, Allah seals for him security and faith. Whoever dies loving you has shed his sins as he departs, and whoever dies hating you dies the death of the ignorant, and will be called to account for what he did in Islam."

Abdullah ibn Shaddad narrated, saying:

"A delegation came to the Messenger of Allah — may Allah bless him and his family — from Yemen. The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said to them: 'Establish the prayer and pay the zakat, or I will send to you a man like myself who will fight your warriors, enslave your children, and take your wealth.' Then he took hold of 'Ali (peace be upon him)."

Safiyyah bint Shaybah narrated from Ibn Anas:

---

(3) "And safety" in the explanation of the reports.
(4) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 111/1.
(5) Additional detail from the explanation of the reports.

<!-- page 517 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) addressed the people of Ta'if, saying: "O people of Ta'if, establish the prayer and pay the zakat, or I will send to you a man like myself who will strike you with the sword." He then took 'Ali's hand and raised it. 'Umar said: "This is enough — this is for virtue."

Muhammad ibn Humayd narrated, saying:

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was intercepted, so he took him — peace be upon him — repaired the matter and set it aside. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was saying: 'If the Banu Wali'ah do not desist, I will send against them a man like myself who will kill their fighters and take their women and children captive.'" 'Umar said to Abu Dharr: "O Abu Dharr, whom does he mean?" Abu Dharr replied — and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) could hear him — "It does not concern you, O 'Umar, nor your companion. He means the owner of the sandal."

It is narrated with another chain going back to Malik ibn Anas, who said:

"I was the servant of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him). He called me to bring water for ablution, so I came to him. He performed ablution, then prayed two rak'ahs, then called me again and said: 'O Anas, there will enter upon you now the Commander of the Faithful, the Master of the Muslims, the Best of the Guardians, and the First of all people to believe.'"

Anas said: I said to myself, "O Allah, let him be one of the Ansar," and then someone knocked at the door.

---
(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 111/1.
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 112/1.
(3) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 119/1.

<!-- page 518 -->
I opened the door, and it was 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him).

The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) rose to receive him and wiped his face with his own hand, then wiped 'Ali ibn Abi Talib's face (peace be upon him) with it. 'Ali's eyes filled with tears and he said: "O Messenger of Allah, has something been revealed? I have never seen you do anything like this for me before."

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to him: "What I have done to you is so that you may hear my voice, relieve me of my burden, and make clear to the people the matters over which they will differ after me." He then said: "And it shall be made clear to the people what they differ about after me." This is consistent with the word of Allah, the Exalted and Glorified: ⟪ar:وَمَا أَنزَلْنَا عَلَيْكَ الْكِتَابَ إِلَّا لِتُبَيِّنَ لَهُمُ الَّذِي اخْتَلَفُوا فِيهِ⟫ "And We have not sent down the Book to you except that you may make clear to them that wherein they differ" [al-Nahl: 64]. Thus he raised 'Ali (peace be upon him) to fulfill that clarifying role after him.

It is also narrated with a chain going back to Hudhayfah ibn al-Yaman, who said:

"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) came to us one day carrying al-Hasan and al-Husayn, one on each arm, and said: 'These two are the best of people in father and mother. Their father is 'Ali ibn Abi Talib, the brother of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), his minister, his guardian, his cousin, and his successor after him — and the first among men in all the worlds to believe in Allah and His Messenger. Their mother, Fatimah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), is the best woman of the two worlds.'"

---
(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 119/1–121.

<!-- page 519 -->
"These two are the best of people in grandfather and grandmother. Their grandfather is the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and their grandmother was among the first to believe in Allah.

These two are the best of people in paternal uncle and paternal aunt. Their uncle is Ja'far al-Tayyar, who is in Paradise. Their aunt, Umm Hani bint Abi Talib, never associated anything with Allah, not even for the blink of an eye.

These two are the best of people in maternal uncle and maternal aunt. Their maternal uncle is al-Qasim, son of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and their maternal aunt is Zaynab bint the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)."

There are many reports in this regard about the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him), narrated both by those close to him and by the wider community, all of them reliable.

We have narrated from 'Ali (peace be upon him) that some people came to him and said: "O Commander of the Faithful, tell us the greatest of your virtues." He replied: "My greatest virtues are those in which I played no part myself." They said: "And what are those, O Commander of the Faithful?" He said: "When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) came to Madinah, he ordered the construction of the mosque. Every one of his companions had opened a door into the mosque. Then Gabriel (peace be upon him) came and commanded him to order them to close their doors and leave only my door open. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent Mu'adh ibn Jabal to them. He went to Abu Bakr and ordered him to close his door. He said, 'I hear and I obey,' and closed his door. He then sent word to 'Umar, ordering him likewise. 'Umar came to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and said: 'O Messenger of Allah,

---
(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 16/1–19.

<!-- page 520 -->
allow me at least to look upon you with my own eyes.' The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) refused, and so 'Umar closed his door. Then he sent word to Talhah, al-Zubayr, 'Uthman, 'Abd al-Rahman, Sa'd, Hamzah, and al-'Abbas, ordering them all to close their doors. They heard and obeyed. Hamzah and al-'Abbas said: 'He orders us to close our doors yet leaves 'Ali's door open.'

Word of this reached the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and he said: 'I have heard what you have said about the closing of the doors. By Allah, I did not do this — Allah did it. Allah revealed to Moses that he should take a pure house in which only he, Aaron, and Aaron's sons would reside, and no others should be admitted. And Allah inspired me to take this house as a place of purity, in which none would dwell except me, 'Ali, al-Hasan, and al-Husayn. By Allah, I did not order your doors closed, nor did I open 'Ali's door — Allah commanded me to do it.'"

They said: "O Commander of the Faithful, tell us more." He said: "The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) received a delegation of Christian scholars from Najran, and they disputed with him concerning Jesus. Then Allah sent down this verse upon him: ⟪ar:إِنَّ مَثَلَ عِيسَىٰ عِندَ اللَّهِ كَمَثَلِ آدَمَ خَلَقَهُ مِن تُرَابٍ⟫ 'Truly, the likeness of Jesus in the sight of Allah is as the likeness of Adam, whom He created from dust' [Al-'Imran: 59], to the end of the verse.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then went in and took by the hand himself, al-Hasan, al-Husayn, and Fatimah, and went out to perform the mubahala. He raised his palm to the sky and interlaced his fingers with theirs, and invited the delegation to the mubahala. When the two leading men of the delegation saw him, one said to the other: 'By Allah, if he is a prophet, we are destroyed; and if he is not a prophet, his own people will suffice against us.' So they held back and withdrew."

They said: "O Commander of the Faithful, tell us more."

<!-- page 521 -->
"The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) sent Abu Bakr with the declaration of acquittal to the people at the season of Hajj, to recite it to them. Then Gabriel (peace be upon him) descended and said: 'O Muhammad, none may convey this on your behalf except 'Ali.' So the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) called me and commanded me to ride his she-camel, ride after Abu Bakr, take the declaration of acquittal from him, and recite it to the people in Mecca. Abu Bakr said: 'Is this a reproach against me?' I said: 'No, but it was revealed to him that only a man of his household should convey it.' When we reached Mecca — it was the day of sacrifice in the afternoon, the Day of the Greater Hajj — I stood when the people had gathered and said: 'Am I not a messenger of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) to you?' Then I recited to them: ⟪ar:بَرَاءَةٌ مِّنَ اللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِ إِلَى الَّذِينَ عَاهَدتُّم مِّنَ الْمُشْرِكِينَ فَسِيحُوا فِي الْأَرْضِ أَرْبَعَةَ أَشْهُرٍ⟫ 'A declaration of acquittal from Allah and His Messenger to those polytheists with whom you made a covenant — travel freely in the land for four months' [al-Tawbah: 1–2] — twenty days of Dhu al-Hijjah, then Muharram, Safar, Rabi' al-Awwal, and the tenth of Rabi' al-Thani. I said: 'No one shall circumambulate the House naked; no polytheist, male or female, shall do so — except those who had a covenant with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), whose covenant stands for those four months.' And the designation by my name in the Book of Allah Almighty — none knows this but me."

They said: "O Commander of the Faithful, tell us more." He said: "I was in the Sacred Mosque with 'Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib and 'Uthman ibn Shaybah. 'Uthman ibn Shaybah boasted over 'Ali, saying: 'The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) entrusted me with the Sadanah' — meaning the keys. Al-'Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib said: 'The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) entrusted me with the watering — that is, Zamzam.' They said: 'And he gave you nothing, O 'Ali.' Then Allah the Almighty sent down: ⟪ar:أَجَعَلْتُمْ سِقَايَةَ الْحَاجِّ وَعِمَارَةَ الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ⟫ 'Do you consider the watering of the pilgrim and the upkeep of the Sacred Mosque

<!-- page 522 -->
⟪ar:كَمَنْ آمَنَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ وَجَاهَدَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ لَا يَسْتَوُونَ عِندَ اللَّهِ وَاللَّهُ لَا يَهْدِي الْقَوْمَ الظَّالِمِينَ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَهَاجَرُوا وَجَاهَدُوا فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ بِأَمْوَالِهِمْ وَأَنفُسِهِمْ أَعْظَمُ دَرَجَةً عِندَ اللَّهِ وَأُولَٰئِكَ هُمُ الْفَائِزُونَ يُبَشِّرُهُمْ رَبُّهُم بِرَحْمَةٍ مِّنْهُ وَرِضْوَانٍ وَجَنَّاتٍ لَّهُمْ فِيهَا نَعِيمٌ مُّقِيمٌ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا أَبَدًا إِنَّ اللَّهَ عِندَهُ أَجْرٌ عَظِيمٌ⟫ equal to those who believe in Allah and the Last Day and strive in the cause of Allah? They are not equal in the sight of Allah. Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people. Those who believed, emigrated, and strove for the cause of Allah with their wealth and their persons are far greater in rank with Allah — and it is they who are the triumphant. Their Lord gives them glad tidings of mercy from Him, and His pleasure, and gardens in which there is for them everlasting bliss, abiding therein forever. Truly with Allah is a great reward' [al-Tawbah: 19–22]."

They said: "O Commander of the Faithful, tell us more."

"When he concluded his Farewell Pilgrimage and set out toward Madinah, he stopped at Ghadir Khumm. He ordered the trees there cleared of what grew beneath them, gathered the people, then took my hand and raised it toward the sky. He said: 'Am I not more deserving of you than your own selves?' They said: 'Yes.' He said: 'Whoever I am his master — I am his master. O Allah, befriend those who befriend him and be an enemy to those who are his enemies.' And he returned from his return."

These virtues testify to the Imamate of the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), and to his succession after the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). They are reports unanimously transmitted among the people of Islam, conveyed by those closest to the Prophet and by the wider community alike.

That the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) kept 'Ali's door open while ordering all others closed is itself a testimony to the Imamate — for whoever had their door closed was cut off by Allah and His Messenger from that proximity, and it would be inconceivable for the Messenger of Allah to do such a thing without purpose or in vain.

The verse of al-Mubahalah likewise testifies to 'Ali's distinction, and to the distinction of his children and his family. It shows that 'Ali (peace be upon him) was, in a real sense, as the self of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), since Allah called him by that designation;

<!-- page 523 -->
saying: ⟪ar:فَقُلْ تَعَالَوْا نَدْعُ أَبْنَاءَنَا وَأَبْنَاءَكُمْ وَنِسَاءَنَا وَنِسَاءَكُمْ وَأَنفُسَنَا وَأَنفُسَكُمْ ثُمَّ نَبْتَهِلْ فَنَجْعَل لَّعْنَتَ اللَّهِ عَلَى الْكَاذِبِينَ⟫ "Say: Come, let us call upon our sons and your sons, our women and your women, and ourselves and yourselves — then let us pray earnestly and invoke the curse of Allah upon the liars" [Al-'Imran: 61].

Numerous authentic narrations, in which there is no dispute or ambiguity, confirm that this verse was revealed in connection with the matter of the mubahala, and that the pronoun in Allah's words "and ourselves" refers to the Commander of the Faithful and his guardian — upon him be the finest of prayers.

Any one of these virtues alone would be sufficient — for none of them is shared by anyone else, and Allah the Almighty singled out by each of them only the one He chose to succeed the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him).

As for the matter of the acquittal and Abu Bakr's being turned back from conveying it: the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said, "None can deliver it on my behalf except a man of my own household."

It is narrated from al-Baqir, Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him), that he said regarding the words of Allah the Almighty: ⟪ar:أَفَمَن كَانَ عَلَىٰ بَيِّنَةٍ مِّن رَّبِّهِ وَيَتْلُوهُ شَاهِدٌ مِّنْهُ⟫ "Is one who stands upon a clear proof from his Lord, and a witness from him follows him?" [Hud: 17]: "He who stands upon a clear proof from his Lord is the Messenger — may Allah bless him and his family. And 'the witness from him who follows him' is 'Ali (peace be upon him) — reciting as an Imam after him, and as an argument against those of his community who have deviated from him."

---
(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 19/1.
(2) Addition from manuscript "B".
(3) Addition from the pillars of Islam.


<!-- page 524 -->
It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that he said: "Ali is from me and I am from him, and he is the guardian of every believer after me."

The Shi'a raised the objection against those who put forward Abu Bakr: the very man who could not complete the recitation of Surat Bara'ah from the Quran — how could he be fit to succeed the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)? This is one of the clearest arguments, one that cannot be deflected or dismissed by anyone whose nature God has not sealed upon his heart, his hearing, and his sight to no avail.

The hadith of Ghadir Khumm, and the Prophet's public declaration of Ali's guardianship (peace be upon him), together with what was revealed in the Quran concerning it, constitute the clearest proof and the most unambiguous statement.

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) represented himself in Medina through Ali, making him the deputy of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), his crown prince, and the most deserving of his position after him. Whoever claims otherwise is a liar, and none but the people of ingratitude and doubt deny this. Even if we were to cite everything that has been transmitted on the matter, this book could not contain it all. In what we have already mentioned there is more than enough richness and sufficiency for those whom God has guided to righteousness, led to faith, and turned away from inclination toward the path of the people of error and transgression.

(1) See: Pillars of Islam, 20/1.

<!-- page 525 -->
**Farewell Argument**

**[The emergence of the hypocrites and their envy of Ali (peace be upon him)]**

When the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) repeatedly designated Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) as his guardian and declared his guardianship obligatory — that he was the one to come after him — certain people among his companions became consumed with envy. They conspired to refute what Allah and His Messenger had established, to cut off what God had commanded him to convey, and they agreed among themselves that prophecy and guardianship should not both reside in Bani Hashim. They said: "We are the first and the foremost." Such was their envy of the guardian and their hostility toward him when God had bestowed upon him immense grace and placed him in a position of great consequence.

"And there were in the city nine factions who were spreading corruption in the land, and they would not reform. They swore by Allah: 'We will attack him and his family by night, and then we will say to his avenger: We did not witness the destruction of his family, and we are truthful.'" [Al-Naml: 48–49]

These were the hypocrites: they dared to scheme against the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and to discredit him. But Allah made them the losers, for Allah is the best of planners.

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) cursed them, and among those he cursed was Abu Musa Abdullah ibn Qays al-Ash'ari. Ammar ibn Yasir (may Allah be pleased with him) said to him in Kufa, when Abu Musa was discouraging the people from fighting alongside the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him): "I heard the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) curse you, O Abu Musa." Abu Musa replied: "That did happen, but he forgave me afterward." Ammar said: "I witnessed the curse. I did not witness the forgiveness."

This curse was uttered by the Messenger of Allah (may peace be upon him) because Abu Musa harbored envy toward

<!-- page 526 -->
Ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him) and felt affinity toward the people of hypocrisy.

Abu Dharr al-Ghifari (may Allah have mercy on him) was made aware of what they had secretly agreed upon — namely, to betray the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family if they found the means to do so, and if they were unable to act directly, to bequeath that betrayal as a charge to those after them. Abu Dharr went to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and informed him. The Prophet confronted them, but they denied it in the most vehement terms and swore oaths that they had said nothing of the sort, openly or in secret. The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to Abu Dharr: "By Allah, were it not that a sign from heaven has come down to me confirming the truth of your words, I would not have believed you over them."

Then Allah revealed: "They swear by Allah that they said nothing, yet they did say the word of disbelief; they disbelieved after their Islam, and they aspired to something they could not attain." [Al-Tawbah: 74] — to the end of the verse.

The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "I have never seen, under green sky or upon brown earth, anyone more truthful in speech than Abu Dharr." The full account of this is lengthy, and it will be related in detail in what follows.

**[The dispatch of Usama ibn Zayd to the land of Palestine]**

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) returned to Medina and spent the remainder of Dhu al-Hijjah and the month of Muharram there. He then dispatched Usama ibn Zayd to the Levant, commanding him to

(1) See: Ansab al-Ashraf 173/5, Issues and Answers 313, Tahdhib al-Athar "Musnad Ali" 158, Al-Ghaybah by al-Nu'mani 84, Amali al-Tusi 53, 710, Specialization 13, Taqrib al-Ma'rifa 269, Al-Manaqib by al-Khawarizmi 84, Fara'id al-Samtayn 166/1.

<!-- page 527 -->
have his horsemen set foot upon the borders of Balqa and Darum in the land of Palestine, so that the people would be prepared and well-provisioned, and so that none would lag behind from Usama's army.

He placed among those he sent out with him Abu Bakr, Umar, and Uthman ibn Affan, and he said (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "Dispatch the army of Usama. May Allah curse those who fall behind."

(1) See: Pillars of Islam 41/1, which states: "Execute the army of Usama. May Allah curse those who fall behind."

<!-- page 528 -->
**[The beginning of the Prophet's illness (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)]**

This was the beginning of the illness of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), through which Allah took him by the hand and chose for him what He had in store for him.

According to what Ibn Hisham (1) reported, the Messenger of Allah (may peace be upon him) fell ill in Safar, or at the start of Rabi' al-Awwal. On the first day that he felt unwell, he went out at the end of the night to Baqi' al-Gharqad. When he reached the graves, he prayed for forgiveness for their inhabitants, then said (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "Peace be upon you, O people of the graves. May you be congratulated for the state you have entered, compared to what the people now face — trials advancing like the dark waves of the night, each successive one worse than the last."

He then turned to those present and said: "I was given the treasuries of this world and an abiding place within it, then Paradise — and I was given the choice between that and the meeting of my Lord." He said: "So I chose the meeting of my Lord and Paradise." He then prayed for forgiveness for the people of al-Baqi' and departed.

His pain grew upon him from that day, the day on which Allah took him by the hand. It was also said that the cause of his death traced back to the poison that had been prepared for him in the days of Khaybar in a sheep's foreleg — something of it had remained in him, and it now made itself known. He cried out: "I am poisoned!" His illness was the same one in which the sister of Bishr ibn al-Bara' ibn Ma'rur died. She said to her: "O mother of so-and-so, the time has come for you to find

(1) See: Sirat Ibn Hisham 642/4.

<!-- page 529 -->
the aortic artery severed from the food you ate with your brother in Khaybar." She meant her brother Bishr ibn al-Bara'. His case has already been mentioned, and the Muslims consider that the Prophet died a martyr, honored by Allah with the rank of prophethood.

**[Return to the matter of Usama's army]**

When the Prophet's illness grew severe, the army that had gone out with Usama — who had taken them out of the city by a distance of one farsakh — turned back. Abu Bakr was among them. They abandoned the command of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and went back with their commander alone, paying no heed to the Prophet's words: "Dispatch the army of Usama. May Allah curse those who fall behind." The Prophet's intent (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had been for all of them to depart together, so that the matter of his guardian Ali (peace be upon him) would be settled without dispute. They did not do so. Among those who returned were Abu Bakr, Umar, Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah, and others.

**[The account of Abu Bakr's stepping forward to lead prayer]**

Abu Bakr led the people in prayer, and when the Messenger of Allah heard his takbir — loudly called out — he came out, supported between Ali ibn Abi Talib and his uncle al-Abbas, whereupon Abu Bakr stepped back and the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) led the people himself. The people differed greatly over this matter.

(1) An addition from Sirat al-Nabi 338/3.

<!-- page 530 -->
The sound account, as transmitted from the Ahl al-Bayt (peace be upon them), is as follows:

"When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was at the height of his illness, Bilal came to call the adhan. Aisha said: 'The Messenger of Allah has fainted — do not disturb him. Tell Abu Bakr to lead the people in prayer.' So word was sent to him, and he came forward and led the prayer. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) heard the sound of his takbir and said: 'What is this?' He was told: 'Abu Bakr is leading the people in prayer.' He said: 'You are the companions of Yusuf.' He then took the hand of Ali (peace be upon him), leaned upon him, went out, and dismissed Abu Bakr from the prayer, removing him from it. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) then led the people himself."

As for the first argument advanced in favor of Abu Bakr — the claim that the one whom the Prophet appointed to lead prayer must be given precedence over the whole community — the narrations differ on this point, and no consensus exists on it, so it cannot be treated as definitive. Moreover, they themselves permit the righteous and the immoral alike to lead prayer, and allow prayer behind anyone.

It is well established that Abu Bakr did not surpass Ali ibn Abi Talib, and that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had prayed with Abu Bakr and Amr ibn

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 240/2–241.
(2) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.

<!-- page 531 -->
al-As when they were accompanying him during the Campaign of Dhat al-Salasil, and that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) appointed Usama ibn Zayd as commander over both Abu Bakr and Umar — and Usama was among them — with one of them praying behind him. The argument on this point is extensive, and it is readily available to anyone who seeks it with fairness and sets aside arrogance, for one who abandons a sound argument loses nothing by the quality of his abandonment.

It is narrated from al-A'mash, with his chain of transmission, from Abu Ayyub al-Ansari, that he said: "The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) fell ill, so his daughter Fatima (peace be upon her) came to visit him. When she saw the condition of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), she wept. He said: 'O my daughter, do you not know that Allah the Almighty looked upon the people of the earth with a look of knowledge, then chose from among them a companion for you, and He revealed to me that I should marry you to him, teach them knowledge, grant them forbearance, and make them the greatest of people in their standing before Allah?'"

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: "When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was in the throes of death, he fell into a faint. Fatima (peace be upon her) woke and began to weep, saying: 'Who will there be for us after you, O Messenger of Allah?' He said: 'After me, the weak will be honored — and by Allah.'"

(1) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(2) Addition from Sharh al-Akhbar.
(3) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 211/2.
(4) See: Da'a'im al-Islam, 228/1.

<!-- page 532 -->
**[The document he wished to write — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him]**

Muhammad ibn Isma'il al-Bukhari narrated with his chain of transmission from Abdullah ibn Abbas, who said: "When the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was in the illness from which he died, he said: 'Bring me writing materials, and I will write you a document so that you will never go astray after me.' Umar said: 'The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) has been overcome by pain. We have the Book of Allah — it is sufficient for us.'" Those present fell to disputing, and the noise grew. The Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Get up and leave me — disputing in my presence is not fitting." Ibn Abbas said: "The calamity — all of it, every bit of it — was what came between us and the writing of the Messenger of Allah."

What Ibn al-Khattab did — preventing the covenant of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) from being set down in writing, and what he caused the community to be deprived of — is in flat contradiction to the words of Allah the Almighty: "O you who believe, do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet, and do not speak loudly to him as you speak loudly to one another, lest your deeds be rendered void while you are unaware." [Al-Hujurat: 2]

**[The final moments of the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him)]**

It is narrated: "When he was dying, al-Hasan and al-Husayn (peace be upon them) were called to him. He embraced them and drew them close, until he fell into a faint, at which point they were moved away from him."

(1) See: Sahih al-Bukhari 32/1, chapter on the writing of knowledge.
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 99/3–100, Fada'il al-Talibin 38.


<!-- page 533 -->
When the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) opened his eyes, he said to Ali (peace be upon him): "Let them take pleasure in me and I in them — though my passing will weigh heavily on them."

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) later sat in the mosque, after Abu Bakr had led the people in prayer. He addressed the people, warning them of fitnah, and said: "O people, do not cling to me. I make lawful only what Allah the Almighty has made lawful in the Quran, and I forbid only what the Quran has forbidden. O Safiyyah bint Abd al-Muttalib, aunt of the Messenger of Allah; O Fatimah bint Muhammad — work for what is with Allah, for I cannot spare you anything from Allah."

Abu Dharr (may Allah be pleased with him) said: "I was with the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family during his illness. He was confined to his bed and said, 'Come close, O Abu Dharr — lean on me.' So I leaned against him, and he rested against my chest, until Ali (peace be upon him) entered and said: 'Rise, O Abu Dharr — Ali has more right to this than you.' The Commander of the Faithful then sat, and the Prophet (peace be upon him) leaned upon him. Then he said to me, 'Come here, O Abu Dharr, take my hand.' I sat holding his hand, and he said to me:

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 2/237–238.
(2) Addition from the explanation of the news.
(3) Addition from the explanation of the news.
(4) Addition from "B".
(5) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 222/1–223.

<!-- page 534 -->
'Hold fast to this contract: whoever seals his final moments with the testimony that there is no god but Allah will enter Paradise; whoever seals them by feeding a poor person will enter Paradise; whoever seals them by a valid excuse will enter Paradise; whoever seals them with an Umrah will enter Paradise; and whoever seals them with jihad in the way of Allah — even if it amounts to no more than a brief moment — will enter Paradise.'"

He then mentioned the rest of the hadith.

With a chain of narration, Salman al-Farsi (may Allah be pleased with him) said: "I asked the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family): 'O Messenger of Allah, every prophet has had a guardian — who is yours?' He said: 'My guardian, my confidant, and my successor over my family, and the best of those I leave behind, who will uphold my religion and fulfil my trust, is Ali ibn Abi Talib.'"

On the authority of al-Tabari, attributed to Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), he said: "The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) charged me at the time of his death, while I was his support. He said to me, 'O Ali, I commend the Arabs to your good care' — he said this three times — and then he breathed his last in my arms, may the prayers and peace of Allah be upon him."

It was narrated from the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), that he said: "The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) clasped my head,

(1) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 117/1.
(2) See: Sharh al-Akhbar 117/1.
(3) See: Nahj al-Balagha 422; Manaqib Amir al-Mu'minin 556/2.

<!-- page 535 -->
placed my hand in his, and wiped my face with it."

When the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) passed away, Ali (peace be upon him) had no time for anything else — he was wholly occupied with what had fallen to him: washing the body of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), shrouding him, preparing him for burial, and consoling his family for the calamity Allah had visited upon them in the death of their Prophet. They were overwhelmed by grief and devastation at his departure, while Abu Bakr, Umar, and Uthman gathered the people at the hall of the Bani Sa'idah, seeking to wrest the caliphate from its rightful people and assign it to themselves. Even had their gathering been a proper one, it ought to have been held in the mosque of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and in the presence of his family — at the time of his funeral, to pray that Allah would guide them to obey the rightful caliph after him, the one worthy of leading his ummah after the Prophet's passing. We will recount what is to be recounted of that.

It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) that he said: "When the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) passed away, a voice was heard — its speaker unseen — saying: 'Peace be upon you, O People of the House, and the mercy and blessings of Allah. Every soul shall taste death, and on the Day of Resurrection you shall receive your full reward. Whoever is kept away from the Fire and admitted to Paradise has truly triumphed, and the life of this world is nothing but the enjoyment of delusion' [Al-Imran: 185]. In Allah there is consolation for every calamity, and a successor to every perishing thing.

(1) Addition in Nahj al-Balagha.
(2) Addition in Nahj al-Balagha.
(3) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 225/1–226.

<!-- page 536 -->
So be patient and know that the one who bears affliction with patience will find his reward preserved, and the mercy of Allah is upon you.'"

It was then said to Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him): "Who did you understand to be the speaker, O son of the Messenger of Allah?" He replied: "We understood it to be Gabriel (peace be upon him)."

[On the washing of the Prophet — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family]

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, from his father, from his fathers, from Ali (peace be upon him), said: "When the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) instructed me to wash him, and that someone should wash him alongside me, I said: 'O Messenger of Allah, you are a large man — I cannot turn you over alone.' He said to me: 'Gabriel will be with you to wash him.' I said: 'Who will pour the water?' He said: 'Al-Fadl ibn al-Abbas will pour it, but tell him to cover his eyes — for no one who looks upon my nakedness except you will retain his sight.'"

And narrated from al-Sadiq, from his father, from his fathers, from Ali — peace be upon him — he said:

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 231/1.
(2) Addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.
(3) Addition from "B".
(4) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 231/1.

<!-- page 537 -->
"The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) instructed me to wash him. He was the one who served as his guardian — peace be upon him. He said: When I began to wash him, I heard a voice from the side of the house saying: 'Do not remove his shirt.' So I washed him — may the prayers of Allah be upon him and his family — in his shirt. As I washed him, I felt a hand passing alongside my hand — whenever I wished to turn him, it would turn him; and when I wanted to wash his front, it would position him, and I would wash his back. A voice called out: 'Do not tire yourself.' So I turned him to his side and washed his back."

Al-Baqir Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon him) said: "Al-Fadl ibn al-Abbas poured the water with his eyes blindfolded, while Gabriel washed him — the prayers of Allah be upon him and upon them all. He said: He was washed three times: once with water and lotus leaves, once with water mixed with ground spices and camphor, and once with pure water — this being the last of the three."

[On the shrouding of the Prophet — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family]

From al-Sadiq (peace be upon him): "'Aliyah (peace be upon him), upon completing the washing of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), shrouded him in three garments: two Yemeni garments and a shirt."

(1) Addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 231/1–232.
(3) Addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.
(4) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 235/1.

<!-- page 538 -->
"And a turban."

It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad that he said: "The shroud consists of three garments: a shirt that is not buttoned or sealed, and which is wrapped as a full winding sheet with a cloth. He said: My father charged me to shroud him in three garments, one of which was a garment he used to wear on Fridays, another garment, and a shirt."

[On the funeral prayer and burial — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family]

From al-Sadiq (peace be upon him): He mentioned the death of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and said: When Ali (peace be upon him) had washed and shrouded him, he came out and said: "O Ali, when the people gather to pray over the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and they propose that he be buried in al-Baqi' with one of them leading the prayer — go out and address them." So Ali went out and said: "O people, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) was a living and present Imam, and no prophet has ever been taken except that he was buried in the very place where he died." They said: "Do as you see fit." So Ali (peace be upon him) stood at the door of the house and led the funeral prayer over the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), with the people entering in groups of ten

(1) Addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.
(2) "Robe" in Da'a'im al-Islam.
(3) Addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.
(4) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 239/1.

<!-- page 539 -->
to pray over him and then depart.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, from his father, from his fathers, from Ali (peace be upon him): "A niche was cut for the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) — and the niche is that a recess is carved in the grave on the side facing the qibla, along the wall of the grave, and the niche is cut for the deceased in the middle of the grave."

From Ali (peace be upon him): "A mat of woven palm fronds was laid in the grave of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), because the ground was damp."

And from him (peace be upon him): "When the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) was buried, a quarter of the grave was filled."

From what was narrated on the authority of Abu Rafi', who said: "When the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) passed away, there was no one among the people more bereaved than the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace be upon him), who now rose to address them.

(1) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 241/1–242.
(2) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 242/1.
(3) See: Da'a'im al-Islam 243/1.
(4) Addition from Da'a'im al-Islam.

<!-- page 540 -->
He praised and glorified Allah, invoked blessings upon the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and spoke of his blessings upon them — that Allah had sent a Messenger from among them who had lifted abomination from them and purified them. Then he said: "I am the cousin of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) — no one can say this truthfully but me — I accepted Islam and prayed with him before all the people, and I am his guardian and his successor after him, and the husband of his daughter, the mistress of the women of all the worlds. We are the People of the House of Mercy, through whom Allah guided you out of error and gave sight to your blindness. So fear Allah, that He may preserve His grace upon you."

[The poetry of Hassan ibn Thabit lamenting the Prophet]

Hassan ibn Thabit wept for the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), saying:

I recognize the image of the Prophet and the marks of his age,
Luminous — may Allah forgive the sins and grant peace.
The verses have not been erased from the house of sanctity
Where the pulpit of the Guide once stood, upon which he used to ascend.
There is a quarter of the mosque with a prayer-ground and a minbar,
And clear traces and other enduring landmarks,
From Allah a light that shines and radiates.
There were chambers in which he used to dwell —
Worn by time, yet renewed by longing.
Familiar features not effaced by passing years —
And I see him buried, lying in the earth as in a niche,
With eyes whose like bring joy to the melody of grief.
He left behind a self-sufficient soul, and I am bereft —
Yet the signs of the Prophet remain, abundant and enduring.
I knew of it through the image of the Prophet and his era.
I kept weeping for the Prophet while others rejoiced,
Remembering the banners of the Prophet and all that I witness.
A tragedy — that Ahmad has departed.

(1) See: Diwan 455/1–457.

517.
The Prophet's Complaint: His Death
In the handwriting of the humble servant before his poor Lord, Abdul Hussein ibn Abdul Hayy al-Muqaddas, Sidi Abd al-Qadir Talwari, may God make him obedient to the Imam of the Time and his preacher, may God's prayers be upon him.
"B": The first part of the book "Eyes of News" in the biography of the chosen prophet
And his guardian and the imams from their pure family — upon them the prayers of the Almighty, the Forgiving — with the help of the King al-Jabbar and his guardian, the author of antiquities.
Then the blessed copy was completed in the era of the caller of the guardian of God and his successor in His land, may the peace of God be upon him, on the third day of the month of Safar al-Muzaffar in the year 1349 AH.
Abdul Mawlana al-Mannan al-Aqsa Hamid Ali al-Jabal.

(Al-Mumtaḥanah, 60)
O you who believe, do not take My enemies and your enemies as allies,
extending to them affection, while they have disbelieved in what has come to you of the truth — having driven out the Messenger and yourselves — because you believe in Allah, your Lord. If you have gone forth to strive in My cause and seeking My approval, secretly extending to them affection — but I am most knowing of what you have concealed and what you have declared. And whoever does this among you has certainly strayed from the straight path.
If they gain the upper hand over you, they will be your enemies and will extend against you their hands and their tongues with evil, and they wish that you would disbelieve.
Neither your relatives nor your children will benefit you on the Day of Resurrection — He will separate you, and Allah sees what you do.
There is an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people: "Indeed, we are disassociated from you and from whatever you worship other than Allah. We have denied you, and there has arisen between us and you animosity and hatred
forever, until you believe in Allah alone."
p. 226.
O you who believe, when believing women come to you as emigrants, test them —
Allah knows what they are. If you find them to be believers, do not return them to the disbelievers. They are not lawful for them, nor are those men lawful for them. And give them what they have spent. And there is no blame upon you if you marry them when you have given them their due compensation. And do not hold fast to the bonds of disbelieving women.

witnesses, and I spread out for him great ease. Then he covets that I should add more. No! Indeed, he has been opposed to Our verses. I will cover him with arduous torment. Then he looked, then he frowned and scowled, then he turned back and was arrogant, and said: "This is nothing but magic handed down — this is nothing but the word of a human being."
I will drive him into Saqar. And what will make you know what Saqar is? It leaves nothing and spares nothing,
scorching the flesh of the human being.

Sources of Investigation
Ibn Makula [d. 475 AH], Completion, researched by: 'Abd al-Rahman al-Mu'allimi [1–7], Dar al-Farouq al-Hadithah, Cairo, n.d.
Abu al-Salah al-Halabi [d. 447 AH], Al-Kafi fi fiqh, researched by: Rida Ustadi, Amir al-Mu'minin Library, Isfahan, 1403 AH.
Abu Talib ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib [d. 3 BH], Al-Durrat al-Ghara in the poetry of Sheikh al-Batha (Diwan of Abi Talib), compiled, investigated and annotated by: Baqir Ghorbani Zarrin, Ministry of Culture and Islamic Guidance, Tehran, 1995.
Abu Talib ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib [d. 3 BH], the Diwan of Abu Talib ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib (the work of Abi Hafan al-Mahzumi), researched by: Sheikh Muhammad Hassan al-Yassin, Dar al-Hilal wa-Maktabatuhu, Beirut, 2000.
Abu Nasr al-Farabi, The Book of Letters, researched by: Mohsen Mahdi, Dar al-Mashreq, Beirut, 1990.
